GodotNext • Renderer R&D • Engineering plan

Adding Lumen-class GI and Nanite-class geometry to GodotNext

A clean-room implementation plan for NextGI (dynamic multi-bounce global illumination and reflections) and NextGeo (clustered virtual geometry), grounded in an audit of the actual GodotNext snapshot — not in marketing parity claims.

Repo Legoless/GodotNext GodotNext commit 7cfeb47 Upstream source godotengine/godot@95c0fa5 Engine 4.8.0 beta Date 2026-11-02 Version 3.0 — full UE 5.8 rendering parity in v1 (user directive 2026-11-02: “follow UE 5.8, whatever they do”); v2.4 content stands except where §21 governs Status Ready for P0 — implementation not started

One-page summary

Goal: hardware-ray-traced dynamic GI and reflections (NextGI) and virtual geometry (NextGeo) inside Forward+.

v1 promise (v3.0: UE 5.8 parity)

  • Everything UE 5.8 does around Lumen, Nanite, ray tracing, shadows/lights and upscaling is v1 scope, with UE’s maturity labels and defaults (experimental stays experimental). §21 is the parity program and governs where older sections conflict.
  • Lumen-class GI: hardware RT on D3D12 DXR (primary on Windows, as UE), Vulkan and Metal; a Lumen-SWRT-class software tier (mesh SDFs, global SDF, surface cache) replaces the SDFGI fallback; Lumen Lite as Medium; hit lighting, far field, translucency GI and front-layer reflections (§21.2).
  • Nanite-class geometry: hybrid HW/SW raster, programmable raster (masked, WPO, PDO), skinned, foliage/assemblies, tessellation/displacement, fallback-mesh ray tracing (full-detail RT experimental, as UE) (§21.4).
  • Shadows and lights: Virtual Shadow Maps, MegaLights and ray-traced shadows (§21.3).
  • Upscaling: a clean-room TSR as the default temporal upscaler; full DLSS (SR, DLAA, Ray Reconstruction, Frame Generation, MFG, Reflex) built against the developer’s own NVIDIA SDK; AMD and Intel plugin seams (§21.5).
  • Platforms: Windows DX12-first once gate GD passes (Vulkan is the interim default); Mac HWRT M2+ experimental, software tier by default, as UE.
  • validated RTX 4080 SUPER (Windows), RTX 3060 (mid-range), Apple M5 Max; an M2 Mac is bought for the experimental Mac tier. Other GPUs enabled, unvalidated.
  • not in v1 PCG, World Partition, Mass, consoles, Substrate, landscape (Godot core has no terrain), and NVIDIA-only NvRTX features (PX, post-v1). No proprietary SDK file is ever committed.

Planning assumptions for one lead plus AI agents, re-estimated at every gate. Full UE 5.8 parity is a multi-year program: v1 (M8) at about month 36–60. Lanes run in parallel wherever dependencies allow (§21.0), so M5–M8 overlap with P3–P5. More engineers on the GPU-debugging critical path (RHI, Nanite raster, Lumen) shorten it roughly in proportion; agents compress boilerplate, not GPU debugging.

Revision history B. Evidence index
Names: “Lumen-class” and “Nanite-class” describe target behavior. Product systems are called NextGI and NextGeo to avoid Epic trademarks and implied code reuse.

1. Goal

Make GodotNext capable of dynamic, high-fidelity 3D scenes that today force a choice of Unreal Engine: dense source geometry without manual LOD chains, and moving lights/emissives/geometry with stable multi-bounce indirect light and glossy reflections. v1 follows Unreal Engine 5.8’s tiering: hardware ray tracing wherever the GPU and graphics API support it, and an automatic fallback elsewhere.

In scope for the program

  • NextGeo: offline cluster hierarchy, GPU cut selection, on-demand page streaming, visibility-buffer material resolve, shadow cuts and one fixed RT proxy per mesh.
  • NextGI: screen traces; hardware ray tracing (inline ray query in compute) against fixed NextGeo proxy, legacy-mesh and skinned BLASes on Vulkan (RT-capable NVIDIA, AMD and Intel GPUs, Windows/Linux) and Metal (Apple M3+); Surface and World Radiance Caches; denoised diffuse and specular indirect light; an in-engine near-unbiased reference path tracer; automatic SDFGI fallback on configurations without HWRT.
  • Upscaling: NVIDIA DLSS Super Resolution and DLAA as a Forward+ temporal upscaler beside TAA, FSR2 and MetalFX, compiled only against a developer-supplied NVIDIA SDK (D19, D20). It is in the v1 claim once GU passes. DLSS Ray Reconstruction is designed now and ships post-v1 (D21, P6i).
  • Shared editor import, debug views, profiling, test scenes, capability gating, and legacy fallback.

Explicit non-goals for v1 (v3.0)

  • Not a source port of Epic code: public documentation, talks and papers only (§19). UE 5.8 behaviour, status labels and defaults are matched; Epic’s implementation is not.
  • Outside the rendering domains: PCG, World Partition, Mass, and console platforms.
  • Substrate-class materials, except pieces that Lumen, Nanite or RT parity strictly needs (each named in §21).
  • Nanite landscape: Godot core has no terrain system (stated deviation, §21.4).
  • NVIDIA-only features that live outside UE mainline (NvRTX RTX Mega Geometry, cluster AS): PX, post-v1.
  • No NVIDIA, AMD, Intel or Epic SDK/source file in the repository, CI or official binaries (§19).

Parity statement (v3.0): v1 targets full UE 5.8 rendering parity across Lumen (HWRT and SWRT tiers, hit lighting, far field, translucency, Lumen Lite), Nanite (HW/SW raster, programmable raster, skinned, foliage, tessellation, fallback-mesh RT), Virtual Shadow Maps, MegaLights and RT shadows, the path tracer, TSR, and the vendor upscalers UE users get through plugins. Each feature keeps UE 5.8’s maturity label and default. Remaining deviations are listed in §21.0 and each §21.x risks table.

The strategic objective is a renderer we can maintain under Godot’s MIT license, built from public papers/docs and inspected open implementations where the license permits. That constraint shapes the design as much as GPU performance does. Proprietary vendor runtimes such as NVIDIA DLSS are optional modules, loaded only on matching hardware, and the MIT tree never contains their files (§19).

2. Success criteria

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

These are failable v1 acceptance targets. Each row names its harness mode ([T] timing, [C] capture, [V] validation run, [CPU] doctest/CPU tool) and uses the metric definitions Q1–Q21 in §13.4. Ceilings are provisional until their first measurement: G1a/G1 for diffuse lines, G3 for reflection and denoise lines, G0 for AS lines. That measurement may reset each line once, logged in Appendix A with the data; afterwards measurement may only tighten. The total-frame caps (6.6 ms Ada / 16.6 ms mid-range p95, Dense City + NextGI High) are fixed. Ada (RTX 4080 SUPER) ceilings are iteration proxies derived by the §2.1 scaling rule. Mid-range ceilings are gated on a measured RTX 3060 12 GB and never inferred from Ada. The same ceiling rule applies to the upscaler lines (§2.1). Lines U1 and U1M equal NVIDIA’s published table values, so §2 row 7’s ≤1.25× pass rule applies exactly once. The provisional lines U1A (DLAA) and U1Q (Quality) are first measured at GU and may reset once.

Areav1 acceptance targetEvidence
1. Geometry scale [C/T]Scene: Dense City Blocks (≥10M source triangles, ≥5k INSTANCE_MESH instances, ≥1M DAG clusters reachable, working set ≥2× the geometry-cache budget), rendered with no authored LODs at max_pixel_error = 1.0 px into the main view + 4 directional splits + positional shadow faces.
  • 0 crack pixels (Q5).
  • FLIP mean ≤0.05 (Q7) vs the full-resolution forward reference.
  • ≤1.0× the GPU frame time of the discrete-LOD Dense City baseline with NextGI High on.
  • Rasterized-triangle-size histogram attached.
  • A 10M/100M/1B instanced-triangle sweep is reported, not gated.
G5-1, G5-2, G5-9
2. Geometry memory [T]
  • The default-stripped export keeps total resident geometry VRAM (Q15) at ≤0.5× the discrete-LOD baseline export. The total counts the authored remainder, LODs, shadow mesh, coarse fallback, RT arena, BLAS, TLAS, AS scratch, DAG headers and page cache.
  • RT memory ≤25% of that total.
  • Exported geometry bytes ≤1.6× raw source vertex+index bytes.
  • retain_authored exports are reported, not gated.
G4-4, G5-4
3. Streaming [T]
  • 0 hole pixels (Q6) during camera flight.
  • Slow IO Flight, with IO delays injected in wall time: p99 frame time ≤2.0× median.
  • GPU page upload ≤0.4 ms Ada / ≤1.0 ms mid-range per frame.
G5-2, G5-3
4. Dynamic GI, HWRT tier [C]
  • Static scenes (Color Box Room variants A/B and the off-screen-light variant; Concave Courtyard):
    • indirect diffuse patch error (Q1) ≤20% vs the path-traced reference;
    • card-hit coverage (Q2) ≥85%;
    • leak ratio (Q3) ≤1% through both 10 cm and 20 cm walls.
  • Dynamic scenes (Moving Light + Door, Time of Day), 0 rebakes:
    • step events (Q4a: light toggled, sun jumps 15°, door teleported 1 m open, each at frame 0): e(10) ≤25%, e(32) ≤10%;
    • continuous motion (Q4b: door opens over 2 s, sun sweeps 90° over 10 s): mean elag ≤25% over the motion vs a per-frame path-traced reference, and e(32) ≤10% 32 frames after the motion ends.
G1a, G1, G2-3
5. Reflections, HWRT tier [C]Roughness Corridor, SSR off, no ReflectionProbe:
  • mean reflection radiance error (Q9) ≤30% for roughness ≤0.4;
  • reflection-only FLIP ≤0.10 in [0.25, 0.4] and ≤0.15 in [0.1, 0.25);
  • [0, 0.1) reported only, since mirrors need post-v1 hit lighting;
  • off-screen emissive patch error ≤30%.
G3-2
6. Dynamic actors & translucency [C]
  • Character Walk: character indirect error ≤25% at 5 fixed poses; the vacated region returns to within 10% of the empty endpoint by frame 10.
  • Glass & Water: translucent ambient is non-zero, sigma (Q8) ≤3%, and reacts to a light change by frame 10. There is no reference gate for translucency in v1.
G3-4, G3-5
7. Performance [T]The §2.1 budget tables. Every pass (Q11) must be ≤1.25× its line item. NextGI lines are timed on the NextGI Perf scene (§13.3); frame totals (Q12) on Dense City + NextGI High. Headline ceilings (ms p95, 1080p, steady state): NextGI High 1.60 Ada / 4.00 mid-range / 4.00 M5 Max; Medium 0.87 / 2.18 / 2.18; Epic 3.20 / 8.00 / 8.00; Dense City + NextGI High frame 6.6 / 16.6 (Ada / mid-range). Upscaler lines are separate (§2.1 upscaler table). NextGI and NextGeo lines stay gated at 1080p internal under any upscaler. The total-frame caps stay at 1080p native with no upscaler.G1-3, G2-4, G3-1, G5-1
8. Tier selection & compatibility [V]
  • Each fleet configuration selects the tier the rule predicts, with 0 errors.
  • Fallback costs ≤2% (Q13) over plain SDFGI.
  • NextGeo fallback (Q14) covers <20% of opaque pixels in Dense City; at or above 20% the gate fails as a scope failure, never a warning.
G0-2, G2-5, G5-5
9. Regression safety [CPU/C/T]
  • Existing tests pass.
  • The GPU-backed import → save → reload → LightmapGI::bake round trip is non-empty, with FLIP ≤0.005.
  • With NextGI off: ≤2% GPU time (Q13) and FLIP ≤0.005 vs baseline. This replaces every earlier “0% overhead” target.
  • With modules/dlss compiled in but DLSS not selected: ≤2% GPU time (Q13) and FLIP ≤0.005 against a build without the module (GU-5).
G1-4, G4-7
10. Upscaling, NVIDIA DLSS [CPU/V/C/T]In the v1 claim only once GU is signed off; it never blocks M3 or M4. Condition: NVIDIA RTX under Forward+ Vulkan, a DLSS-enabled build, and NVIDIA’s rel runtime present.
  • Modes: DLSS SR at Quality, Balanced, Performance and Ultra Performance, and DLAA at scale 1.0.
  • Quality: mean Q19 at 1440p and 2160p output ≤ FSR2 at the same scale (DLAA: ≤ TAA-native) + tolerance; Q21 = 0; Q8 ≤1.5%; Q10 after an occluder move ≤ FSR2 + 2 pp.
  • Cost: the §2.1 upscaler lines U1, U1M, U1A and U1Q.
  • Fallback: every non-qualifying Forward+ configuration falls back to MetalFX temporal or FSR2 with one log line and its ViewportScaling3DReason.
  • Validation: DLSS-on validation follows the GU-2 attribution rule.
  • Hygiene: default builds contain no NVIDIA files or symbols.
GU-1 to GU-6; the conditional DLSS rows of G3-3 and G5-7
Quality reference: the in-engine path tracer (D16, §8.8), near-unbiased (64-bounce cap, truncation bias < 1e-5), is authoritative for every error gate. G0 closes on its analytic tests only (furnace box, sealed boxes A/B); scene references start in P1a. LightmapGI (bounces 16, quality Ultra, use_denoiser off, use_texture_for_bounces off) is a report-only cross-check: a mismatch above 10% opens an investigation, which may find LightmapGI at fault. Gate scenes use constant-parameter StandardMaterial3D, so the reference needs no texture access at hits. For the post-v1 DLSS-RR mode, Q20 compares the final image at output resolution with D16 rendered at output resolution, using Q1’s patch method (§13.4).

2.1 Budgets

All times are ms p95 at 1080p internal render resolution unless stated, and the ceiling process in §2 applies to every line. Budgets are for the main view: the AS, SRC and WRC passes run once per scenario, and each secondary view adds only its own gather and denoise cost (lines 4–8). Upscaler lines are per output resolution (table below).

Scaling rule

  • Until G1, Ada (RTX 4080 SUPER) ceiling = mid-range ceiling / 2.5 for every line. G1 measures per-pass Ada:RTX 3060 ratios, which replace the 2.5× factor from then on. Ada lines are iteration proxies only.
  • Mid-range lines are gated on a measured RTX 3060 12 GB, bought at G0, never inferred from Ada.
  • M5 Max ceilings equal the mid-range ceilings (first measured at G2).
  • Epic total = 2× High; per-line values as tabled.
  • Medium: every line is High × 0.5, except line 1a, which is unchanged.
  • Validated: Ada on Windows (RTX 4080 SUPER) and Apple10 (M5 Max). Turing, Ampere (until the RTX 3060 arrives), Blackwell, all Linux drivers, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Arc and Apple M3/M4 are enabled but unvalidated, with no gate.
  • Upscaler lines do not use the 2.5× rule. U1 and U1M are NVIDIA’s published RTX 4080 and RTX 3060 values, measured by a command-line tool without a renderer (guide p.5). The RTX 4080 SUPER stands in for the RTX 4080; the equivalence is unverified. The license packet re-reads the tables at the pinned SDK tag.

Presets

  • rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/quality = auto (default) | medium | high | epic. auto picks High, Medium or the SDFGI fallback from a ≤5 ms startup microbenchmark on the G0 ray-query fixture plus a coarse compute-unit or GPU-core class, and logs the choice with its reason (§3).
  • Medium: 1/32 rpp diffuse; reflections only for roughness ≤0.25, at quarter resolution; SRC relight ≤128k texels and ≤128k shadow rays per frame. Total ≤0.87 Ada / ≤2.18 mid-range.
  • Epic: SRC relight ≤512k texels and ≤512k shadow rays per frame; per-line values below.

CPU budgets

  • TLAS instance fill: ≤1.0 ms at 50k instances; ≤2.0 ms at the 100k cap.
  • NextGI scene update, excluding the TLAS fill: ≤0.5 ms.
  • Skinned refit: ≤200k triangles per frame on Ada (≤100k mid-range), inside line 1a.
  • Batched card capture: ≤0.25 ms for a 64-card batch (P1b gate).

Memory (High, configurable)

  • SRC radiance atlas: 4096² R11G11B10F, 64 MiB.
  • SRC material atlas: 128 MiB, shared per (mesh, material override set). Epic doubles both.
  • WRC: about 14 MiB, plus history if double-buffered.
  • Translucency volume: 1/16 screen × 32 slices (about 261k froxels at 1080p), FP16 SH-L1, about 6 MiB. Its fill is measured against line 3 at G3-5.
  • RT arena + BLAS + TLAS + scratch: reported, and ≤25% of geometry VRAM at G5.
  • NGX feature memory per view: ≤1.25× NVIDIA’s table (RTX 40/50, 1080p / 1440p / 2160p output): preset K 85.77 / 143.54 / 307.37 MB (≤107 / ≤179 / ≤384 MB); preset M 116.32 / 204.39 / 456.02 MB (≤145 / ≤255 / ≤570 MB). It is reported as a separate NGX category in the RD memory report.

Balanced and Ultra Performance are report-only. U1A and U1Q are provisional until GU (R21). Source: DLSS Programming Guide 310.6.0, “DLSS Execution Times & GPU RAM Usage”. U2 (DLSS-RR) and U3 (RR guides) are defined with P6i.

Other tiers

  • SDFGI fallback (v1): no new ms budget; selection overhead ≤2% vs plain SDFGI.
  • SDF software tier (P6b, defined now):
    • forced on the RTX 3060: High ≤4.0 ms, Epic ≤8.0 ms;
    • on the UHD 770 class: a P6b-only Low preset (not part of the v1 quality enum) ≤6.0 ms at 720p internal, quality report-only;
    • quality: diffuse ≤20%, coverage ≥85%, leak ≤5% at 20 cm (10 cm reported), Q4a e(10) ≤25%.

Post-v1 indicative costs (not part of v1)

  • Ray-query sun + ≤8 local shadows including denoise: ≤0.4 ms Ada / ≤1.0 ms mid-range (P6c).
  • Hit lighting, Epic only: +2–9 ms mid-range (P6d).
  • Far-field TLAS: separate budget (P6e).
Grounding: Epic’s UE 5.8 Lumen performance guide targets 4 ms (60 fps) and 8 ms (30 fps) at 1080p on current consoles. PS5 is roughly RX 6700 class; that mapping is an approximation. Matrix Awakens with HWRT plus far field cost 6.38 ms High on PS5 in 2022 (SIGGRAPH 2022 Lumen talk). So 4.0 ms mid-range for a first implementation without far field is aggressive and stays a provisional ceiling. The 2.5× Ada:mid-range factor is unverified; the per-pass ratios measured at G1 replace it.

3. Verdict and v1 scope

v3.0 tier model: per §21 — D3D12 DXR is the primary Windows HWRT path and new projects default to D3D12 once gate GD passes (Vulkan stays the interim default and a supported RT path); with NextGI enabled, every HWRT failure selects the Lumen-SWRT-class software tier (PS), and SDFGI runs only when NextGI is off; Mac HWRT is M2+ experimental and off by default, with the software tier as the Mac default (PL); quality = medium becomes Lumen Lite. All features carry UE 5.8’s maturity labels. Where §3 conflicts with §21, §21 governs; the remaining stated differences from UE 5.8 are landscape, NvRTX-only features and clean-room limits.

Forward+ already contains useful scaffolding for a compute-driven visibility-buffer NextGeo and a hardware-ray-traced NextGI: a thin depth/normal-roughness prepass, clustered light buffers, compute/indirect submission, and a minimal Vulkan acceleration-structure and ray-query API. That API is build-only and CPU-fed, and ray-query-only use is blocked until the hit-SBT check is relaxed.

It does not contain the other prerequisites: persistent GPU scene, GPU cluster Hi-Z/culling, mesh/task shader stages, ray-query-only TLAS builds, AS update/compaction, Metal and D3D12 ray tracing, geometry paging, a unified GPU material-binding/resolve model, virtual shadows, or a real-time denoiser.

Honest v1 cut: opaque content, static and skinned, lit by NextGI hardware ray tracing on Vulkan RT GPUs and Apple M3+ Metal; static opaque virtual geometry (NextGeo) on Forward+ Vulkan, Metal and D3D12. Validated: Ada on Windows (RTX 4080 SUPER) and Apple10 (M5 Max). Enabled but unvalidated: Turing, Ampere (until the RTX 3060 arrives), Blackwell, all Linux drivers, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Arc, and Apple M3/M4. Windows projects on the Direct3D 12 driver get SDFGI until P6a. New GodotNext projects default to Vulkan. Everything else stays on current paths, and configurations without HWRT fall back to SDFGI automatically. NVIDIA RTX GPUs also get DLSS SR/DLAA under Forward+ Vulkan, in DLSS-enabled builds, once GU passes (D19).
Capabilityv1Later / separate funding

Decision: do not market v1 as “Lumen + Nanite for Godot.” Ship it as an experimental desktop tier: hardware-ray-traced dynamic GI and reflections on RT-capable Vulkan GPUs and Apple M3+, with automatic fallback. Parity language stays false until hit lighting, RT shadows, a software surface-cache fallback, D3D12 RT and AMD/Intel validation are funded and measured.

v1 does not claim:

  • D3D12 hardware RT (P6a), or any silent API switch to reach it. New GodotNext projects default to Vulkan; an existing d3d12 project switches only if the user accepts the one-time modal shown when nextgi/enabled is turned on (it keeps fallback_to_d3d12 = true and needs a restart).
  • A software ray-traced tier: configurations without HWRT get SDFGI, and the SDF+SRC+WRC tier is P6b.
  • Hit lighting or mirror-grade reflections below roughness 0.1 (P6d, Epic only).
  • Ray-traced shadows or many-light sampling (P6c), or a far-field TLAS beyond rt_scene_radius (P6e).
  • Full-detail virtual-geometry ray tracing: NextGeo traces fixed proxies. NVIDIA cluster AS plus partitioned TLAS, opacity micromaps and invocation reorder are vendor-optional post-v1 tiers behind capability flags (P6f); DXR Clustered Geometry follows P6a once it leaves preview.
  • Validated behaviour beyond Ada on Windows and Apple10. Turing, Ampere (until the RTX 3060 arrives), Blackwell, all Linux drivers, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Arc and Apple M3/M4 are enabled but unvalidated. Linux/NVIDIA becomes validated only if the optional P3 run of G3 on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine booted into Linux (NVIDIA proprietary driver) passes.
  • HWRT on Apple M1/M2: force only, labelled unsupported; an M2 opt-in is P6g.
  • Alpha-blended or alpha-tested surfaces in the TLAS (alpha-tested materials have a per-material “treat as opaque for RT” override).
  • DLSS in official GodotNext binaries, until NVIDIA confirms distribution in writing (§19).
  • DLSS Ray Reconstruction (P6i), Frame Generation, Multi Frame Generation or Reflex (P6j).
  • DLSS on non-NVIDIA GPUs, Metal, Mobile, Compatibility, Web or multiview.
  • DLSS at all if GU has not passed before the milestone’s sign-off.

3.1 Tier model

The tier is selected by capability inside the active API (D11). HWRT is the default wherever the rule passes, and GodotNext never switches graphics API silently (D12).

Selection inputs

New project settings under rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/:

  • enabled (bool, default false). When true on a device with SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY, MeshStorage creates RT-arena copies at upload; projects that leave it false pay no memory.
  • hardware_ray_tracing = auto (default) | off | force.
  • quality = auto (default) | medium | high | epic.
  • rt_scene_radius (default 200 m).
  • rt_max_error (default 2 cm at import scale), the world-space bound on RT proxy error.

Environment: nextgi_enabled plus nextgi_* parameters, following the sdfgi_* conventions.

RenderingDevice:

  • the existing SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY; on Vulkan it is accelerationStructure && rayQuery, tightened to also require bufferDeviceAddress. Each driver returns a reason code with it: METAL_SYNC_MODE or METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS on Metal, D3D12_NO_DXR on D3D12, NO_RAY_QUERY otherwise;
  • a new SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE, which is driver knowledge only: Apple9+ on Metal; on Vulkan, a PCI vendor/device-ID allowlist of RT-core architectures (NVIDIA Turing+ RTX, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Xe-HPG/Xe2 Arc);
  • get_device_type();
  • AS limits, newly exposed.

Rule

Evaluated at RD init and whenever the setting changes. The tier and its reason are shown in the debug overlay and printed once.

The HWRT tier is active if and only if all of these hold:

  1. the project and the Environment enable NextGI;
  2. hardware_ray_tracing != off (else SETTING_OFF);
  3. has_feature(SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY) (else the driver’s reason code);
  4. either hardware_ray_tracing == force, or both of these, checked in this order: get_device_type() != DEVICE_TYPE_CPU (else CPU_DEVICE; this check lives in the rule alone), then has_feature(SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE) (else NOT_HW_ACCELERATED);
  5. the startup self-test passes: one triangle plus a 64-ray fixture, compared against analytic IDs and t (else SELFTEST_FAILED);
  6. no HWRT fault is latched for this driver version (else HWRT_FAULTED). A device loss while HWRT is active (VK_EXT_device_fault is already enabled on Vulkan) persists the flag under user://; it clears when the driver version changes, and force overrides it;
  7. the TLAS/BLAS memory budget can be met (else AS_MEMORY).

Otherwise the v1 fallback tier applies:

  • existing SDFGI, driven by the Environment’s sdfgi_* parameters;
  • the reason comes from the tier reason enum (§6.3): NO_RAY_QUERY, NOT_HW_ACCELERATED, CPU_DEVICE, D3D12_NO_DXR, METAL_SYNC_MODE, METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS, SETTING_OFF, AS_MEMORY, SELFTEST_FAILED or HWRT_FAULTED. Per-instance RT exclusions (for example NOT_IN_RT_SCENE, RT_INSTANCE_CAP, RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED) belong to the separate per-(instance, surface) Fallback enum;
  • fallback never raises an error and costs ≤2% GPU time over plain SDFGI;
  • force on a device without SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY falls back with one warning;
  • ray-query ShaderRDs are initialized only while the HWRT tier is active, so Metal (before P2) and D3D12 never compile them. Their versions are created non-embedded, or filtered in ShaderBakerExportPlugin::_customize_shader_version for containers without acceleration-structure support, so the shader baker bakes them only for Vulkan, and for Metal from P2 on.

When the rule passes with quality = auto, a ≤5 ms startup microbenchmark on the G0 ray-query fixture, plus a coarse compute-unit or GPU-core class, picks High, Medium or the SDFGI fallback, and the choice is logged with its reason.

Tier matrix (v1 unless stated)

In an existing d3d12 project, turning on nextgi/enabled shows a one-time modal offering the Vulkan switch: it keeps fallback_to_d3d12=true, states the HDR/DComp loss and offers a restart. 10D3D12 with DXR Tier 1.1 and SM 6.5 (P6a, post-v1)POST-V1 HWRTRequires OPTIONS5.RaytracingTier ≥ 1.1 on a non-WARP adapter.
11Metal, Apple9+ (M3, M4, M5, M6), macOS ≥14, argument buffers on, sync mode BarriersHWRTDetected by supportsRaytracing() && supportsFamily(Apple9). Validated on the M5 Max (Apple10, which the driver currently reports as Apple9). M3/M4 enabled but unvalidated.
12Metal, Apple9+ under GODOT_MTL_SYNC_MODE=none (hazard tracking) or with argument buffers disabledFALLBACK METAL_SYNC_MODE or METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERSSUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY reports false there, with the matching reason.
13Metal, Apple7/8 (M1, M2): the ray-tracing API exists but there is no RT hardwareFALLBACK NOT_HW_ACCELERATEDforce is allowed, labelled unsupported and unvalidated, since there is no M1/M2 in the fleet. UE 5.8 offers M2+ as experimental.
14Mobile, Compatibility, WebUNCHANGEDRenderer behaviour unchanged.

What the HWRT tier runs and traces

  • Quality presets (auto chooses Medium, High or the fallback, as in the rule above; budgets in §2.1):
    • Medium: 1/32 ray per pixel diffuse; reflections only for roughness ≤0.25, at quarter resolution; SRC relight cap 128k texels/frame.
    • High (60 fps class): 1/16 rpp diffuse; reflections at 1/4 rpp, half resolution; relight cap 256k texels/frame.
    • Epic (30 fps class): 1/4 rpp diffuse; 1 rpp reflections at full resolution; relight cap 512k texels/frame. Epic total = 2× High.
  • TLAS: rebuilt on the CPU once per scenario per frame, following NVIDIA and Intel guidance, anchored on the main viewport camera and shared by all views. Its instances are all GI-contributing opaque instances in the scenario within rt_scene_radius, not the camera cull list. Beyond 30 m, instances under 0.5° of angular radius are dropped, which caps the TLAS at 100k instances.
  • BLAS sources (D13), with eps_rt = min(max_extent/256, rt_max_error): the NextGeo fixed proxy from Slice 2 (a DAG cut at error ≤ eps_rt, capped at 128k triangles per BLAS; when the cap binds, the proxy is split into several BLASes rather than coarsened); for legacy static meshes, the coarsest ArrayMesh LOD whose stored edge_length ≤ eps_rt, as a float32/uint32 RT-arena copy; for skinned meshes, a per-instance arena slot written by the skinning compute pass, with the BLAS refit in place.
  • Opaque only: traversal is a single proceed with no candidate loop. Alpha-tested and transparent surfaces are excluded from the TLAS.
  • MultiMesh, GridMap octants and particles: static MultiMesh, including non-baked GridMap octants, is CPU-fed into the TLAS from the existing CPU cache (_multimesh_make_local) in P1b, within the 100k instance cap. Particles and dynamic MultiMesh enter through GPU-written TLAS instances in P3 (§14 cut list, item 1). RT coverage (Q18), the share of GI-contributing opaque instances within rt_scene_radius that are in the TLAS, must reach ≥95% on NextGI Perf at G1 and G3.
  • NextGeo is independent of the tier: virtual raster runs on any Forward+ Vulkan, D3D12 or Metal device that passes NextGeo’s own capability check. Proxy BLASes are built only while the HWRT tier is active.

Stated differences from UE 5.8

  • Windows: UE’s HWRT is DX12-first. The 5.8 HWRT page labels Vulkan RT experimental while the 5.5 notes call it default with DX12 parity, so the sources conflict. GodotNext is Vulkan-first, with Vulkan as the new-project default and D3D12 post-v1 (P6a).
  • macOS: UE’s Mac HWRT is M2+ experimental. GodotNext enables it by default on M3+.
  • Non-RT GPUs: UE falls back to Lumen SWRT. GodotNext v1 falls back to SDFGI, and the SWRT-class tier is P6b.

3.2 Upscaler selection

DLSS is requested as VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_DLSS; DLAA is DLSS at scaling_3d_scale 1.0. Requesting DLSS never raises an error and never switches graphics API.

Resolution order in RendererViewport

  1. Compatibility or Mobile → the existing bilinear rule (RENDERER_UNSUPPORTED).
  2. Driver not Vulkan → API_UNSUPPORTED. D3D12 counts as supported only once pu-dlss-d3d12 lands.
  3. No DLSS TemporalUpscaler registered → MODULE_DISABLED.
  4. The module’s own checks, latched per session: NOT_NVIDIA_RTX, DRIVER_TOO_OLD, RUNTIME_MISSING, EXTENSION_MISSING, PROJECT_ID_MISSING, FEATURE_CREATE_FAILED.
  5. Scale outside [0.30, 1.0], or output under 32×32 → SCALE_UNSUPPORTED.
  6. view_count > 1 → VIEW_UNSUPPORTED.

If every check passes, DLSS is active with reason NONE.

Fallback and reporting

  • When a check from 2 to 6 fails, the viewport falls back to MetalFX temporal (Metal with SUPPORTS_METALFX_TEMPORAL), else FSR2, and prints one log line per reason per process.
  • As for MetalFX temporal, MSAA is disabled internally and TAA is off while DLSS is active.
  • RenderingServer.scaling_3d_mode_get_availability(mode) lets games build settings menus. RenderingServer.viewport_get_scaling_3d_status(viewport) and the debug overlay report the requested mode, active backend, preset and reason.
  • The reason enum, ViewportScaling3DReason, is separate from the tier reason enum (§3.1) and the per-(instance, surface) Fallback enum (I3). P6i appends the RR reasons.

DLSS SR/DLAA by API and platform

#API and platformv1 statusResult and validation

Also not in v1: NVIDIA Image Scaling, a DLSS sharpness control (sharpening is deprecated, guide §3.11) and DLSS 5 neural rendering (no public SDK was found).

4. Current snapshot audit

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

Audit baseline: 7cfeb47 is our single-commit GodotNext import; the only later commit, 3a38f68, adds this plan. Its file contents came from upstream 95c0fa5, so the workflow notes’ “label mismatch” is explained, not a content discrepancy. 95c0fa5 is not in the local object store and no refs/replace graft exists, so clones carry no upstream history; P0 creates and publishes the replace ref (§12). Code facts were re-verified against 7cfeb47 on 2026-11-01; this section cites functions, and the line citations are collected in Appendix B. The §4.6 upscaler facts were verified against 7cfeb47 on 2026-11-02.

4.1 Current Forward+ frame

Scene setupSDFGI/VoxelGI setup; CPU render lists
→
Thin prepassOptional depth + normal/roughness
→
Pre-opaqueShadows, GI, SSAO/SSIL/SSR, cluster bake, fog
→
Opaque colorPASS_MODE_COLOR; materials and lighting fused
→
Sky + SSSSpecular merge and screen copies
→
TransparentForward sorted alpha
→
TemporalFSR2 / MetalFX / TAA, tonemap
  • Best hook:
    • Force the depth + normal-roughness prepass whenever NextGI or NextGeo is active. Both the force_depth_pre_pass condition and the depth_pass_mode selection in RenderForwardClustered::_render_scene need the flag.
    • Velocity is written today in the opaque color pass, where RENDER_LIST_MOTION redraws moving instances with PASS_MODE_COLOR. From P1b, NextGI’s prepass variant (depth + normal-roughness + VoxelGI id) writes motion vectors for moving and skinned instances, and the color pass stops re-writing them while NextGI is active (§6.1).
    • Run the NextGI block immediately after _render_shadow_end inside _pre_opaque_render, replacing gi.process_gi() for that view. Never run it between _render_shadow_begin and _render_shadow_end: those share RENDER_LIST_SECONDARY with _render_material.
    • Feed NextGI’s ambient volume to _update_volumetric_fog.
    • Use load_color only when Stage B wrote pixels (§6.1).
    • Temporal upscalers run in one pre-tonemap slot after the POST_TRANSPARENT compositor effects. FSR2 and MetalFX are hard-coded there; DLSS joins through a core hook (§4.6, D20).
    Transparents stay forward.
  • GI light precedent: gi.process_gi() reads SDFGI cascades and VoxelGI data, not the clustered light buffers. SDFGI injects lights from its own scenario-wide list (RendererSceneCull::_render_scene passes scenario->dynamic_lights to GI::SDFGI). That list holds only non-directional lights in DYNAMIC bake mode. NextGI follows the precedent (D14), so its SRC lighting does not depend on update_light_buffers() or bake_cluster(), and adds a scenario static-light list next to scenario->dynamic_lights: STATIC lights dirty SRC pages only when their properties change and are skipped for lightmapped receivers, and DISABLED lights are excluded.
  • Do not create a fourth renderer first: renderer selection is a hardcoded allowlist in main/main.cpp. Put v1 inside forward_plus; consider a RendererSceneRenderRD sibling only when the visibility path is proven.

4.2 GPU-driven substrate: present vs absent

Present

  • Compute dispatch and compute-indirect; indexed indirect draws (currently centered on MultiMesh).
  • Driver-level indirect-count wrappers on Vulkan/D3D12, not yet exposed through RenderingDevice/graph or enabled via Vulkan 1.2 device features.
  • Clustered light assignment buffer; normal/roughness, velocity, specular buffers in relevant modes.
  • RenderingDevice BLAS/TLAS, hit SBT, RT pipeline and ray-query APIs (script-bound, experimental), with a non-Apple Vulkan backend. Limits:
    • builds are MODE_BUILD only, with no update, refit or compaction;
    • TLAS instances are CPU-written into a host-mapped buffer;
    • tlas_build rejects zero hit-SBT ranges and hit_sbt_create requires an RT pipeline, so ray-query-only consumers cannot build a populated TLAS;
    • every AS keeps a persistent scratch buffer, and AS storage is missing from buffer_memory;
    • instance writes use the BLAS buffer address instead of vkGetAccelerationStructureDeviceAddressKHR, a latent spec-compliance issue, since the addresses coincide at offset 0;
    • mesh buffers lack DEVICE_ADDRESS/AS-input flags;
    • imported positions default to R16G16B16A16_UNORM normalized per surface AABB, which is not a mandatory Vulkan AS vertex format;
    • compute shaders are SPIR-V 1.3 with #version 450, while rayQueryEXT needs #version 460.
  • Feedback-driven texture mip streaming as an architectural template for geometry paging.

Absent

  • TASK/MESH shader stages and vertex-less pipelines; pipeline creation still requires a vertex stage.
  • GPU instance/cluster culling and two-phase occlusion (a GPU depth pyramid already exists for SSR; cluster occlusion does not), GPU LOD choice, persistent GPU scene.
  • Per-cluster vertex data, cluster DAG, geometry page cache, and residency feedback.
  • Unified GPU material/texture resolve path (neither an RD bindless descriptor table with deferred-free lifetime nor per-material classified compute resolve exists).
  • Real-time denoiser, persistent SVGF guides/history, virtual shadow-map paging.
  • Metal and D3D12 acceleration structures (stubs). Metal shader-container support for acceleration-structure bindings (CRASH_NOW_MSG on UNIFORM_TYPE_ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE). D3D12 ray-query translation (the pinned Mesa 25.3.1 nir_to_dxil has no RayQuery opcodes).

No mid-range, AMD or Intel Arc RT GPU is in the fleet yet; an RTX 3060 12 GB is bought at G0, for a second PCIe slot or another host (§18). The M5 Max is Apple10 with third-generation hardware RT and is Metal 4-capable. Windows driver default: the engine-level default for driver.windows is vulkan (main/main.cpp GLOBAL_DEF), but EditorNode::get_initial_settings writes d3d12 into every new project (Godot 4.6+); GodotNext changes that initial setting to vulkan in P0 (§3.1, tier matrix row 9). Windows switches API only when initialization fails, never by feature. The NGX-related Vulkan extension exposure is recorded in the P0 vulkaninfo archive. The actual NGX requirement list is logged into fleet.md at the first NGX init (pu-dlss-sr-effect).
