4.5 Existing geometry path

  • ArrayMesh LODs are index-only simplifications that share full-resolution vertex buffers, so they save raster triangles but not vertex memory or streaming bandwidth.
  • ImporterMesh::generate_lods() already uses meshoptimizer attribute-aware simplification and is the correct import hook for a cluster DAG builder. It simplifies whole surfaces today; it does not group clusters, lock group borders, or propagate parent error.
  • meshoptimizer: register_types.cpp wires simplification plus vertex cache, fetch, remap and tangent hooks. SCsub compiles clusterizer, partition and simplifier, but not meshletutils.cpp (meshopt_computeClusterBounds, optimizeMeshlet) or meshletcodec.cpp. SimplifyWithAttribFunc already carries vertex_lock.
  • Imported meshes use vertex compression by default (R16G16B16A16_UNORM positions in surface-AABB space; disabled for skinned, morph-target and normal-less meshes). Skinned meshes write float positions into double-buffered outputs (MeshStorage::MeshInstance::Surface::vertex_buffer[2]) when motion vectors are on.
  • mesh_add_surface eagerly uploads all vertices/attributes/skins/indices/LOD indices. There is no page-fault, priority, VRAM-budget, or upload-queue API for geometry.
  • Occlusion culling is CPU HZ/AABB and baked occluders, not a GPU depth pyramid produced from the previous rendered frame.
  • create_shadow_mesh() is the precedent for an always-resident position-only proxy (queried via MeshStorage::mesh_get_shadow_mesh and cached once per surface into GeometryInstanceSurfaceDataCache::surface_shadow inside RenderForwardClustered::_geometry_instance_add_surface_with_material), not a per-view scene-cull selector, raster fallback, or BLAS source today.

4.6 Existing temporal upscalers and vendor hooks

Upscaler slot (Forward+)

  • RenderForwardClustered::_render_scene maps the scaling mode to a local SCALE_NONE, SCALE_FSR2 or SCALE_MFX (MFX only under METAL_MFXTEMPORAL_ENABLED). In the motion pass it clears velocity to (-1,-1,0,0) for FSR2 and runs MotionVectorsStore::process for MetalFX. After the POST_TRANSPARENT compositor effects it dispatches FSR2, MetalFX or TAA, then _render_buffers_post_process_and_tonemap.
  • MotionVectorsStore is created only under METAL_MFXTEMPORAL_ENABLED, although its shader is portable. It applies one centre-camera reprojection to every view, so MetalFX multiview already gets wrong per-eye camera velocity (an upstream-relevant finding, §18.2). derive_motion_vector divides by the previous w with no clamp.
  • FSR2 never resets accumulation (reset_accumulation = false, a FIXME); MetalFX resets only when ensure_mfx_temporal creates its context. FSR2 and MetalFX share one context across views and, under auto-exposure, receive the average-luminance buffer as “exposure”. FSR2 also gets an alpha-derived reactive mask (get_internal_texture_reactive).

Vendor hook (render graph)

  • MFXTemporalEffect::process is the vendor template: RD::get_driver_resource(DRIVER_RESOURCE_TEXTURE) for native textures, and RD::driver_callback_add with CallbackResource usages so the graph places the barriers.
  • RenderingDeviceGraph::add_driver_callback sets no self_stages, so Vulkan barriers around a callback lack its stages (code reading; Metal ignores stage masks, which hides this from MetalFX). Callbacks run at record time, inside RenderingDeviceGraph::_run_render_commands.
  • driver_callback_add is not script-bound; get_driver_resource is.
  • In DEBUG_ENABLED builds, GODOT_RG_FULL_BARRIERS and GODOT_RG_REORDER override the graph’s barriers and reordering (RenderingDevice::_configure_draw_graph_flags).

Vulkan driver

  • RenderingDeviceDriverVulkan::CommandBufferInfo, which holds the VkCommandBuffer, is private.
  • get_resource_native_handle(DRIVER_RESOURCE_TEXTURE) returns the parent VkImage, with no subresource range or extent.
  • Instance and device extension registration (_register_requested_instance_extension, _register_requested_device_extension) is private.
  • VulkanHooks is a single-owner singleton that replaces instance and device creation. OpenXR implements it (OpenXRVulkanExtension) and also chooses the physical device.
  • RenderingContextDriverVulkan::_debug_messenger_callback receives each message’s pObjects, and set_object_name exists.

Viewport and RenderingServer

  • RendererViewport::_configure_3d_render_buffers resolves the mode. Compatibility and Mobile fall back to bilinear. An unsupported MetalFX temporal falls back to MetalFX spatial, else FSR2, each with WARN_PRINT_ONCE. MetalFX temporal checks its scale limits and disables MSAA internally, and TAA is turned off under any temporal upscaler. Temporal modes still run at scale 1.0.
  • The jitter phase count is truncated: uint32(8·(target/render)²).
  • RenderSceneBuffersRD::update_samplers applies a −0.5 sampler bias under temporal modes.
  • The mode hint strings are built in RenderingServer::init, after modules initialize. Viewport::_bind_methods has its own hint string, which Viewport::_validate_property can adjust in the editor.

D3D12: on the legacy-barrier path RenderingDeviceDriverD3D12::command_pipeline_barrier is a no-op. _check_capabilities enables enhanced barriers whenever OPTIONS12.EnhancedBarriersSupported is set. The driver caches PSO, root-signature and descriptor-heap state per command buffer (reset in command_buffer_end), which NGX would clobber.

5. Key decisions

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

D4, D11, D12 and D13 implement the user decision of 2026-11-01. D7 and D16 replace v2.2 mechanisms that the review found unworkable. D19–D22 implement the user request of 2026-11-02 (DLSS), which also amends D1, D8, D12 and D18.

IDDecisionWhySupersedes
D1Build inside forward_plus; transparents remain forward. DLSS SR/DLAA (and, post-v1, DLSS-RR) use the same pre-tonemap upscaler slot as FSR2 and MetalFX. That slot runs after the POST_TRANSPARENT compositor effects and before tonemap, through the core TemporalUpscaler hook (D20).Reuses clustered lights, shadows, fog, SSS/specular merge, screen copies and the TAA/FSR2/MetalFX paths, and avoids renderer-selection churn. DLSS evaluates before tone mapping (DLSS guide §3.1), which matches that slot in RenderForwardClustered::_render_scene. Depth of field already runs after the upscale (RendererSceneRenderRD::_render_buffers_post_process_and_tonemap), as RR requires.D1 (v2.2), unchanged; v2.4 adds the upscaler slot.
D2NextGeo draws with compute culling plus hardware raster.
  • Default draw-identity path, (c): one indirect instanced draw over the compacted visible-cluster list. draw_count = 1; instanceCount = the visible-cluster count written by compute; vertexCount = 3 × max triangles per cluster. The vertex shader takes the cluster from gl_InstanceIndex and the triangle from gl_VertexIndex, and collapses triangles beyond the cluster’s count.
  • (a), indirect-count multi-draw with shaderDrawParameters, is adopted only if it beats (c) by ≥15% GPU time at G4-5. Only then do the VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan12Features drawIndirectCount consolidation and the RD/graph indirect-count plumbing happen.
  • (b), compute-expanded streams, is benchmark-only. If ever kept: ≤8M triangles in the main view and ≤2M per shadow split, with overflow resolved by re-cutting at 2× error, never by holes.
(c) needs no new driver work on Vulkan, D3D12 or Metal:
  • On Metal, (c) uses the non-indexed MDCommandBuffer::render_draw_indirect, whose per-draw CPU loop runs once for draw_count 1 and issues one GPU-indirect draw.
  • On D3D12, the zeroed base instance is harmless with firstInstance 0.
(c) costs about 4 B per visible cluster, against about 150 MB worst case per view for (b) (DRV-1, DRV-2, GEO-9).		D2 (v2.2): options (a)/(b) only, with (b) called ‘the sole portable path on Metal’.
D3meshoptimizer stays the builder base. Add meshletutils.cpp and meshletcodec.cpp to modules/meshoptimizer/SCsub. Group-border locking uses the existing SimplifyWithAttribFunc vertex_lock parameter.meshopt_computeClusterBounds, optimizeMeshlet and the meshlet codec are vendored but not compiled (GEO-14, AST-8).D3 (v2.2) wording.
D4NextGI v1 traces with hardware ray tracing (inline ray query in compute) wherever the §3 tier rule selects it. Sequence:
  1. Screen trace.
  2. Ray query against the TLAS.
  3. Hit shaded from the Surface Radiance Cache (SRC), with the card chosen by hit identity.
  4. World Radiance Cache (WRC) at the handoff distance or on a miss.
  5. Sky.
Configurations that fail the tier rule fall back to existing SDFGI in v1. The SDF+SRC+WRC software tier is post-v1 (P6b).		User decision of 2026-11-01. UE 5.8 enables Lumen HWRT by default where supported, deprecated SWRT detail traces in 5.6 and turned mesh-SDF tracing off by default in 5.7. HWRT hit identity removes the unsolved SDF-hit-to-card lookup (GI-2) and the SDF near-field precision risk (GI-3). Every named performance GPU has RT hardware (GI-1, PLN-4).D4 (v2.2): SDF-first v1, with HWRT as a post-v1 tier and an early HWRT-vs-SDF comparator spike (phase map in §12).
D5Ray query in compute only: no RT pipelines, hit shaders or SBTs in v1.
  • RenderingDevice::tlas_build accepts instances with hit_sbt_range 0 (offset 0) when no RT pipeline is used.
  • Ray-query shaders use #version 460 with GL_EXT_ray_query and are compiled only while the HWRT tier is active. Their ShaderRD versions are non-embedded, or filtered in _customize_shader_version for containers without acceleration-structure support, so the shader baker bakes them only for Vulkan, and for Metal from P2.
  • Traversal is opaque-only: gl_RayFlagsOpaqueEXT and a single rayQueryProceedEXT.
  • Workgroup shared memory stays ≤8 KiB.
  • No position fetch, no OpConvertUToAccelerationStructureKHR and no SBT-offset getter, because SPIRV-Cross MSL cannot translate them.
  • A per-shader SPIR-V 1.4 opt-in (an RD parameter plus a ShaderRD flag folded into the cache key) is added only if spirv-val or a driver rejects the default SPIR-V 1.3.
Today a ray-query-only consumer, Metal included, cannot build a populated TLAS: RenderingDevice::tlas_build rejects a zero hit-SBT range, and RenderingDevice::hit_sbt_create requires an RT pipeline. glslang accepts rayQueryEXT only under #version 460. RDNA2 traverses with an LDS stack, hence the shared-memory cap (DRV-10).D5 (v2.2): ray query in compute for a post-v1 tier, conditional on the TLAS-address fix.
D6In v1, HWRT hits are shaded by a surface-cache lookup:
  1. The committed instance custom index (24 bits) selects the NextGI instance record (SRC page table, card set, RT-arena chunk index and offset).
  2. The geometry index selects the surface.
  3. The primitive ID and barycentrics give the object-space hit point and, from the RT-arena positions, the face normal.
  4. The card whose axis best matches the face normal and whose depth matches within 2 card texels supplies the radiance.
  5. If no card qualifies, the WRC is used.
Direct material evaluation at hits (hit lighting) is post-v1 and Epic-only (P6d).		This is the Lumen surface-cache HWRT mode. Hit lighting costs about 4–5× as much: Matrix Awakens reflections on PS5 took 2.44 ms with the surface cache versus 11.54 ms with hit lighting. It also needs material access at hit points.D6 (v2.2): surface cache required for SDF hits, direct hit shading as an HWRT quality option.
D7Virtual-geometry materials resolve through a ShaderCompiler ‘visibility resolve’ variant of the existing scene_forward_clustered shader template, one dispatch per material bucket:
  • A compute pass classifies 8×8 tiles per bucket, and each bucket draws its tile quads with one indirect instanced draw.
  • The fragment shader decodes the visibility ID and runs the material’s vertex() once per triangle vertex (3×) for varyings. It interpolates them with barycentrics and rewrites texture() as textureGrad with analytic derivatives.
  • It then runs the material’s fragment() and light() and the unchanged Forward+ lighting includes.
  • Stage A is the depth/normal-roughness variant; Stage B is the color variant.
Per-material uniform sets are reused, so there is no bindless and no hand-written StandardMaterial3D record interpreter.		Parity comes by construction for features the v2.2 Stage B omitted (FWD-1, GEO-5, AST-5, FWD-11): clustered decals, reflection probes, area lights, projectors, depth and height fog with aerial perspective, SH captures, multi-bounce AO, specular occlusion, the roughness limiter and texture-streaming feedback. It also extends to ShaderMaterials with an eligible vertex(), and drops Option A bindless (DRV-6) from v1.D7 (v2.2): GPU material records for a StandardMaterial3D subset, plus the Option A/B binding decision.
D8Denoising is split by signal:
  • Diffuse is filtered in screen-probe space: spatial filtering across neighbouring probes weighted by depth, normal and hit distance, plus per-probe temporal accumulation. One light per-pixel temporal stabilizer follows.
  • Specular uses per-pixel SVGF/ReLAX-class filtering with hit-distance-aware reprojection.
Each signal has exactly one temporal accumulator. Under the post-v1 DLSS-RR mode (D21, P6i), §2.1 line 8 is not dispatched and line 7’s per-probe history is capped. D8 remains the default and the fallback; P3 adds only default-off switches for this.		Avoids stacking two temporal stages on diffuse (GI-8). Fits the High denoise line of 0.32 ms Ada / 0.80 ms mid-range. The RR switches change nothing on the default path, and each P3 issue’s acceptance criteria check that byte for byte.D8 (v2.2): full SVGF on both signals after a temporally filtered gather.
D9LightmapGI, VoxelGI and SDFGI remain:
  • SDFGI is the automatic v1 fallback for NextGI-enabled environments on configurations without HWRT, or where the auto quality preset rejects HWRT.
  • While NextGI is active in a view, SDFGI and VoxelGI are disabled for that view, so NextGI is the only writer of RB_TEX_AMBIENT and RB_TEX_REFLECTION.
  • Lightmapped instances keep their lightmaps.
  • SH-capture receivers switch to the NextGI buffers.
A single writer prevents double counting (FWD-7), and the fallback reuses shipping code.D9 (v2.2): LightmapGI, VoxelGI and SDFGI kept as lower/fallback tiers.
D10retain_authored stays the import and editor default.
  • Export presets carrying the ‘nextgeo’ feature tag strip the authored surfaces of virtualized meshes through EditorExportPlugin::_customize_resource, leaving the embedded coarse fallback. The tag is added by default to Windows, Linux and macOS desktop presets in projects that contain virtualized meshes.
  • Virtualized surfaces skip generate_lods() and create_shadow_mesh() at import.
  • Total resident geometry memory is gated (§2).
Under retain_authored, a shipped game keeps the authored, LOD and shadow buffers resident beside the page cache, so NextGeo saved no VRAM, and no v2.2 gate measured it (AST-1, GEO-4).D10 (v2.2): stripping opt-in only.
D11The tier is selected by capability inside the active API, using the §3 rule:
  • HWRT is on by default wherever the rule passes. With quality = auto (the default), a ≤5 ms startup microbenchmark then picks High, Medium or the SDFGI fallback and logs the choice with its reason.
  • force and off override the rule.
  • Otherwise the SDFGI fallback runs with a tier reason (§6.3), logged once.
  • SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE is driver knowledge only: Apple9+ on Metal, and on Vulkan a PCI vendor/device-ID allowlist of RT-core architectures (NVIDIA Turing+ RTX, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Xe-HPG/Xe2 Arc). The allowlist keeps GTX driver emulation and RADV emulate_rt off the HWRT tier, and the rule’s own CPU device-type check keeps lavapipe off.
  • A startup self-test at tier selection (one triangle plus a 64-ray fixture against analytic IDs and t) and a crash latch keyed by driver version guard unvalidated drivers (SELFTEST_FAILED, HWRT_FAULTED).
Matches UE 5.8’s documented rule: hardware ray tracing “when supported by the video card, RHI, and operating system”, falling back to software ray tracing otherwise.The v2.2 §12 Gate 5 rule that kept SDF as the default even when HWRT passed.
D12D3D12 DXR is not in v1. It becomes P6a, the first post-v1 item, scoped to ray query in compute (DXR Tier 1.1, SM 6.5).
  • It builds on godotengine/godot PR #118654 plus Skyth’s Mesa spirv2dxil ray-tracing patch through a godot-nir-static fork, not on our own nir_to_dxil work.
  • It is pulled into v1 only if upstream merges both during the v1 window.
v1 policy: new projects default to Vulkan, and an existing project’s API never switches silently.
  • GodotNext changes EditorNode::get_initial_settings so new projects get rendering/rendering_device/driver.windows = "vulkan", a fork-level default that lands in P0. New GodotNext projects give up D3D12’s HDR output and DComp transparency on Windows unless they switch back.
  • In an existing d3d12 project, turning on nextgi/enabled shows a one-time modal, ‘Use Vulkan on Windows for hardware RT’. The switch keeps fallback_to_d3d12 = true, states the HDR/DComp loss and offers a restart.
  • A project that stays on, or falls back to, d3d12 logs D3D12_NO_DXR once and runs SDFGI.
DLSS SR/DLAA on the Direct3D 12 driver needs neither DXR nor P6a. It is a v1 stretch item (pu-dlss-d3d12) that can be cut at any time; until it lands, d3d12 projects get FSR2 with API_UNSUPPORTED. The item implements only the barrier path that the RTX 4080 SUPER machine reports. DLSS-RR on D3D12 waits for P6a.		Neither the pinned Mesa 25.3.1 nor Mesa main can emit RayQuery DXIL; the only implementation is an unreviewed draft. NVIDIA RTX, AMD RDNA2+ and Intel Arc on Windows are all reachable through Vulkan. Upstream writes d3d12 into every new project from EditorNode::get_initial_settings, chosen for HDR output and DComp transparency; keeping it would hide hardware RT on Windows by default. For DLSS, RenderingDeviceDriverD3D12::command_pipeline_barrier is a no-op on the legacy-barrier path, and enhanced barriers are enabled whenever OPTIONS12 reports support (_check_capabilities); whether the RTX 4080 SUPER machine reports it is unverified. The driver also caches PSO, root-signature and descriptor-heap state that NGX would clobber.v2.2 §8.4/§12 P6: D3D12 DXR and Metal RT together as post-v1 P6; upstream’s d3d12 default for new projects.
D13How geometry is represented for ray tracing:
  1. NextGeo: one fixed import-time proxy per mesh.
    • A DAG cut at geometric error ≤ eps_rt = min(max_extent/256, rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/rt_max_error); the setting defaults to 2 cm at import scale.
    • Never view-dependent, with residency independent of raster streaming. This matches UE’s fallback mesh.
  2. Legacy static meshes: a float32-position / uint32-index copy of the coarsest ArrayMesh LOD whose stored edge_length ≤ eps_rt, decoded on the CPU in MeshStorage::mesh_add_surface.
  3. Skinned meshes: a per-instance arena slot that the skinning compute pass writes every frame, with the BLAS refit in place.
Each BLAS holds ≤128k triangles; when the cap binds, the geometry is split into several BLASes per mesh, never coarsened. BLASes are compacted after build (from P1b).
  • RT geometry arena: created only when nextgi/enabled and SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY. It is allocated in 256 MiB chunks with a free list, and chunks never move. Defragmentation means rebuilding the affected BLASes and rewriting their records within the §2.1 line 1b build budget.
  • Mutation: mesh_surface_update_vertex_region and the other surface-mutation paths update the CPU-decoded copy and mark the BLAS dirty. Surfaces updated every frame are treated as dynamic: refit, or excluded with RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED.
Full-detail tracing with NVIDIA cluster AS is a post-v1 vendor-optional tier.		The arena sidesteps four problems (AST-12):
  • R16G16B16A16_UNORM is not a mandatory Vulkan AS vertex format.
  • Positions are normalized per surface AABB.
  • RD has no per-geometry transform.
  • The skinned double buffer swaps addresses every frame.
Hit reconstruction reads arena positions by chunk index + offset, through buffer device address on Vulkan (already required by the SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY rule) and gpuAddress on Metal, with no bindless. The world-unit bound matters for large meshes: at 40 m, max_extent/256 is 15.6 cm, coarser than a 10 cm interior wall (GEO-11). UE 5.8 mainline traces Nanite fallback meshes; native Nanite RT is experimental there, and Mega Geometry exists only in NvRTX.		§7.5/§8.4 (v2.2): ‘one static BLAS per mesh’ built from mesh buffers with AS usage flags.
D14Direct lighting of the SRC is computed in world space:
  • Light set: the scenario’s lights, culled against resident page bounds: scenario->dynamic_lights, a scenario static-light list maintained next to it, and all directional lights. As in SDFGI (RendererSceneCull::_render_scene, GI::SDFGI::pre_process_gi), the set comes from the scenario, never from the camera light buffers or the screen cluster.
  • Bake modes: DYNAMIC lights relight every scheduled pass. STATIC lights dirty pages only when their properties change, and are skipped for lightmapped receivers. DISABLED lights are excluded.
  • Relight cap: ≤256k texels/frame at High, ≤128k at Medium, ≤512k at Epic.
  • Shadows: up to 8 lights per page are shadowed, ranked by unshadowed irradiance at the page centre; further lights add unshadowed light and are counted. Shadows are evaluated at 1/4 card resolution (a 2×2-texel mask) for ≤2 shadowed lights per texel per frame, rotated temporally; the other lights reuse their last mask.
  • Shadow-map reuse: when a card texel projects into a valid camera directional split or positional shadow-atlas tile, that shadow-map lookup is reused. Only the remainder is ray traced, at ≤256k shadow rays/frame at High (128k Medium, 512k Epic).
  • Invalidation: adding, removing, moving or changing a light’s energy, colour, range or shadow setting dirties the pages in its old-plus-new influence AABB. A directional change dirties all resident pages.
update_light_buffers() receives lights already culled by frustum, layer and occlusion, and shadow-atlas space is reserved only for those, so off-screen cards were lit wrongly (FWD-2, GI-4). A moving light previously dirtied nothing (GI-5). The texel and ray caps hold §2.1 line 2b (relight + shadow rays) to 0.14 ms Ada / 0.35 ms mid-range (GI-7).§6.1 Step 5 / §8.3 (v2.2): card lighting at the post-bake_cluster call site.
D15The WRC is a radiance cache, not an irradiance grid:
  • Layout: one WRC per scenario, 4 clipmap levels of 16³ probes centred on the main viewport camera, at 1.0, 2.0, 4.0 and 8.0 m spacing.
  • Each probe holds 8×8 octahedral R11G11B10F radiance with a 1-texel border, prefiltered 4×4 and 2×2 mips for cone lookups, and an 8×8 RG16F depth-moment visibility map.
  • Probes are relocated off geometry by up to 0.4× spacing.
  • Diffuse gather rays hand off to the WRC at 1.5× the local probe spacing.
  • Specular rays trace up to rt_scene_radius and use the WRC only on a miss.
  • SH irradiance is derived for fog and the translucency volume.
Irradiance probes cannot terminate directional or glossy rays, and SDFGI’s 1.6 m grid is too coarse (GI-6). The cache takes about 14 MiB, plus history if double-buffered.§8.3 (v2.2): ‘camera-centered clipmap of irradiance probes’.
D16The authoritative reference is an in-engine progressive path tracer in compute ray query (Vulkan first; the Metal port is in P2).
  • P0 reference v0: an analytic path tracer over a TLAS that the GDScript RD testbed builds from known triangle soups: the furnace box, sealed box A (10 cm wall) and sealed box B (20 cm wall). The test passes in the material table. It does not depend on P1 infrastructure, and G0 closes on these analytic tests only.
  • Furnace: a closed box with Lambertian inner faces (ρ = 0.8), uniform emission Le on every inner face and no other lights. Every pixel must read Le/(1−ρ) within 1%.
  • P1a scene path: Godot scenes (Color Box Room, Concave Courtyard) rendered through the RT arena and CPU TLAS. G1a depends on it.
  • It matches Godot’s StandardMaterial3D BRDF for constant-parameter materials (albedo, roughness, metallic, emission), Godot light units, exposure and sky, and compares linear HDR before tonemapping.
  • It is near-unbiased (64-bounce cap, truncation bias < 1e-5): Russian roulette after 4 bounces, no clamp, no denoiser.
NextGI quality scenes use constant-parameter materials. The LightmapGI cross-check (16 bounces, ULTRA, denoiser off, texture_for_bounces off) is report-only and runs on the P1a scene path. A mismatch larger than 10% opens an investigation, which may conclude LightmapGI is the wrong side.		Nothing in the tree produced the promised ‘path-traced reference’. LightmapGI defaults are truncated and biased (3 bounces, denoiser on). In a closed albedo-0.8 box, bounces beyond the third carry about 41% of the radiance (PLN-6, GI-11). The testbed-built v0 removes the P0→P1 dependency loop (PLN-7), and because the TLAS is built anyway, the reference costs a few weeks.§2/§13 (v2.2): unspecified ‘LightmapGI / high-sample path-traced reference’.
D17Policy for dynamic and translucent content:
  • All opaque instances, skinned ones included, are full NextGI receivers. From P1b their denoiser history is driven by the velocity the NextGI prepass writes (§6.1); P1a is static-scene only.
  • In the HWRT tier, skinned instances also occlude and bounce light through a per-frame BLAS refit:
    • budget: ≤200k skinned triangles per frame on Ada, ≤100k on mid-range;
    • full rebuild every 32 frames;
    • characters beyond 30 m or over budget drop out of the TLAS;
    • 6 cards around the bind-pose bounds, relit every frame.
  • Alpha-blended surfaces are excluded from the TLAS. They get NextGI from a camera froxel translucency volume (1/16 screen × 32 slices, SH-L1, filled from the WRC; about 261k froxels and 6 MiB at 1080p in FP16), and their reflections come from WRC radiance or a ReflectionProbe.
  • Alpha-tested surfaces are excluded from the TLAS by default; a per-material ‘treat as opaque for RT’ override exists.
  • Emissives smaller than 4 card texels must be authored as lights.
HWRT makes skinned BLAS practical; UE 5.8 HWRT traces skinned meshes. Skinned history is valid because MeshStorage already double-buffers skinned vertices for motion vectors (GI-9, PLN-15, GI-13, GI-14).§1 (v2.2) program-wide non-goal ‘No skinned characters’ and §8.6 ‘skinned pixels use probe/legacy GI’.
D18Execution model: a solo lead plus AI agents, one slice at a time.
  • Slice 1 (NextGI, P0-P3) finishes at M3 before Slice 2 runtime work (P4-P5) starts. Only the offline DAG prototype may run earlier, in an agent lane.
  • Within Slice 1, the Metal driver lane (P2 items 1–6 plus G2-1 parity) starts right after G0, in parallel with P1. P2’s NextGI items still wait for G1.
  • Schedule slips consume the §14 cut list in order. Gate failures go through that phase’s outcome rows, and a cut applies only if it removes the failing workload.
  • Six agent lanes (§14). Lane 6, upscalers, takes the DLSS work after G1 (PU) and later P6i/P6j. The three PU-core MIT RHI items (driver-callback stages, Vulkan native interop, Vulkan extension providers) run in lane rhi-vulkan after G0, ordered behind that lane’s P1 items.
  • Neither part of PU blocks any G1–G5 item. If PU review competes with the P3 critical path, PU waits.
Resolves the §12/§14 contradiction (PLN-1) and the missing solo estimate (PLN-16). One human review loop is the bottleneck. The early Metal lane puts ray query on the M5 Max at about month 3–4. PU’s only NextGI coupling is the DENSE velocity row, which lands after p1b-prepass-velocity (inside G1). Starting the module and the renderer-side core pieces after G1 keeps them out of the lead’s busiest window, months 3–8 (P1a, P1b, the Metal lane, G1a/M1/M2), and off RenderForwardClustered::_render_scene, P1b’s hot code. The PU-core items edit rhi-vulkan’s hot code (_register_requested_device_extension, _initialize_device), so keeping one lane per hot file avoids parallel rebases.§12 ‘Slice 2 after P1’ vs §14 ‘Slice 2 after P4’ (v2.2).
D19DLSS Super Resolution and DLAA form an optional Forward+ temporal upscaler for NVIDIA RTX under Vulkan. DLAA is DLSS at scaling_3d_scale 1.0.
  • Implementation: a new module, modules/dlss, calls NGX directly; Streamline is not used for SR. The module is MIT source, and every NGX call sits behind an internal MIT DLSSBackend interface. dlss_backend=ngx compiles dlss_ngx_*.cpp against a developer-supplied NVIDIA/DLSS checkout at the pinned tag (module_dlss_enabled=yes dlss_sdk_path=...); dlss_backend=mock compiles without any SDK, and CI builds and tests it.
  • Nothing from NVIDIA in the tree: no NVIDIA header, library, DLL, sample or documentation text enters the repository, testbed, CI or official binaries. Official GodotNext editor and export-template binaries exclude the module until NVIDIA confirms distribution in writing and counsel signs off (§19).
  • v1 claim: DLSS SR/DLAA joins the v1 claim of the first milestone (M3, then M4) whose gate is signed off after gate GU passes. GU never blocks G1–G5.
  • Platforms: Windows Vulkan is validated. Linux Vulkan and other RTX GPUs are enabled but unvalidated. D3D12 is a v1 stretch item, cuttable at any time (D12). Multiview reports VIEW_UNSUPPORTED. Everything else falls back DLSS → MetalFX temporal → FSR2 with one log line and a ViewportScaling3DReason (§3.2).
User request of 2026-11-02, ‘We need DLSS support as well’.
  • NGX direct, not Streamline: NGX is NVIDIA’s documented SR path on Vulkan for Windows and Linux (DLSS Programming Guide 310.6.0 §2.1, §5.2). It needs only pre-device extension injection and the driver-callback slot MetalFX already uses (MFXTemporalEffect::process). Streamline is Windows-only and needs its interposer or manual hooking of present/acquire, with slInit before any Vulkan call. Its one real advantage, an engine binary holding only MIT Streamline code, is kept as the §16 deferred alternative and as the outcome row if NVIDIA declines NGX-linked builds.
  • SDK outside the tree and official builds: the NVIDIA RTX SDKs License requires downstream terms at least as protective (§2(c)), forbids stand-alone redistribution (§4(b)) and forbids making the SDK subject to an open-source license (§4(e)). Supplement §7.1(b) requires NVIDIA Marks in applications that incorporate NGX. The NGX headers are SPDX LicenseRef-NvidiaProprietary. Precedents: Bevy’s dlss_wgpu has users supply the SDK, and upstream Godot will not take DLSS (godot-proposals #2239).
  • Not a GDExtension: RenderingDevice::driver_callback_add is not script-bound (only get_driver_resource is), and no hook runs before vkCreateInstance.
  • GU does not block M3 or M4: the user asked for DLSS, not for DLSS to gate NextGI v1; the lead’s single review loop is the plan’s bottleneck (D18); and DLSS quality depends on NVIDIA’s model, which GodotNext does not control.
Nothing (new).
D20Temporal-upscaler integration contract: core MIT code with no SDK dependency.
  1. Driver-callback stages: RD::driver_callback_add gains BitField<RDD::PipelineStageBits> p_stages = PIPELINE_STAGE_ALL_COMMANDS_BIT, stored as the command’s self_stages.
  2. Vulkan native interop: RDD gains command_buffer_get_native_handle() and texture_get_native_info() (image, view, format, aspect, mip/layer range and extent). The base class returns 0 or ERR_UNAVAILABLE on Metal and D3D12 (there is no dummy RenderingDeviceDriver). The D3D12 handle and command_buffer_invalidate_cached_state belong to the D3D12 stretch item (D12).
  3. Vulkan extension providers: a composable registry adds optional instance and device extensions and pNext features before vkCreateInstance and vkCreateDevice, and coexists with VulkanHooks and OpenXR. Its validation-message hook and a debug-build-only created-handle registry (DEBUG_ENABLED; never in template_release), populated only under --gpu-validation, implement the object-attributed allowlist rule (§14 Never cut, amended).
  4. TemporalUpscaler interface: an interface and registry add SCALE_EXTERNAL dispatch in the existing pre-tonemap slot, with one context per (viewport, view) (§6.3).
  5. Scaling mode and hints: VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_DLSS is appended as 6, so MAX becomes 7, and an extension-API .txt records the change. DLSS is TEMPORAL. The DLSS entry is appended to the mode hint string at runtime, from the registered upscaler’s display name, only when one is registered.
  6. Velocity: DLSS uses the DENSE row of the §6.1 velocity table, which equals the MetalFX row. MotionVectorsStore is no longer limited to METAL_MFXTEMPORAL_ENABLED; its exact-sentinel, w and reset rules are in that table.
  7. DLSS inputs follow the §6.1 conventions, including the DLSS-only mip bias and rounded jitter phase count. NGX flags: MVJittered off, MVLowRes on, DepthInverted, IsHDR and AutoExposure on.
  8. History reset: Viewport.reset_temporal_history() → RenderingServer.viewport_reset_temporal_history() is a one-shot reset that every temporal upscaler consumes (§6.1).
Items 1–4 are not script-bound in v1. The reset, status and availability APIs are bound.		Snapshot facts at 7cfeb47:
  • RenderingDeviceGraph::add_driver_callback never sets self_stages, while add_compute_list_end and add_draw_list_end do (code reading, not runtime-verified). Callbacks run while the command buffer is recorded (_run_render_commands).
  • The VkCommandBuffer is private to the Vulkan driver’s CommandBufferInfo. Extension registration is private (_register_requested_instance_extension, _register_requested_device_extension). VulkanHooks is a single-owner singleton, and OpenXR implements it.
  • The scaling enum is script-bound with MAX (6 in Viewport.xml and RenderingServer.xml), and CI’s validate_extension_api.sh fails without an expected-change file.
  • Modules initialize before RenderingServer::init builds the hint strings (Main::setup2), and Viewport::_validate_property can adjust the hint.
  • DLSS has no derive-from-depth sentinel (guide §3.6), while the FSR2 path in RenderForwardClustered::_render_scene clears velocity to (-1,-1). derive_motion_vector divides by the previous w, so points behind the previous camera produce non-finite values.
  • DLSS wants a mip bias of log2(r/d) − 1.0 (guide §3.5); RenderSceneBuffersRD::update_samplers applies −0.5. RendererViewport::_configure_3d_render_buffers truncates the jitter phase count, giving 17 for Quality and 23 for Balanced.
  • FSR2 never resets (the reset_accumulation FIXME). FSR2 and MetalFX receive the average-luminance buffer as exposure, the inverse of DLSS’s exposure multiplier (guide §3.9), so v1 uses AutoExposure.
  • FSR2 and MetalFX share one context across views; DLSS needs one per view (guide §3.17).
  • The reset and status APIs are bound because the Upscaler Torture scene calls them from GDScript.
Nothing (new).
D21DLSS Ray Reconstruction is post-v1 (P6i), RTX only and Vulkan first. Its design is fixed now.
  • Activation requires rendering/scaling_3d/dlss/ray_reconstruction (default false), DLSS as the active backend and the NextGI HWRT tier at quality = epic. quality = auto never enables RR.
  • Replaced, per view: §2.1 line 8 (the diffuse per-pixel stabilizer and the specular SVGF/ReLAX are not dispatched) and the upscaler slot (DLSS SR, FSR2 or TAA).
  • Kept: line 7 (gather integration, the probe spatial filter and the bilateral upsample), with the per-probe temporal history capped at 16, 4 or 0, chosen by the P6i spike. Specular is traced at full resolution (Epic) and passed raw after the firefly clamp, so Q16 still applies. The spike also chooses the sampling mode (blue-noise tile or white-noise xxhash32).
  • Guides: diffuse albedo (sky 0.5) and specular albedo (sky 0); world-space normal plus linear roughness, RGBA16F (sky roughness 0.0); specular hit distance, R16F (miss/sky 65504), with row-major world-to-view and view-to-clip matrices; dense motion vectors and depth.
  • Constraints: at least 32 jitter phases, a fixed scale, no exposure input, and depth of field after RR.
  • Fallback: D8 stays the default and the fallback. RR falls back to DLSS SR + D8 with an RR_* reason.
  • Metrics: Q20 (final-image indirect error vs D16 at output resolution) applies Q1’s patch method and Q9’s roughness bins to the final image; Q8 and Q10 apply unchanged. RR must be non-inferior to D8 + DLSS SR within 2 percentage points.
  • What v1 P3 adds: only default-off MIT hooks (hit distance, a sampling-mode switch, a specular-filter bypass, a stabilizer switch and a probe-history cap). Each keeps the default path byte-identical.
  • RR is ‘a unified denoise + upscale model’ that ‘effectively overrides DLSS-SR’ (DLSS-RR guide ch. 1, §3.2), so it cannot replace only the specular denoiser, as v2.3’s P6f step 7 said.
  • Line 7 stays because NextGI diffuse runs at 1/16 rpp (High) and 1/4 rpp (Epic) in probe space; raw sparse probe rays would leave most pixels without a sample.
  • Post-v1, because RR assumes independent samples (RR guide §3.5), costs 5.83–22.92 ms on an RTX 3060 (Table 3), and its guides and bypasses edit P3 critical-path files (scene_forward_clustered.glsl, nextgi_reflect.glsl, nextgi_gather.glsl, denoise.cpp). §14 cut 2 (Epic preset) would remove RR’s precondition anyway.
  • The P3 hooks are push-constant switches inside the issues that already own those files, so P6i does not reopen P3 shaders.
  • Open: whether ‘linear roughness’ means Godot’s perceptual roughness or its square is unverified; the P6i spike decides.
P6f step 7, ‘DLSS-RR replaces the specular denoiser’ (v2.3).
D22DLSS Frame Generation, Multi Frame Generation and NVIDIA Reflex are post-v1, as P6j, in this order:
  1. A spike chooses between Streamline manual hooking on Windows and NGX-direct DLSS-G with a GodotNext-owned pacer. It also settles Linux FG and whether NGX-direct and Streamline can coexist.
  2. Reflex: VK_NV_low_latency2 plus VK_KHR_present_id through the extension-provider registry (D20), or Streamline Reflex if Streamline is chosen.
  3. A HUD-less 3D colour / UI split in the viewport compositor.
  4. DLSS-G on the main window’s swapchain only.
  5. Its own gate, written before the phase starts (minimums in §12).
FG over Vulkan is documented: the NGX-direct *_vk helpers (DLSS-FG guide 310.7.0) and Streamline, where only Dynamic MFG is D3D12-only. The blockers are elsewhere:
  • NGX-direct FG leaves timing and presentation to the application (FG guide §7).
  • Streamline proxies vkQueuePresentKHR and vkAcquireNextImageKHR, requires Streamline Reflex and attaches to a single swapchain.
  • Godot composites the UI into the same target, so there is no HUD-less colour.
  • The FG guide lists Windows 10 20H1 as the OS requirement, yet also documents Linux libraries.
  • MFG needs RTX 50, which the fleet lacks.
SR and DLAA need none of this.		Nothing (new).

6. Target architecture

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

6.1 Proposed frame flow

1. Frame setup (CPU)Dirty regions, light events, RT instance registry, skinning into the RT arena
→
2. PrepassForced depth + normal-roughness + velocity; Slice 2 visibility raster and Stage A
→
3. PRE_OPAQUE effectsCompositor effects, unchanged
→
4. ShadowsSlice 2 virtual shadow cuts; process_gi skipped under NextGI
→
5. NextGI blockSRC, AS updates, relight, WRC, HWRT gather, denoise
→
6. Screen space + lightsSSAO/SSCS/SSR, light buffers, cluster, volumetric fog
→
7. Stage BSlice 2 color resolve for virtual pixels
→
8. Legacy color + postPASS_MODE_COLOR, sky, transparents, history, upscale (TAA / FSR2 / MetalFX / registered temporal upscaler: DLSS SR-DLAA), tonemap

Items tagged Slice 2 arrive with NextGeo (P4-P5); everything else is Slice 1 (P0-P3).

  1. Frame setup (CPU).
    • RendererSceneCull emits NextGI dirty regions (old and new AABB) inside _update_instance, plus light-change events.
    • It fills the NextGI RT instance registry from every GI-contributing instance in the scenario within rt_scene_radius of the main viewport camera, not from the camera cull list. From P1b this includes static MultiMesh and non-baked GridMap octants, fed from the existing MultiMesh CPU cache (_multimesh_make_local) within the 100k instance cap.
    • MeshStorage::update_mesh_instances skins meshes and writes the RT-arena slots of skinned TLAS instances.
    • Slice 2 Page requests and uploads.
  2. Prepass.
    • Forced depth + normal-roughness: the force_depth_pre_pass condition and the depth_pass_mode selection in RenderForwardClustered::_render_scene take the NextGI and NextGeo flags.
    • Velocity, from P1b while NextGI is active: the prepass variants (depth + normal-roughness, and the VoxelGI-id variant) pre-clear velocity and write MOTION_VECTORS for moving and skinned instances, per the upscaler table below.
    • Slice 2 Virtual visibility raster after the prepass clear, before the MSAA resolve and the PRE_OPAQUE compositor effects.
    • Slice 2 Stage A resolve, depth/normal-roughness variant, using the identical guide encoding (§6.3).
  3. PRE_OPAQUE compositor effects, unchanged.
  4. Shadows.
    • Slice 2 Virtual shadow cuts dispatched before the omni cube-shadow loop and the directional batch.
    • Shadow rendering unchanged.
    • gi.process_gi() skipped for views where NextGI is active.
  5. NextGI block, immediately after _render_shadow_end:
    • SRC capture (batched raster, up to 64 cards per render pass; §8.3);
    • AS updates: TLAS rebuild and skinned refit every frame, plus new BLAS builds and compaction copies amortized under a per-frame triangle cap (§2.1 lines 1a and 1b);
    • SRC relight (world-space light set, shadow-map reuse plus ray-query shadows; D14);
    • WRC update and translucency volume;
    • screen trace and HWRT diffuse/specular gather;
    • denoise (D8); from P1b, gather accumulation and denoise read this frame’s prepass velocity;
    • writes RB_TEX_AMBIENT, RB_TEX_REFLECTION and the NextGI ambient volume.
  6. Screen space and lights. SSAO, SSCS and SSR as today; update_light_buffers(); bake_cluster(); _update_volumetric_fog reads the NextGI ambient volume.
  7. Slice 2 Stage B resolve, color variant, for virtual pixels.
  8. Legacy color and post. PASS_MODE_COLOR (load/clear rules below), sky, SSS, specular merge, transparents (the forward alpha branch samples the translucency volume), previous-frame color copy (post-transparent; full-resolution full-mip under using_nextgi), TAA/FSR2/MetalFX or a registered temporal upscaler (SCALE_EXTERNAL; DLSS SR/DLAA in v1, DLSS-RR in P6i), tonemap.

Why this order.

  1. NextGI no longer needs camera light buffers (D14), so it sits next to the old process_gi position and keeps the existing ordering.
  2. NextGI’s temporal passes run before the color and motion passes that write velocity today, so the velocity they consume must come from the prepass.
  3. Raster card capture shares RENDER_LIST_SECONDARY with the shadow batch, so it runs only after _render_shadow_end.
  4. Slice 2 raster and Stage A must come after the prepass clear and before the MSAA resolve and PRE_OPAQUE effects, and virtual shadow culling before the cube-shadow loop.
  5. The NextGI ambient volume must exist before _update_volumetric_fog.

Load/clear rules.

  • When Stage B writes pixels, clear color to the linear clear_color (alpha 0 when separate specular is on) and specular to 0 over the render region before Stage B. Then run PASS_MODE_COLOR with load_color = true (RD::DRAW_DEFAULT_ALL).
  • Frames where only NextGI runs clear color and specular as today. On the TAA path from P1b, the velocity attachment is loaded rather than cleared (per-attachment RD draw flags), so the prepass velocity survives.
  • Never use RD::DRAW_IGNORE_ALL: it maps to ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_DONT_CARE.

Velocity per upscaler path.

  • P1a is static-scene only. NextGI reprojects with camera-only motion derived from depth, and velocity behaviour is unchanged.
  • From P1b, while NextGI is active, the Step 2 prepass writes MOTION_VECTORS for moving and skinned instances (every surface RENDER_LIST_MOTION draws today). The opaque color pass and RENDER_LIST_MOTION do not re-write velocity for them.
  • NextGI’s gather accumulation and denoise read that velocity where the normal-roughness guide carries the dynamic flag, and derive camera motion from depth elsewhere. While NextGI is active, _fill_render_list sets INSTANCE_FLAGS_DYNAMIC for these instances on every path; today the FSR2 and MetalFX paths route them to RENDER_LIST_MOTION instead.
  • Slice 2 Virtual pixels write velocity in Step 2 on every path, and the Step 2 pre-clear also runs whenever virtual geometry draws.
PathStep 2 pre-clearOpaque color passPost-opaque
TAA without upscaling(0, 0, 0, 0), moved from the color-pass clearLoads the velocity attachment; writes velocity only for instances the prepass did not writeNo motion pass, as today
FSR2(-1, -1, 0, 0), the FSR2 invalid sentinel, which FSR2 derives from depthNo velocity, as todayThe clear-only pass on get_velocity_only_fb() is skipped. RENDER_LIST_MOTION draws only movers the prepass did not write (legacy movers when only NextGeo runs).
MetalFX (SCALE_MFX)(-1, -1, 0, 0)No velocity, as todaymotion_vectors_store (PRESERVE_WRITTEN) writes camera velocity only where a texel is bit-equal to the pre-clear (-1.0, -1.0), never by a ≤ −1 test. It writes 0 where the previous w ≤ 1e-6, and 0 everywhere on a reset frame. RENDER_LIST_MOTION as for FSR2.
DLSS SR/DLAA (SCALE_EXTERNAL, velocity_mode DENSE)(-1, -1, 0, 0)No velocityAs MetalFX. DLSS has no sentinel, so no (-1, -1) or non-finite texel may reach evaluate (Q21 = 0).

When neither NextGI (from P1b) nor virtual geometry is active, every path keeps today’s velocity behaviour, apart from the motion_vectors_store w and reset rules. DLSS then uses today’s MetalFX behaviour: motion_vectors_store writes camera velocity for every texel, then RENDER_LIST_MOTION draws movers.

Temporal upscaler inputs (D20), per view.

  • Textures: color = the internal texture (linear HDR, pre-tonemap); depth = the reverse-Z depth view (DEPTH aspect); velocity = RG16F, UV-space previous − current, jitter removed, y-down; output = the upscaled RGBA16F storage texture.
  • Scalars: MV scale (DLSS InMVScale) = internal_size; jitter (InJitterOffset) = taa_jitter × internal_size × 0.5 px (sign unverified, confirmed with the dev-DLL overlay); near, far and fovy; row-major world-to-view and view-to-clip matrices; delta time; reset.
  • Omitted: no reactive mask and no exposure texture (AutoExposure); pre-exposure (InPreExposure) 1.
  • DLSS only: sampler bias log2(render/display) − 1.0; jitter phase count rounded to nearest (8/18/24/32/72 for DLAA/Quality/Balanced/Performance/Ultra Performance). FSR2, MetalFX and TAA keep −0.5 and the truncated count.
  • Contexts: one per (viewport, view). FSR2 and MetalFX keep their shared context unchanged.

History reset. Viewport.reset_temporal_history() sets a one-shot flag for the next frame, and render-buffer reconfiguration sets it too. DLSS InReset, FSR2 reset_accumulation (closing its FIXME in RenderForwardClustered::_render_scene) and the MetalFX reset parameter consume it. NextGI histories do not consume it in v1 (§18.2).

Multiple views.

  • There is one RT scene and one TLAS rebuild per scenario per frame, anchored on the main (active) viewport camera and shared by all views.
  • The SRC and WRC are per scenario: AS updates, SRC capture and relight, and the WRC update run once per scenario per frame. Gather and denoise run per view.
  • NextGI is disabled in ReflectionProbe and preview renders, which sample the WRC and sky only.
  • §2.1 budgets apply to the main view. Each secondary view carries its own gather-and-denoise cost (§2.1 lines 4–8). G3’s two-viewport test checks, by timestamps, that a second view adds no AS, SRC or WRC passes and costs ≤ the sum of lines 4–8.
  • In v1, DLSS reports VIEW_UNSUPPORTED for view_count > 1, because MotionVectorsStore applies a single centre-camera reprojection to every view (§4.6). Split-screen viewports, each single-view with its own temporal upscaler context, are supported.

6.2 Ownership boundaries

LayerOwnsMust not own
Scene resourcesVirtualGeometryData payload (including embedded coarse renderer fallback + fixed proxy) on ArrayMesh, preserved authored surfaces, per-surface eligibilityGPU residency or per-frame cut state
Editor/importCluster DAG build, proxy/Stage-2 card predata, .ngm writing + export remap metadata, import diagnosticsRuntime page eviction policy
RenderingServer storagePersistent GPU instance records, one NextGI RT scene per scenario (instance registry, chunked RT geometry arena, BLAS/TLAS RIDs), material-bucket tables, page arenasScene-tree traversal policy
Forward+ rendererPer-(instance, surface, view) routing, Stage A/B visibility/resolve passes, legacy blending order, debug viewsAsset simplification or file IO
NextGI subsystemTier selection (with the startup self-test and crash latch), quality preset choice, world-space light set, SRC/WRC, TLAS management and budgets, invalidation, gather, translucency volume, denoiser, reference path tracerMaterial authoring or cluster LOD decisions
EffectsReusable guide, history and reprojection helpers used by the NextGI denoiserScene representation or GI sampling policy

Vendor upscaler module (modules/dlss)DLSSBackend (NGX or mock), NGX init, features and evaluate, Vulkan extension requirements, NGX validation attribution, export pluginRenderer policy, NextGI quality decisions
