6.3 Data contracts

Each contract is stated once, here; other sections refer to it. The Slice 1 contracts (GPU instance record RT fields, RT hit record, NextGI outputs, the tier reason enum, the RT-scene values of the Fallback enum) are fixed in P0-P1a. The Slice 2 contracts are fixed in P4, before any Slice 2 runtime code (D18). The upscaler contracts (a)–(c) below are fixed when PU starts after G1, before any module code; (d), a PU-core contract, is fixed after G0.

  1. Visibility ID Slice 2: R32 = 25-bit visible-cluster index | 7-bit triangle (at most 128 triangles per cluster). Stage A writes a separate stable instance-ID guide used for denoiser rejection on virtual pixels. Legacy pixels use depth, normal and velocity rejection only.
  2. GPU instance record: current and previous transform; current and previous world AABB; mesh/page-table index; per-surface material override indices and eligibility mask; layer mask, cast-shadow mode, GI mode and lightmap instance data; skeleton presence (NextGeo exclusion trigger); instance shader-uniform presence (NextGeo exclusion trigger in v1); double-precision handling. RT fields: TLAS instance index, BLAS handle, RT-arena chunk index + offset, SRC page-table handle, card-set index, and RT mask bits (static, skinned, alpha-as-opaque override, optional far). A single material-override index is insufficient. Dirty regions (old and new AABB) are emitted inside RendererSceneCull::_update_instance, before prev_transformed_aabb is overwritten. free, scenario change and the early-return paths get explicit hooks.
  3. Exclusive dispatch per (instance, surface, view) Slice 2: virtual data is an optional payload on the existing ArrayMesh mesh RID, with no second mesh RID and no new instance base type. A multi-surface mesh renders its eligible surfaces through virtual cuts and keeps excluded surfaces on the legacy lists, never dropping or double-drawing either.
    • FLAG_VIRTUAL is set at surface-cache build. A routing table in _fill_render_list, keyed by pass mode across its six call sites, selects virtual or legacy per (instance, surface, view).
    • next_pass, material_overlay and stencil-reading chains make the whole chain ineligible.
    • MultiMesh, particles and GridMap octant cells ignore VirtualGeometryData in v1, with a counted editor warning. Baked GridMap (GridMap::make_baked_meshes) stays legacy in v1.
    • Any ArrayMesh surface mutation detaches VirtualGeometryData before the next frame.
  4. Material-bucket record Slice 2: resolve shader variant, per-material uniform set, material_feedback_index for texture streaming, fallback reason (D7).
  5. Cluster record Slice 2: per-cluster culling sphere/cone; group_lod {sphere, error} and parent_lod {sphere, error} stored identically for every cluster of a group; refined_group index; page ID; vertex/index offsets; surface/material ID. Parent LOD spheres enclose child LOD spheres, and error is monotonic via max(). Projected error uses closest-point distance, the instance’s maximum scale and lod_bias.
  6. Guide encoding Slice 2: encode24 normals; roughness × 127/255, flipped for INSTANCE_FLAGS_DYNAMIC; the VoxelGI instance index written, or VoxelGI excluded for virtual pixels. The D7 depth/normal-roughness variant meets it by construction.
  7. RT hit record: instance custom index (24 bits) → NextGI instance record; geometry index → surface; primitive + barycentrics → RT-arena positions (chunk index + offset, read through buffer device address on Vulkan and gpuAddress on Metal) → face normal → card per D6. A mesh split into several BLASes under the 128k-triangle cap (D13) gets one TLAS instance and one record per BLAS, sharing the SRC page table and card set.
  8. NextGI outputs:
    • NextGI is the only writer of RB_TEX_AMBIENT and RB_TEX_REFLECTION at the resolution process_gi would allocate.
    • using_nextgi joins the opaque-GI condition that sets INSTANCE_DATA_FLAG_USE_GI_BUFFERS.
    • Lightmapped instances keep their lightmaps; SH-capture receivers switch to NextGI.
    • Reflection probes blend with weight (1 − NextGI confidence).
    • SDFGI and VoxelGI are disabled for the view.
  9. Tier reason enum (why NextGI runs SDFGI; §3 rule): NO_RAY_QUERY, NOT_HW_ACCELERATED, CPU_DEVICE, D3D12_NO_DXR, METAL_SYNC_MODE, METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS, SETTING_OFF, AS_MEMORY, SELFTEST_FAILED, HWRT_FAULTED.
    • Each driver returns its reason alongside SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY. CPU_DEVICE comes from the rule’s device-type check, and AS_MEMORY is raised when the TLAS/BLAS memory budget cannot be met.
    • SELFTEST_FAILED comes from the startup self-test. HWRT_FAULTED comes from the crash latch, a flag under user:// keyed by driver version that is set on device loss while HWRT is active and holds until the driver changes or hardware_ray_tracing = force.
    • When quality = auto picks the SDFGI fallback on a device that passes the rule, no tier reason is set; the one-time log records the microbenchmark result and GPU class instead.
  10. Fallback enum, per (instance, surface): a separate shared enum used by import, the editor inspector, runtime stats, the debug overlay and tests.
    • RT scene (instance not traced): NOT_IN_RT_SCENE (outside rt_scene_radius or solid-angle culled), RT_INSTANCE_CAP, RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED, ALPHA_TESTED_RT_EXCLUDED.
    • Viewport (every surface of a NextGeo view stays legacy): MSAA, MULTIVIEW, DEBUG_DRAW.
    • Surface or instance (NextGeo surface stays legacy): NEXT_PASS_OR_OVERLAY, VERTEX_LIGHTING, BAKED_GRIDMAP, MUTATED_MESH, UNSUPPORTED_DERIVATIVE, plus the v2.2 reasons: alpha, skinning, unsupported custom shader feature, instance uniforms, lightmapped instance, MultiMesh/particles, missing page.

Upscaler contracts (D20).

  1. Upscaler reason enum (ViewportScaling3DReason, set by the §3.2 resolution order): NONE, RENDERER_UNSUPPORTED, API_UNSUPPORTED, MODULE_DISABLED, NOT_NVIDIA_RTX, DRIVER_TOO_OLD, RUNTIME_MISSING, EXTENSION_MISSING, PROJECT_ID_MISSING, FEATURE_CREATE_FAILED, SCALE_UNSUPPORTED, VIEW_UNSUPPORTED. Append-only; P6i appends the RR values. Separate from the tier reason enum (item 9) and the per-instance Fallback enum (item 10).
  2. TemporalUpscaler: get_display_name(); get_availability() → reason; get_render_size(target, requested_scale); velocity_mode() = DENSE; mip_bias_offset(); jitter_phase_count(target, render); ensure_context(rb, view); process(rb, view, inputs); free_contexts(rb); shutdown(), called before RD teardown. Inputs are as in §6.1.
  3. History reset signal: one-shot, per viewport.
  4. Validation attribution: extension providers receive each debug-utils message, and the debug-build-only created-handle registry (DEBUG_ENABLED; never in template_release), populated only under --gpu-validation, decides GodotNext attribution (§14 Never cut, amended).

7. NextGeo design — Nanite-class virtual geometry

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

7.1 Offline DAG builder

  1. Hook: ImporterMesh::build_virtual_geometry() sits beside generate_lods(). Scene importer options: meshes/generate_virtual_geometry plus a per-mesh generate/virtual_geometry override. OBJ importer options: generate_virtual_geometry and virtual_geometry_fallback_mode, with no per-mesh overrides. Single-mesh import mode runs the build after merge_importer_meshes. Virtualized surfaces skip generate_lods() and create_shadow_mesh().
  2. Clusterize: wire meshoptimizer meshlet construction (at most 128 triangles per cluster, §6.3) through new SurfaceTool function-pointer hooks or a dedicated modules/nextgeo builder. meshletutils.cpp and meshletcodec.cpp are added to modules/meshoptimizer/SCsub (D3). If the optional meshoptimizer module is disabled, import must skip cleanly like current LOD generation.
  3. Group and simplify: build one DAG per mesh over all eligible opaque surfaces. The material/surface ID is an attribute seam, so collapses along seams are allowed, and every cluster stays single-material, which keeps per-(instance, surface, view) routing (§6.3) intact. Partition adjacent clusters into groups, simplify each group with attribute-aware simplification, and re-cluster. Lock borders only against ineligible sibling surfaces, through the existing vertex_lock parameter of SurfaceTool::SimplifyWithAttribFunc. Record why each excluded surface is legacy-only.
  4. LOD bounds and hierarchy: store group-shared LOD bounds per §6.3. Every cluster of a group carries identical group_lod and parent_lod {sphere, error}; parent spheres are the merged enclosure of child spheres, never recomputed from simplified geometry; error is monotonic via max(). Build an offline BVH8 over cluster groups, with merged LOD spheres and max parent error per node, and store it in the DAG header. G4-1 property-tests both rules; G4-2 bounds the root cut and DAG depth on a multi-material kit asset.
  5. Quantize and encode: quantized positions/normals/tangents/UVs and meshlet-local indices. Validate normal/UV seam behavior, tangent handedness, and double-sided flags against legacy renders.
  6. Build dependents: (a) Root pages ship inside VirtualGeometryData and render through the virtual pipeline while children stream. A legacy-format coarse mesh exists only for auxiliary views, tiers without virtual geometry and strip mode. (b) One fixed RT proxy per mesh: the DAG cut at geometric error ≤ eps_rt = min(max_extent/256, rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/rt_max_error), where /256 is half a card texel at 128-texel cards and the setting defaults to 2 cm at import scale. Each BLAS is capped at 128k triangles; when the cap binds, the proxy is split into several BLASes rather than coarsened. Float32 positions and uint32 indices, never view-dependent. It is also used for SRC card and material captures and for SDFGI/VoxelGI voxelization. (c) Editor bounds/preview metadata. (d) Stage-2 per-cluster-group axis-dominant Surface Radiance Cache card descriptors (P5), replacing the Stage-1 cards of up to 12 normal-clustered cards per mesh (§8.3). Import never overwrites authored ArrayMesh surfaces under the default retain_authored (§7.2). Collision/navigation shapes remain built from full authored geometry and do not read streamed pages.

7.2 Resource and file format

PieceProposed shapeNotes
VirtualGeometryDataNew sub-resource referenced by ArrayMesh (not a new Mesh subclass), in scene/resources/3d/virtual_geometry_data.{h,cpp}Holds the DAG header summary, per-surface eligibility mask, root pages, legacy-format coarse mesh, fixed RT proxy, Stage-2 SRC card descriptors, .ngm page-file UID/path, and format version. Any ArrayMesh surface mutation detaches it before the next frame (§6.3, MUTATED_MESH); the mesh then renders and traces as a legacy mesh, whose mutation hooks mark its BLAS dirty (D13). Keeping the existing ArrayMesh RID and the authored surface arrays in the editor means LightmapGI::bake (the D16 cross-check), Mesh::get_faces(), create_trimesh_shape(), navmesh baking, and MeshInstance3D picking work uncorrupted.
.ngm sidecar & PCK exportVersioned paged binary, one .ngm per mesh; proposed 64–128 KiB pages as a starting hypothesis; a cluster group lies within one page (§7.3)Layout: EditorExportPlatform reads each exported file wholly into RAM via FileAccess::get_file_as_bytes, so pages are split as one .ngm per virtualized mesh, never a multi-GB project monolith. Imported meshes write .godot/imported/<key>-<md5>.ngm; embedded meshes use the stable key scene path + unique mesh name. External save_to_file/enabled and standalone .res/.tres meshes write a deterministic sibling <mesh_path>.ngm. Stale .ngm files are removed on reimport.
Export: a built-in EditorExportPlugin whose _export_file reads an importer-written .ngm manifest and calls add_file(path, bytes, false) for each .ngm, independent of _customize_resource. .ngm is not a recognized resource extension, so the stock export loop never exports it twice or loads it during customization. Selected-scenes export mode is supported; G4-4 checks that each .ngm appears in the PCK exactly once.
Encryption: FileAccessEncrypted::open_and_parse buffers whole encrypted files in RAM to verify a whole-file MD5, and plugin files cannot bypass the preset’s encryption filters. The check runs before any byte is written (preset validation, or the start of export_project_files once the file list is known). The rule stays: never silently weaken encryption, and require an explicit *.ngm exclude filter. Seekable AES-CFB .ngm reads are post-v1.
Reader: unencrypted PCKs seek in O(1) via FileAccessPack::seek. One handle per pack with PackedData offsets (bounded LRU handle pool). ZIP exports store .ngm uncompressed or reject it.
DAG headerPer-cluster culling sphere/cone; group-shared group_lod/parent_lod {sphere, error}; refined-group index; page mapping; surface/material IDs; root cluster IDs; the BVH8 over cluster groups; total triangle countsFormat frozen in P4. Uploaded to GPU only for meshes with live virtual instances in the scene, under the same VRAM budget as pages — not unconditionally for every imported mesh. Version and endian/word-size rules must be explicit.
Fallback mode + GPU readback safetyPer-importer fallback-mode option (§7.1): retain_authored (import and editor default) or strip_authored_to_coarse (explicit opt-in), plus default export stripping for presets with the nextgeo feature tag (D10)Critical readback invariant: ArrayMesh::Surface stores metadata only. ArrayMesh::_set_surfaces uploads arrays to MeshStorage and drops them, while surface_get_arrays() and the serializer ArrayMesh::_get_surfaces() read back from GPU buffers via MeshStorage::mesh_get_surface. Skipping GPU upload in MeshStorage would make importer saves and EditorExportPlatform customization / .tres→.res resaves write empty meshes (while headless RendererDummy::MeshStorage CPU tests pass blindly because dummy storage retains CPU copies). Therefore retain_authored keeps normal GPU buffer upload in the editor, so saves, LightmapGI::bake, and create_trimesh_shape() stay intact.
Export stripping: export presets with the nextgeo feature tag strip authored surfaces of virtualized meshes by default through EditorExportPlugin::_customize_resource, leaving the embedded coarse fallback (D10). The tag is added by default to Windows, Linux and macOS desktop presets in projects that contain virtualized meshes. On stripped meshes, runtime surface_get_arrays consumers (Mesh::get_faces, create_trimesh_shape) see the coarse fallback. Import-time collision is unaffected. strip_authored_to_coarse strips at import instead, with prominent inspector warnings. G5-4 gates the default-stripped export at ≤0.5× the discrete-LOD baseline’s total resident geometry VRAM.

7.3 Runtime storage, streaming, and culling

  • GPU arenas: new virtual_mesh_storage owns paged vertex/index arenas, cluster records, instance records, page table, free lists, and upload staging. Shared residency belongs in storage singletons, not a viewport-local structure.
  • Residency: a group lies within one page; refine into a group only when all its pages are resident; load parents before children; evict children first; only the main view issues page requests. Root pages stay resident. Requests feed a CPU/IO streamer modeled on TextureStreaming that prioritizes visible, front-to-back pages, enforces a project VRAM budget, decompresses on worker threads, and uploads within 0.4 ms Ada / 1.0 ms mid-range of GPU time per frame. G4-1 fuzzes random resident page sets.
  • Cut selection: GPU instance cull (frustum + previous-frame Hi-Z) over persistent instance records; level-synchronous BVH traversal (one indirect dispatch per level, no persistent threads); cluster test with group LOD bounds at projected error ≤ rendering/nextgeo/max_pixel_error (default 1.0 px at internal resolution). Projected error uses closest-point distance, the instance’s maximum scale and lod_bias (§6.3). Under temporal upscalers, max_pixel_error stays measured at internal resolution in v1. Whether to bias the cut toward output resolution is a P5 report-only evidence item (p5-hierarchical-cull-hiz), measured under FSR2 Quality and, if GU is signed off, DLSS Quality; DLSS guide §2.2 asks engines to support negative LOD bias for textures and geometry.
  • GPU Hi-Z: build a depth pyramid from rendered depth; use two-phase occlusion (draw previously visible, build Hi-Z, cull/draw newly visible) to avoid one-frame holes. This replaces—not extends—the CPU baked-occluder path for virtual content.
  • Compaction: compact surviving clusters into the visible-cluster list consumed by draw path (c) (D2). Indirect-count plumbing is built only if (a) wins G4-5.
  • Explicit auxiliary-view policy: directional splits and positional faces run dedicated cluster cuts. Shadow cuts use error ≤ 1 shadow texel and issue no page requests. A positional light whose cut changes because of residency is marked shadow-dirty. Shadow sub-budget: 0.6 ms Ada / 1.5 ms mid-range. Other auxiliary INSTANCE_GEOMETRY_MASK renders — GPUParticlesCollisionHeightField3D orthographic heightfield captures, ReflectionProbe updates, SDFGI/VoxelGI voxelizations, SRC card and material captures — draw the fixed proxy or the legacy-format coarse mesh from VirtualGeometryData rather than multiplying cluster-cut passes or forcing full legacy GPU buffer uploads.

7.4 Visibility and material resolve

  • Hardware-rasterize clusters through a vertex shader that pulls cluster vertices from storage buffers. Write depth, velocity per the §6.1 upscaler table (including the DLSS DENSE row, gated in G5-7 if GU is signed off), and the R32 visibility ID (25-bit visible-cluster index | 7-bit triangle, §6.3). The v1 promise is hardware-raster continuous LOD at ≤ 1 px projected error; a software micro-raster for subpixel triangles is post-v1 (P6h). If cull + visibility misses its ceiling at 1 px, G5’s outcome row allows only a documented 2 px revision with measured FLIP impact, or funding P6h.
  • Draw identity (D2): compute culling feeds hardware raster through one of three paths. G4-5 measures compaction + indirect submit only, at Dense City scale: ≤0.16 ms GPU Ada / ≤0.40 ms mid-range, plus ≤0.20 ms CPU. Culling is measured inside G5-1’s cull + Hi-Z + visibility line (0.8 / 2.0 ms), with ≥1M DAG clusters reachable and the main view, 4 directional splits and positional shadow faces all cut.
PathMechanismv1 status
(c) single indirect instanced drawOne indirect draw over the compacted visible-cluster list: draw_count = 1; instanceCount = the visible-cluster count written by compute; vertexCount = 3 × max triangles per cluster. The vertex shader takes the cluster from gl_InstanceIndex and the triangle from gl_VertexIndex, and collapses triangles beyond the cluster’s count. About 4 B per visible cluster.default No new driver work. Vulkan’s gl_InstanceIndex needs no shaderDrawParameters; on D3D12 the zeroed base instance is harmless with firstInstance 0; on Metal, the non-indexed render_draw_indirect with draw_count 1 issues one GPU-indirect draw, so the MDCommandBuffer per-draw CPU loop runs once.
(a) indirect-count multi-drawOne draw per visible cluster through drawIndirectCount, with cluster identity from shaderDrawParameters (gl_DrawID/gl_BaseInstance).conditional Adopted only if it beats (c) by ≥15% GPU time at G4-5. Only then are the VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan12Features drawIndirectCount consolidation and the RD/graph indirect-count plumbing built.
(b) compute-expanded streamsCompute expands visible triangles into an index/vertex stream carrying the cluster ID; about 150 MB worst case per view.benchmark only If ever kept: ≤8M triangles in the main view and ≤2M per shadow split, with overflow resolved by re-cutting at 2× error, never by holes.
  • Material-bucket resolve (D7): virtual-geometry materials resolve through a ShaderCompiler “visibility resolve” variant of the existing scene_forward_clustered shader template, one pass per material bucket. A compute pass classifies 8×8 tiles per bucket, and each bucket draws its tile quads with one indirect instanced draw. The fragment shader decodes the visibility ID, runs the material’s vertex() for varyings and interpolates them with barycentrics, then runs the material’s fragment() and light() and the unchanged Forward+ lighting includes. Per-material uniform sets are reused, so there is no bindless and no hand-written StandardMaterial3D record interpreter.
  • Stage A and Stage B: Stage A is the depth/normal-roughness variant, run in §6.1 Step 2 after the visibility raster with the legacy guide encoding plus a stable instance-ID guide (§6.3). Stage B is the color variant, run in Step 7 after the NextGI block, SSAO/SSCS/SSR and _update_volumetric_fog, before legacy PASS_MODE_COLOR under the §6.1 load/clear rules. Parity comes by construction: clustered decals, reflection probes, area lights, projectors, depth and height fog with aerial perspective, SH captures, multi-bounce AO, specular occlusion, the roughness limiter, and texture-streaming feedback. ShaderMaterials with an eligible vertex() resolve the same way. Budget: Stage A+B 1.0 ms Ada / 2.5 ms mid-range.
  • Resolve gates: G4-6 tests the prototype at the finest cut: FLIP mean ≤0.01 vs forward on the Feature Parity Room, and GPU time ≤1.05× forward PASS_MODE_COLOR at 1080p and 1440p on the RTX 4080 SUPER. If it is slower, G4’s outcome row is a §2/§3 scope review with its own estimate; Forward+ has no deferred lighting path, so there is no G-buffer fallback. G5-5 gates FLIP ≤0.02 vs forward at the default cut.
  • Derivatives and feedback: analytic barycentric derivatives drive textureGrad; vertex() runs per triangle vertex (3×) for varyings; Stage A/B write texture-streaming feedback per material bucket, without the one-instance-per-subgroup shortcut.

Residual parity table. These blocks force per-surface fallback in v1, with a reason from the per-(instance, surface) Fallback enum (§6.3), which is separate from the NextGI tier reasons (§3). Every other scene_forward_clustered block comes through the resolve variant unchanged.

BlockFallback reasonWhy
Vertex lighting modeVERTEX_LIGHTINGLighting is evaluated per vertex and interpolated; the resolve shades per pixel.
next_pass / material_overlay / stencil chainsNEXT_PASS_OR_OVERLAYExtra passes would redraw the authored mesh against a differently tessellated virtual depth. The whole chain, base surface included, is ineligible.
Position-altering vertex()unsupported custom shader featureDisplacing or overriding position/projection invalidates offline bounds and the cut.
dFdx/dFdy/fwidth of non-varying expressionsUNSUPPORTED_DERIVATIVEAnalytic derivatives exist only for barycentric-interpolated varyings.
Screen-texture readsalphaForward+ already treats screen, depth and normal-roughness texture reads as alpha, so these surfaces are not opaque.
MSAA viewsMSAAThe visibility buffer is single-sample; the whole view falls back.
MultiviewMULTIVIEWXR is post-v1 (P6h).
Alpha scissor/hashalphaThe visibility raster does not evaluate materials, so it cannot discard; alpha-tested virtual geometry is post-v1 (P6h).

Blended content, wireframe/points, and priority overlaps stay forward after the resolve; transparent sibling surfaces of a virtualized mesh stay on legacy lists.

7.5 Shadows, proxies, and interaction

  • v1 shadow views select dedicated cluster cuts (≤ 1 shadow texel error, §7.3) per virtualized (instance, surface, view), dispatched in §6.1 Step 4 before the omni cube-shadow loop and the directional batch inside _render_shadow_begin/_render_shadow_end, and write current shadow atlases alongside legacy shadow casters. Fixed atlas resolution and four directional splits still cap shadow sharpness until Virtual Shadow Maps land in P6h.
  • Virtual Shadow Maps are P6h because page allocation, invalidation by light/view/residency, and cluster cuts per shadow page are a system of their own.
  • RT proxy: see D13: one fixed proxy per mesh (several BLASes if the 128k-triangle cap binds), built when the HWRT tier is active within §2.1 line 1b’s per-frame triangle cap, and resident independently of raster streaming. Proxy BLASes join the NextGI TLAS in P5. G5-6 is the proxy-mismatch check: Color Box Room and Concave Courtyard rebuilt with virtualized meshes, plus a single mesh at least 40 m in extent with 10 cm interior walls. NextGeo itself is tier-independent: virtual raster runs on any Forward+ Vulkan, D3D12 or Metal device that passes its own capability check.
  • Physics raycasts, navigation, CSG, LightmapGI::bake, and editor picking do not consume streamed GPU pages in v1. In the editor and in retain_authored exports they consume intact authored ArrayMesh surfaces/collision shapes; in default-stripped exports, runtime surface_get_arrays consumers see the coarse fallback (§7.2). Debug views flag stripped meshes.

8. NextGI design — Lumen-class global illumination

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.
v2.2 → v2.3: hardware ray tracing is the v1 tracer wherever the §3 tier rule selects it, on Vulkan and on Apple9+ (M3 and later) Metal (D4, D11). It is validated on Ada (Windows) and the M5 Max only, and enabled but unvalidated elsewhere (§10). v2.2’s SDF-first tracer with HWRT as a post-v1 tier is gone: the SDF+SRC+WRC software tier moves to P6b (§8.9), and configurations without HWRT run existing SDFGI in v1.

8.1 Solver layering

1. Screen traceNextGI-owned Hi-Z, shared with SSR when both are on
→
2. HWRT ray queryInline ray query in compute against the TLAS (D5)
→
3. SRC hit shadingCard selected by hit identity (D6)
→
4. WRC radiance cacheDiffuse handoff at 1.5× local probe spacing, plus misses (D15); sky last
→
5. Final gatherScreen probes at the preset’s rays per pixel, importance-sampled from previous-frame lighting
→
6. DenoiseSplit by signal, one temporal accumulator each (D8). The post-v1 DLSS-RR mode replaces line 8 and the upscaler (D21, P6i).

Diffuse and specular are separate signals. Diffuse rays hand off to the WRC at the handoff distance; glossy rays (roughness ≤0.4, ≤0.25 at Medium) trace up to rt_scene_radius, need roughness-aware direction logic, and cannot reuse diffuse probe blur as a substitute.

Quality presets (rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/quality = auto | medium | high | epic, default auto):

  • Medium: 1/32 rpp diffuse; reflections only for roughness ≤0.25, at quarter resolution; relight cap 128k texels. Every §2.1 line is High × 0.5 except line 1a, for a total ≤0.87 ms Ada / ≤2.18 ms mid-range.
  • High: 1/16 rpp diffuse; 1/4 rpp half-resolution reflections for roughness ≤0.4; relight cap 256k texels.
  • Epic: 1/4 rpp diffuse; 1 rpp full-resolution reflections; relight cap 512k texels. Epic total = 2× High; per-line values as tabled in §2.1.
  • auto: picks High, Medium or the SDFGI fallback from a ≤5 ms startup microbenchmark on the G0 ray-query fixture plus a coarse compute-unit or GPU-core class, and logs the choice with its reason.

Views: there is one RT scene and one TLAS rebuild per scenario per frame, anchored on the main (active) viewport camera and shared by all views. The SRC and WRC are per scenario; gather and denoise are per view. ReflectionProbe and preview renders disable NextGI and sample only the WRC and sky. §2.1 budgets apply to the main view; each secondary view carries its own gather-and-denoise cost (lines 4–8). G3’s two-viewport test checks that the second view adds no AS, SRC or WRC passes (timestamp-verified) and costs ≤ the sum of lines 4–8.

Configurations without HWRT run SDFGI instead (D9, §8.9).

8.2 Ray-traced scene

  • Registry: RendererSceneCull feeds every GI-contributing opaque instance in the scenario within rt_scene_radius (default 200 m) of the main view camera, not the camera cull list. Instances beyond 30 m with angular radius under 0.5° are culled; cap 100k.
  • Exclusion reasons are per-(instance, surface) values in the shared Fallback enum (§6.3), separate from the §3 tier reasons: NOT_IN_RT_SCENE (outside rt_scene_radius or solid-angle culled), RT_INSTANCE_CAP and RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED.
  • RT coverage (Q18): the fraction of GI-contributing opaque instances within rt_scene_radius that are present in the TLAS, gated at ≥95% on NextGI Perf (G1, G3).
  • The TLAS is rebuilt on the CPU every frame (NVIDIA/Intel guidance). From P1b, static MultiMesh, including non-baked GridMap octants, is CPU-fed from the existing CPU cache (MeshStorage::_multimesh_make_local, a one-time GPU readback for a MultiMesh that has no CPU copy yet) within the 100k cap. GPU-written instances cover only particles and dynamic MultiMesh, in P3 (cut item 1, §14).
  • RT geometry arena per D13:
    • 256 MiB chunks with free-list allocation; chunks never move. Defragmentation rebuilds the affected BLASes and rewrites their records within the §2.1 line 1b build budget.
    • The instance record carries chunk index + offset, read through buffer device address on Vulkan and gpuAddress on Metal.
    • eps_rt = min(max_extent/256, rt_max_error), where rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/rt_max_error defaults to 2 cm at import scale.
    • NextGeo: one fixed import-time proxy per mesh, a DAG cut at error ≤ eps_rt, never view-dependent, at most 128k triangles per BLAS. When that cap binds, the proxy splits into several BLASes per mesh rather than coarsening. Built in P4; joins the TLAS in P5.
    • Legacy static meshes: the coarsest ArrayMesh LOD whose stored edge_length ≤ eps_rt, decoded on the CPU in MeshStorage::mesh_add_surface to float32 positions / uint32 indices in the arena, created only when nextgi/enabled and SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY. mesh_surface_update_vertex_region and the other surface-mutation paths update the CPU-decoded copy and mark the BLAS dirty. Surfaces updated every frame are treated as dynamic: refit, or excluded with RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED.
    • Skinned meshes: a per-instance arena slot written by the skinning compute pass (§8.7).
  • Static BLASes use PREFER_FAST_TRACE and, from P1b, are compacted asynchronously one frame after build; skinned BLASes use PREFER_FAST_BUILD with ALLOW_UPDATE.
  • AS budget (§2.1): the TLAS rebuild and skinned refit are line 1a. New BLAS builds and compaction copies are amortized in line 1b under a per-frame triangle cap set from the G0 microbenchmarks (provisional ≤250k triangles per frame), newly visible first. An instance enters the TLAS only once its BLAS exists.
  • All instances are opaque. Alpha-tested and transparent surfaces are excluded (D17).
  • Self-intersection: offset ray origins along the geometric normal by 4 × eps_rt × instance scale, clamped to ≤5 cm; the screen trace covers the first centimetres and hides proxy mismatch. G1a and G5-6 add a single mesh at least 40 m in extent containing 10 cm interior walls.
  • Kernel rules: workgroup 8×8, shared memory ≤8 KiB, no position fetch (D5).

8.3 Two caches, per-instance radiance pages, and swept invalidation

v1 uses two distinct caches budgeted separately, both per scenario. P1a validates the SRC alone at G1a; P1b adds the WRC, per-instance LRU and dynamic invalidation (§12).

  • Surface Radiance Cache (SRC):
    • Cards: Stage 1: up to 12 normal-clustered cards per mesh from the start (not 6 plus recovery). Skinned meshes get 6 cards around bind-pose bounds. Stage 2 (P5) uses per-cluster-group cards from VirtualGeometryData. Card-hit coverage (Q2) is gated at ≥85%.
    • Hit lookup (D6): the committed instance custom index (24 bits) selects the NextGI instance record; the geometry index selects the surface; primitive ID and barycentrics give the object-space hit point and, from RT-arena positions, the face normal. The card whose axis best matches that normal and whose depth matches within 2 card texels supplies radiance; otherwise the WRC does. Hit lighting is post-v1, Epic-only (P6d).
    • Atlases:
      • The material atlas (albedo, normal, roughness, emission) is shared per (mesh, material override set); radiance pages are per instance.
      • Budgets (High): radiance 4096² R11G11B10F (64 MiB) and material 128 MiB; Epic doubles both.
      • Solid-angle priority; LRU eviction to the WRC.
    • Capture (§2.1 line 2a: 0.06 ms Ada / 0.15 ms mid-range):
      • P1a captures with _render_material per card.
      • From P1b, capture is one batched render pass per frame into the material atlas. A capture variant of the scene shader reads each card’s view-projection from a storage buffer indexed by push constant, with the per-card transform folded into instance data; up to 64 cards share the pass, each with its own viewport and scissor (draw_list_set_viewport, draw_list_enable_scissor). The fallback is one _setup_environment UBO per card inside the pass, with used_uniform_buffer_count budgeted. P1b gate: a 64-card batch costs ≤0.25 ms CPU.
      • Per frame: ≤64 cards or ≤1M texels, chosen by solid angle.
      • Capture runs only in the NextGI block after _render_shadow_end, because it shares RENDER_LIST_SECONDARY with the shadow batch.
    • Relighting (D14; §2.1 line 2b: 0.14 ms Ada / 0.35 ms mid-range):
      • Light set: scenario lights culled against resident page bounds, modelled on SDFGI’s world-space set (RendererSceneCull::_render_scene, GI::SDFGI::pre_process_gi). DYNAMIC lights relight every scheduled pass; STATIC lights dirty pages only when their properties change and are skipped for lightmapped receivers; DISABLED lights are excluded. A scenario static-light list sits next to scenario->dynamic_lights.
      • Shadows are evaluated at 1/4 card resolution (a 2×2-texel shadow mask) for ≤2 shadowed lights per texel per frame, rotated temporally; other lights reuse the last mask.
      • Where a card texel projects into a valid camera directional split or a positional shadow-atlas tile, the shadow-map lookup is reused. Only the remainder is ray traced, capped at 256k shadow rays per frame at High (128k Medium, 512k Epic).
      • Camera light buffers and the screen cluster are never used.
  • Three SRC work streams, each budgeted:
    1. Material capture: on first residency, mesh, material or scale change. Materials using TIME are flagged by shader analysis and excluded from static caching.
    2. Direct relight: dirty pages from light changes (old and new influence AABB; a directional change dirties all) and from occluder swept volumes projected along directional lights and intersected with local-light radii, nearest and largest first. This is the primary convergence path.
    3. Indirect refresh: a ring of up to 1/16 of resident pages per frame plus pages near dirty regions, as a backstop.
    Streams (2) and (3) share the relight cap: ≤256k texels per frame at High, ≤128k at Medium, ≤512k at Epic, stream (2) first. At the High cap, all resident pages are relit within 8 frames for up to 2M resident texels (32 frames for 8M). Convergence is gated at G1-2 by Q4a (step scenes: e(10) ≤25%, e(32) ≤10%) and Q4b (continuous scenes: mean e_lag ≤25% over the motion, e(32) ≤10% 32 frames after it ends).
  • Swept geometry and shadow invalidation (new work): (1) Geometry dirty regions: RendererSceneCull::_update_instance emits the old and new world AABB for transform, visibility, mesh, material and emissive changes before it overwrites prev_transformed_aabb; free, scenario change and the early-return paths get explicit hooks. Because the TLAS is rebuilt every frame, traces see moved geometry at once; the regions dirty the instance’s own radiance pages and the WRC probes they overlap, and seed stream (3). (2) Static-receiver shadow invalidation: when a shadow-casting occluder moves, its swept volume (prev_transformed_aabb ∪ transformed_aabb) marks newly lit or shadowed static-receiver pages for stream (2), and the WRC probes inside it for update.
  • World Radiance Cache (WRC) — a radiance cache, not an irradiance grid (D15):
    • Layout: 4 clipmap levels of 16³ probes centred on the main view camera, at 1.0, 2.0, 4.0 and 8.0 m spacing.
    • Each probe holds 8×8 octahedral R11G11B10F radiance with a 1-texel border, prefiltered 4×4 and 2×2 mips for cone lookups, and an 8×8 RG16F depth-moment visibility map.
    • Probes are relocated off geometry by up to 0.4× spacing.
    • Diffuse gather rays hand off to the WRC at 1.5× the local probe spacing. Specular rays trace up to rt_scene_radius and use the WRC only on a miss.
    • SH irradiance is derived for fog and the translucency volume. The cache takes about 14 MiB, plus history if double-buffered.
  • Bounce and energy model (v1): one explicit traced diffuse bounce shaded by the SRC; further bounces enter through temporally accumulated SRC/WRC feedback with demodulated albedo and feedback gain 1.0 with albedo clamped to ≤0.95. The firefly clamp’s removed energy is counted (Q16 ≤2%). The result is validated against the path-traced reference (D16, §8.8) at §2’s ≤20% indirect diffuse patch error, so an energy-biased solver cannot pass by converging to its own wrong static value. Specular uses one traced bounce terminating in SRC/WRC/sky.
  • Clean-room boundary: card/clipmap concepts come from public documentation and talks listed in §20; no card constants, layouts, or shader code are copied from any proprietary engine.

8.4 Ray-tracing substrate

Vulkan (v1, P0–P3)

  • AS address query (vkGetAccelerationStructureDeviceAddressKHR) and cache at creation;
  • ray-query-only TLAS: tlas_build accepts hit_sbt_range 0;
  • AS limits exposed;
  • SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE from a PCI vendor/device-ID allowlist of RT-core architectures (NVIDIA Turing+ RTX, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Xe-HPG/Xe2 Arc); SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY also requires bufferDeviceAddress and comes with a reason code (§3);
  • storage-buffer BLAS inputs, with usage bits validated by blas_create;
  • build modes BUILD/UPDATE (P1b, before the Metal lane needs them);
  • shared transient scratch ring;
  • compaction query pool and async copy (P1b);
  • AS memory accounting in buffer_memory and the memory reports; the tier falls back with AS_MEMORY when the TLAS/BLAS budget cannot be met;
  • tlas_build_from_buffer with an opaque instance reference (P3);
  • conditional SPIR-V 1.4 opt-in, only if spirv-val or a driver rejects SPIR-V 1.3.

Metal (v1, P2)

  • Capability detection: supportsRaytracing(); hardware RT = supportsFamily(Apple9). The family scan extends to Apple10/Apple11 in the same change as MetalDeviceProfile::GPU and both switch statements. SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY only on macOS ≥14 with argument buffers on and Barriers sync; otherwise the reason is METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS or METAL_SYNC_MODE.
  • AS objects: MTLPrimitiveAccelerationStructureDescriptor BLASes; an MTLIndirectInstanceAccelerationStructureDescriptor TLAS with 72-byte column-major records referencing each BLAS by gpuResourceID; API_TRAIT_ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE_INSTANCE_SIZE = 72; a driver-owned dummy BLAS with mask 0 for empty instances.
  • Residency: AS objects come from placement heaps (Heap::newAccelerationStructure), so the per-encoder useHeaps keeps indirectly referenced BLASes resident.
  • Encoding: an AS command-encoder state joins the level fences; all BLAS builds finish before the TLAS build.
  • Refit on the P1b build-mode API (needed by P3) and compaction (writeCompactedSize → copyAndCompact → rewrite TLAS records).
  • Shaders: a shader-container case for UNIFORM_TYPE_ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE and RayQueryKHR detection; argument-buffer gpuResourceID write; SPIRV-Cross cherry-pick df0828ca plus an AccelerationStructure case in the argument-buffer padding switch.
  • Reference path tracer on Metal (§8.8).

D3D12 (P6a, per D12)

  • Ray query in compute only (DXR Tier 1.1, SM 6.5), built on godotengine/godot PR #118654 plus the Mesa spirv2dxil ray-tracing patch through a godot-nir-static fork. It is pulled into v1 only if upstream merges both during the v1 window.
  • v1 never switches API silently. New GodotNext projects default to Vulkan on Windows (P0, EditorNode::get_initial_settings) and give up D3D12’s HDR output and DComp transparency unless switched back. A d3d12 project logs D3D12_NO_DXR once and runs SDFGI until P6a; turning on nextgi/enabled there shows a one-time modal offering the Vulkan switch, which keeps fallback_to_d3d12 = true, states the HDR/DComp loss and offers a restart.

8.5 Final gather, reflections, and fog

  • Screen probes are placed on visible surfaces using Stage A / legacy prepass depth/normal/roughness guides, importance-sampled with previous-frame lighting, filtered spatially/temporally, then bilaterally upsampled.
  • When NextGI is active, disable SSIL automatically to prevent double-counting, while extending RenderForwardClustered’s previous-frame color allocation to using_ssil || using_ssr || using_nextgi as a post-transparent copy at full resolution with a full mip chain (SSEffects::allocate_last_frame_buffer parameter), so §8.1’s screen traces retain lit color history even when SSR is off. Keep SSAO as contact detail only after energy tests confirm zero double-darkening.
  • Reflections:
    • Screen trace first, then HWRT for roughness ≤0.4 (≤0.25 at quarter resolution on Medium), shaded from the SRC; WRC or sky on a miss.
    • The specular trace writes the hit distance in world units to an R16F texture at trace resolution (miss/sky 65504), kept per view. It is a default-on cheap output for the post-v1 RR guides and the reflection debug view; at Epic it is full resolution.
    • Rougher surfaces use the WRC with a cone lookup.
    • ReflectionProbe weight is (1 − NextGI confidence).
    • SSR stays as the existing composite over NextGI reflections.
  • NextGI writes its low-resolution ambient volume (ambient_texture/gi_inject, from WRC SH irradiance) in the NextGI block (§6.1 Step 5). _update_volumetric_fog reads it in Step 6, before the Stage B resolve (Step 7) and legacy PASS_MODE_COLOR (Step 8), so 3D fog_map scattering and transmittance match across virtual and legacy opaque pixels.
  • Emissive contract: emissives covering at least 4 card texels contribute through the SRC; smaller ones must be lights. Emissive radiance passes the shared firefly clamp, whose removed energy is counted (Q16).

8.6 Denoiser

Allocate persistent current/previous albedo, normal/roughness, linear depth, velocity, demodulated diffuse/specular, first/second moments, variance, and history length. NextGI forces the depth + normal-roughness (+VoxelGI-id) prepass. From P1b that prepass variant also writes MOTION_VECTORS for moving and skinned instances; while NextGI is active, the opaque color pass and RENDER_LIST_MOTION do not re-write velocity for them, so gather accumulation and denoise consume this frame’s velocity. P1a is static-scene only and reprojects with camera-only motion from depth. Pre-clear and load/clear rules per upscaler (TAA, FSR2, MetalFX, DLSS) follow §6.1’s table.

Denoising is split by signal (D8), with exactly one temporal accumulator per signal:

  • Diffuse is filtered in screen-probe space: spatial filtering across neighbouring probes weighted by depth, normal and hit distance, plus per-probe temporal accumulation. One light per-pixel temporal stabilizer follows.
  • Specular uses per-pixel SVGF/ReLAX-class variance-guided filtering with hit-distance-aware reprojection.
  • Disocclusion rejection uses depth, normal and velocity; virtual pixels add Stage A’s stable instance-ID guide.
  • Firefly clamp before temporal accumulation, with a clamped-energy counter; blue-noise/low-discrepancy sample rotation beyond current TAA jitter.
  • Explicit transparent, particle and first-frame policies. Skinned and moving instances keep history using their prepass motion vectors. Transparents read the translucency volume, and first frames use the WRC.
  • Debug views for raw signal, variance, history length, rejected history, and denoised output are required deliverables, not developer-only hacks.

DLSS-RR mode (D21; post-v1, P6i; design fixed in v2.4). Per view:

  • Kept: line 7 — gather integration, the probe spatial filter and the bilateral upsample. The per-probe history is capped at 16, 4 or 0, chosen by the P6i spike.
  • Not dispatched: line 8 — neither the diffuse stabilizer nor the specular SVGF/ReLAX passes run.
  • Specular: 1 rpp at full resolution, passed raw after the firefly clamp.
  • Sampling: white-noise xxhash32 replaces the 64×64 blue-noise tile if the spike shows the tile hurts RR (RR guide §3.5).
  • RR evaluation: RR runs in the upscaler slot instead of DLSS SR, with at least 32 jitter phases, a fixed scale, no exposure input, and depth of field after RR.
  • Guides: diffuse albedo (RGBA8 linear, albedo × (1 − metallic), sky 0.5); specular albedo (RGBA8, the env-BRDF factor the forward shader applies to RB_TEX_REFLECTION, sky 0); normal + roughness (RGBA16F: world-space unit normal, roughness with the dynamic flag stripped, sky 0.0; the meaning of “linear roughness” is unverified); hit distance (R16F, 65504; §8.5); row-major matrices. Transparents are unguided at first.
  • Activation and fallback: Epic only, and quality = auto never enables RR; RR falls back to DLSS SR + D8 with an RR_* reason.

P3 provides only the default-off switches: probe_temporal_max_history, stabilizer_enabled, sampling_mode and specular_filter_enabled.

Budget: the High denoise line is 0.32 ms Ada / 0.80 ms mid-range for the main view (§2.1); each secondary view pays its own. Under the post-v1 DLSS-RR mode, line 8 is 0 and the RR pass is charged to line U2 (P6i).

8.7 Dynamic actors and translucency

  • All opaque instances, skinned ones included, are full NextGI receivers, with denoiser history driven by their motion vectors. MeshStorage already double-buffers skinned vertices for motion vectors.
  • In the HWRT tier, skinned instances also occlude and bounce light through a per-frame BLAS refit from their RT-arena slot, in §2.1 line 1a:
    • budget: ≤200k skinned triangles per frame on Ada, ≤100k on mid-range;
    • full rebuild every 32 frames;
    • characters beyond 30 m or over budget drop out of the TLAS;
    • 6 cards around the bind-pose bounds, relit every frame.
  • Alpha-blended surfaces are excluded from the TLAS. They get NextGI from a camera froxel translucency volume (1/16 screen resolution × 32 slices, about 261k froxels at 1080p, about 6 MiB at FP16 SH-L1, filled from the WRC within §2.1 line 3), sampled by a forward alpha branch. Its fill cost is measured at G3-5. Their reflections come from WRC radiance or a ReflectionProbe.
  • Alpha-tested surfaces are excluded from the TLAS by default; a per-material “treat as opaque for RT” override exists.
  • Emissives smaller than 4 card texels must be authored as lights.

Gates: Character Walk (G3-4) and Glass & Water (G3-5). Translucency has no reference gate in v1. Cut items 3 and 4 (§14) drop the translucency volume (transparents then use WRC irradiance at the object centre plus a ReflectionProbe) and the skinned refit (skinned meshes become receivers only).

8.8 Reference path tracer

  • The authoritative reference is an in-engine progressive path tracer using compute ray query, built in three steps:
    • P0, reference v0: an analytic path tracer over a TLAS that the GDScript RD testbed builds from known triangle soups (the furnace box, sealed box A with a 10 cm wall and sealed box B with a 20 cm wall). Each test passes in its material table. It does not depend on P1 infrastructure.
    • P1a: the scene path renders Godot scenes (Color Box Room, Concave Courtyard) through the RT arena and CPU TLAS. G1a depends on it.
    • P2: the Metal port.
  • It matches Godot’s StandardMaterial3D BRDF for constant-parameter materials (albedo, roughness, metallic, emission), Godot light units, exposure and sky.
  • It is near-unbiased (64-bounce cap, truncation bias < 1e-5): Russian roulette after 4 bounces, no clamp, no denoiser. It compares linear HDR before tonemapping.
  • NextGI quality scenes use constant-parameter materials. LightmapGI (16 bounces, ULTRA, denoiser off, texture_for_bounces off) is a report-only cross-check: a mismatch above 10% opens an investigation, which may conclude that LightmapGI is the wrong side. Its defaults (3 bounces, denoiser on) are truncated and biased; in a closed albedo-0.8 box, bounces beyond the third carry about 41% of the radiance.
  • Diagnostic mode: shade NextGI hits with path-traced hit radiance to separate SRC error from gather error.
  • G0-3 closes on the analytic tests only. The furnace is a closed box with Lambertian inner faces (ρ = 0.8), uniform emission Le on every inner face and no other lights; every pixel must read Le/(1−ρ) within 1%. Sealed boxes A and B and bit-identical repeated runs complete the gate. The reference is never cut.

8.9 Software fallback

  • v1: SDFGI driven by the Environment’s sdfgi_* parameters, selected by the §3 tier rule with a tier reason (§6.3). It never raises an error and costs ≤2% GPU time over plain SDFGI.
  • P6b SDF+SRC+WRC tier seeds, to be specified when P6b starts:
    • a world-space brick grid listing the instance cards overlapping each brick, with hit-to-card selection by projected-depth tolerance tied to cell size and an opposing-normal tie-break;
    • sparse near-field bricks of 5 cm or finer storing true triangle distances, since current 128³ R8 cascades are not assumed sufficient for near-field tracing;
    • reuse of SDFGI cascade infrastructure where contracts match (scrolling, JFA distance generation, probe history buffers, half-resolution output plumbing);
    • dynamic dirty-region dispatch in RendererSceneCull::_render_scene beyond camera-scroll sdfgi_get_pending_region_count and FLAG_USES_BAKED_LIGHT, culling static and dynamic GI-contributing opaque instances under a per-frame brick budget;
    • skinned instances excluded from voxelization;
    • thin and one-sided geometry as a first-class failure mode: thickness/bias rules, probe relocation/occlusion and sealed-room leak checks belong in the solver and debug UI.
  • Budgets per §2.1.

9. Shared foundations

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

RenderingDevice and graph

  • Indirect-count plumbing and the VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan12Features consolidation are built only if draw path (a) wins G4-5. The default path (c) needs neither (D2).
  • Capability flags:
    • SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY (tightened). Each driver returns it with a tier reason code: METAL_SYNC_MODE or METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS on Metal, D3D12_NO_DXR on D3D12, NO_RAY_QUERY otherwise.
    • The new SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE is driver knowledge only. Metal: Apple9+. Vulkan: a PCI vendor/device-ID allowlist of RT-core architectures (NVIDIA Turing+ RTX, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Xe-HPG/Xe2 Arc). The CPU device-type check lives in the §3 tier rule alone.
    • AS limits and RT pipeline; indirect-count and draw parameters only with (a); mesh shaders in P6h.
    • Image atomics: 32-bit only in v1. The 64-bit plumbing (Vulkan buffer int64 atomics through Vulkan12Features, Metal supports_image_atomic_64_bit read by has_feature, D3D12 unsupported) comes only if P6h funds software raster.
    Renderer code must query and fall back, never hard-fail on software or headless drivers.
  • RT API changes, in order, all with graph usages and dummy-driver behavior:
    1. tlas_build accepts hit_sbt_range 0 for ray-query-only use.
    2. AS address query and cache; AS limits; R16G16B16A16_UNORM AS-format query, recorded for a later SNORM16 optimization.
    3. SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY requires bufferDeviceAddress and carries the driver reason code; new SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE.
    4. AS-invalidated validation in compute_list_bind_uniform_set; raytracing_pipeline_create fails cleanly.
    5. blas_create accepts storage buffers and validates usage bits.
    6. A shared transient scratch ring aligned to minAccelerationStructureScratchOffsetAlignment; AS bytes in buffer_memory.
    7. Asynchronous blas_compact.
    8. Build modes BUILD/UPDATE.
    9. tlas_build_from_buffer plus acceleration_structure_get_instance_reference (opaque; Metal uses ResourceID).
    10. The per-shader SPIR-V 1.4 opt-in (RD parameter plus ShaderRD cache-key flag), only if needed.
    Items 1–6 land on Vulkan in P0 (merged by G0), 7–8 in P1b, 9 in P3, and 10 only if G0 finds SPIR-V 1.3 rejected. The Metal driver lane (P2) starts right after G0 on top of items 1 and 3, and needs item 8 before Metal refit. D3D12 implements them in P6a.
  • HWRT safety net at tier selection:
    • Startup self-test: one triangle plus a 64-ray fixture, compared against analytic IDs and t. A mismatch falls back with SELFTEST_FAILED.
    • Crash latch: a device loss while HWRT is active persists an “HWRT faulted” flag under user://, keyed by driver version, before the driver aborts (Vulkan already requests VK_EXT_device_fault). Later launches fall back with HWRT_FAULTED until the driver version changes or hardware_ray_tracing = force.
    • A TLAS/BLAS memory budget that cannot be met falls back with AS_MEMORY.
  • Every new command needs graph usages/barriers and dummy-driver behavior; bypassing the graph loses the safety model. This includes driver callbacks, which declare usages and pipeline stages. All NGX calls run on the render thread under a mutex.

Persistent GPU scene

  • Convert per-frame CPU instance submission into incrementally updated GPU instance/surface records for virtual content, while retaining current lists for legacy content and excluded sibling surfaces during migration.
  • Track transformed_aabb plus stable IDs across frames for NextGI invalidation, per-instance SRC page tables, temporal history, streaming priority, TLAS instance indices and debug picking. Dirty regions (old and new AABB) are emitted inside RendererSceneCull::_update_instance, before prev_transformed_aabb is overwritten; free, scenario change and the early-return paths get explicit hooks. No separate snapshot state is kept.
  • GPU instance cull pass: frustum plus previous-frame Hi-Z over the persistent records, feeding the level-synchronous BVH and cluster cull (Slice 2).
  • NextGI RT instance registry (scenario-wide, rt_scene_radius around the main viewport camera): every GI-contributing opaque instance within the radius, not the camera cull list. Instances beyond 30 m under 0.5° angular radius are culled (NOT_IN_RT_SCENE); the cap is 100k (RT_INSTANCE_CAP). The v1 TLAS is CPU-fed from it and rebuilt once per scenario per frame, shared by all views. From P1b, static MultiMesh, including non-baked GridMap octants, is fed from the existing CPU cache (_multimesh_make_local) within the same cap. GPU-written instances cover only particles and dynamic MultiMesh (P3, §14 cut list, item 1).
  • RT geometry arena (D13): 256 MiB chunks with free-list allocation; chunks never move. The instance record carries chunk index + offset, read through buffer device address on Vulkan and gpuAddress on Metal. Defragmentation rebuilds the affected BLASes and rewrites their records within the §2.1 line 1b build budget. mesh_surface_update_vertex_region and the other surface-mutation paths update the CPU-decoded copy and mark the BLAS dirty; surfaces updated every frame are treated as dynamic and refit, or excluded with RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED.
  • Free-list/compaction policy must handle scene unload, mesh replacement, skeleton changes, and editor reimport without GPU dangling IDs or un-dirtied vacated AABBs.

Material binding

  • The material-bucket resolve (D7) reuses per-material uniform sets. Bindless is not part of v1.
  • If bindless is revisited, it needs a per-element descriptor write API and a texture_replace_rid hook that rewrites one table slot (DRV-6). Today every texture swap re-creates each dependent uniform set.
  • Resolve variants write texture-streaming feedback per material bucket. Eligibility analysis (§7.4 parity table) is shared by the resolve, SRC capture, shadows and editor warnings.

Editor and observability

  • Viewport debug modes (the full set lands in P1b): active NextGI tier, quality preset and tier reason; TLAS instance heat; BLAS triangle cost; hit-instance ID; RT proxy vs raster overlay; SRC cards and relight age; WRC probe radiance and visibility; card-hit coverage (Q2); screen probes; variance/history; cluster cut/error; page residency; visibility IDs; per-(instance, surface) fallback reasons, including RT exclusions. Tier reasons (§3), per-instance reasons and upscaler reasons (ViewportScaling3DReason, §3.2) are separate enums.
  • Debug overlay: an upscaler status line showing requested mode, active backend, preset and reason.
  • Import panel shows source triangles, clusters, DAG depth, pages, .scn/.res vs .ngm disk split (retain_authored vs strip_authored_to_coarse), estimated VRAM, build time, excluded surfaces, and proxy triangle count.
  • Runtime profiler scopes and counters are part of each phase exit gate: drawn clusters, wanted/resident pages, TLAS instances, RT coverage (Q18), BLAS bytes (compacted/uncompacted), RT-arena bytes, skinned refit triangles, rays per pass, SRC capture and relight texels, dirty AABB/SRC updates, denoise time, clamped energy fraction (Q16), fallback opaque pixel percentage (Q14), upscaler pass time (RD timestamp named by the upscaler), NGX memory per view, and the Q21 invalid motion-vector count (capture mode).
  • New GodotNext projects default to driver.windows=vulkan (§10). In a project still on d3d12, turning on nextgi/enabled shows a one-time modal, and an EditorInspectorPlugin on Environment/WorldEnvironment shows the tier status with a ‘Use Vulkan on Windows for hardware RT’ button. Both set driver.windows=vulkan, keep fallback_to_d3d12=true, state that HDR output and DComp transparency are lost, and offer a restart. The API never switches silently; until the switch, D3D12_NO_DXR is logged once.
  • DLSS-enabled editors add one plain-text NVIDIA attribution line to the About dialog (EditorAbout), with no logos. Distributing such an editor requires NVIDIA’s §7.2(c) approval (§19).
  • The godotnext/testbed project (§11), owned by this program, is the visual language for every decision. No screenshot from an ad-hoc scene counts as a gate.

Temporal upscalers and vendor interop (D20)

Core MIT code with no SDK dependency. Items 1–5 are PU-core in lane rhi-vulkan, after G0 and behind that lane’s P1 items (D18): pu-rd-callback-stages (1), pu-rd-native-interop (2–3) and pu-vk-extension-providers (4–5). The upscaler reasons, inputs and contracts are in §3.2, §6.1 and §6.3.

  1. Driver-callback stages. RD::driver_callback_add(callback, userdata, resources, BitField<RDD::PipelineStageBits> p_stages = PIPELINE_STAGE_ALL_COMMANDS_BIT). RenderingDeviceGraph::add_driver_callback stores the stages as self_stages. MetalFX keeps the default and metal_fx.cpp is unchanged. A DEV_ENABLED-only RenderingDevice method, bound only in dev builds, records a compute write → driver callback → fragment read fixture (GU-2). Its callback calls driver->command_clear_color_texture on the written texture, and a dev flag forces the stages to 0.
  2. Native command buffer. RDD::command_buffer_get_native_handle(CommandBufferID) returns the VkCommandBuffer on Vulkan. The base class returns 0 on the other drivers.
  3. Native texture info. RDD::texture_get_native_info(TextureID), Vulkan only, returns image, view, format, aspect, base and count of mips and layers, width and height. That is enough to build NGX image-view resources for array slices and depth views.
  4. Vulkan extension providers (new drivers/vulkan/vulkan_extension_provider.{h,cpp}):
    • Providers register at MODULE_INITIALIZATION_LEVEL_SERVERS, before DisplayServer::create.
    • Hooks: get_instance_extensions(); get_device_extensions(VkInstance, VkPhysicalDevice); chain_device_features(VkPhysicalDevice, void *&pNext); on_device_created(VkInstance, VkPhysicalDevice, VkDevice, queue family and index, enabled set); filter_validation_message(callback data).
    • All provider extensions are optional. A provider is told which were missing, and device creation never fails because of a provider. Lists are merged before VulkanHooks runs, so OpenXR forwards them and keeps choosing the physical device.
    • P6j reuses this for Reflex.
  5. Created-handle registry (debug-build-only: DEBUG_ENABLED, never in template_release): a hash set of every Vulkan handle RenderingDeviceDriverVulkan creates, populated only under --gpu-validation. It implements the object-attributed NGX rule (§14 Never cut).
  6. D3D12. The D3D12 native handle and command_buffer_invalidate_cached_state belong only to the stretch item pu-dlss-d3d12.
  7. Script binding. The RD, RDD and driver hooks are not script-bound in v1. Viewport.reset_temporal_history, RenderingServer.viewport_reset_temporal_history, viewport_get_scaling_3d_status and scaling_3d_mode_get_availability are bound.

10. Platform strategy

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

Validated: Ada on Windows (RTX 4080 SUPER) and Apple10 (M5 Max). Enabled but unvalidated: Turing, Ampere (until the RTX 3060 12 GB bought at G0 arrives), Blackwell, all Linux drivers, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Arc, and Apple M3/M4.

Develop on the M5 Max. The Metal driver lane starts right after G0, so the M5 Max runs ray query locally from about month 3–4 (G2-1 parity). Until then, HWRT work is validated on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine (Ada). NextGI on Metal follows G1; after M2 the daily edit, compile, capture and HWRT loop runs locally, and the RTX 4080 SUPER machine remains the Vulkan/NVIDIA timing gate. spirv-val comes from the Vulkan SDK as a dev dependency on both hosts; it is not vendored.

DLSS by platform

Linux Vulkan, NVIDIA RTXPUUnvalidated Enabled but unvalidated. Built and fallback-tested under WSL2 on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine (build-only if WSL2 is unavailable), never on the Linux VM (Supplement §3). Validated only through optional Linux boot of the RTX 4080 SUPER machine (GU-2, GU-5).Same code path.
Windows D3D12Stretch after GUStretch DLSS SR/DLAA if pu-dlss-d3d12 lands, else FSR2 with API_UNSUPPORTED. Only the barrier path the RTX 4080 SUPER machine reports; single view.D3D12 native handle, cached-state invalidation, callback transitions (GU-7).
macOS / iOS / visionOS MetalNoneFallback MetalFX temporal or FSR2, API_UNSUPPORTED.§3.2 resolution.
Builds without modules/dlss: official binaries (§19), macOS/MoltenVK, Windows/Linux arm64NoneFallback FSR2, MODULE_DISABLED.§3.2 resolution.
Non-NVIDIA Vulkan, GTX, llvmpipe/lavapipeNoneFallback FSR2, NOT_NVIDIA_RTX.§3.2 resolution.
Mobile, Compatibility, WebNoneFallback Bilinear rule, RENDERER_UNSUPPORTED.§3.2 resolution.
XR / multiviewNone in v1Fallback VIEW_UNSUPPORTED → FSR2. Split-screen viewports are supported.Per-view reprojection plus a stereo fixture: post-v1 backlog (§17).
DLSS-RRP6i, post-v1—D21.
FG/MFG/ReflexP6j, post-v1—D22.

Differences from UE 5.8

  • Windows: UE’s HWRT is DX12-first. Its 5.8 HWRT page calls Vulkan RT experimental, while the 5.5 notes call it default with DX12 parity; the sources conflict. GodotNext is Vulkan-first, new projects default to Vulkan, and D3D12 HWRT follows in P6a.
  • macOS: UE’s Mac HWRT is M2+ experimental. GodotNext’s auto selects HWRT on M3+, validated only on the M5 Max.
  • Non-RT GPUs: UE falls back to Lumen SWRT. GodotNext v1 falls back to SDFGI; the SWRT-class tier is P6b.
  • DLSS: UE uses NVIDIA’s own plugin (Streamline + NGX, maintained by NVIDIA). GodotNext uses an in-tree MIT module over NGX, built against a developer-supplied SDK. UE plugin source is a forbidden input (§19).

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

Build integration follows Godot’s existing rules: a module directory needs register_types.h, SCsub, and config.py; runtime code cannot be gated as editor-only. Any ClassDB/project-setting addition requires documentation regeneration and compatibility handling for renamed import options. modules/dlss is disabled by default. It builds against NGX only with dlss_backend=ngx and dlss_sdk_path, and official CI builds only the mock backend.

12. Phased work plan

Two slices, one at a time (D18). Slice 1 (P0–P3) delivers NextGI on legacy meshes. Slice 2 (P4–P5) starts runtime work after M3, and P5’s G5 is the v1 integration gate that owns Dense City + GI. Every gate is numeric and names its harness mode.

Ceilings are provisional until their first measurement: G1a/G1 for diffuse lines, G3 for reflection and denoise lines, G0 for AS lines. That measurement may reset each line once, logged in Appendix A with the data; afterwards measurement may only tighten. The same rule applies to the upscaler lines (§2.1): U1 and U1M equal NVIDIA’s table values, so the ≤1.25× pass rule applies once. The provisional lines U1A and U1Q are first measured at GU, and U2 and U3 at P6i. The total-frame caps (6.6 ms Ada / 16.6 ms mid-range p95, Dense City + NextGI High) are fixed. Cuts: schedule slips consume the §14 cut list in order. Gate failures go through that phase’s outcome rows, and a cut applies only if it removes the failing workload.

Calendar figures are planning assumptions for one lead plus AI agents (six agent lanes, §14), re-estimated at every gate. Gate tags: [T] timing mode, [C] capture mode, [V] validation run, [CPU] CPU tool or doctest. Metrics Q1–Q21 are defined in §13.4 and budgets in §2.1. Ada (RTX 4080 SUPER) lines are iteration proxies; mid-range lines run on an RTX 3060 12 GB bought at G0, and the per-pass Ada:3060 ratios measured at G1 replace the 2.5× scaling factor. Validated: Ada on Windows (RTX 4080 SUPER) and Apple10 (M5 Max). Enabled but unvalidated, with no gate: Turing, Ampere (until the RTX 3060 arrives), Blackwell, all Linux drivers, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Arc and Apple M3/M4.
v2.2 → v2.3 phase mapping: P0a → P0 + P1a; P1 → P1b; P4 → P3; P5 (HWRT tier) → folded into P1–P3; Metal RT from P6 → P2; P0b + P2 → P4; P3 → P5; P6 → P6a–h. v2.4: PU is new (DLSS SR/DLAA; PU-core after G0 in rhi-vulkan, the rest after G1 in upscalers; never blocks G1–G5). DLSS-RR moves from P6f to P6i, and FG/MFG/Reflex become P6j.

P0 — Bring-up, harness, RT substrate, analytic reference (M0)

Depends on: nothing. Estimate: 8–12 weeks; M0 at about month 2–3.

  1. History graft. A local git replace --graft does not travel to clones, and 95c0fa5 is not in the local object store. Fetch upstream, create the replace ref (7cfeb47 → 95c0fa5), push refs/replace/*, and document git fetch origin 'refs/replace/*:refs/replace/*'. Re-creating the fork on upstream history instead needs a force-push, so it is the lead’s explicit action, not agent work. Merge cadence: each upstream 4.x stable tag. NextGI/NextGeo code lives in new files behind named hooks; lines changed in upstream files are tracked at each gate.
  2. Builds and environment. Editor builds on the M5 Max (macOS arm64), the RTX 4080 SUPER machine (Windows, vulkan and d3d12) and the Linux VM (linuxbsd); fork CI trim. vulkaninfo JSON for the RTX 4080 SUPER (device 1), device 0 (UHD 770) and the Linux VM, recording the AS and ray-query extensions, VkPhysicalDeviceAccelerationStructurePropertiesKHR limits, and the AS vertex-buffer format bit for R16G16B16A16_UNORM; for PU, both the RTX 4080 SUPER machine devices also record the Vulkan API version, the NVIDIA driver version and whether VK_NVX_binary_import, VK_NVX_image_view_handle and VK_KHR_push_descriptor are exposed (the expected NGX requirements, unverified). At G0, buy an RTX 3060 12 GB, or confirm a second PCIe slot or another host for it.
  3. Harness, two modes. Capture: --write-movie --fixed-fps, fixed seeds. Timing: no movie writer, --disable-vsync, locked clocks, RD timestamps per named pass, wall-clock frame times, IO delays injected in wall time. Session-1 runner on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine with --gpu-index. P0 builds timing mode only as far as G0-4 and the G0-6 calibration need; the polish moves to P1b.
  4. RD/Vulkan RT substrate (all size S):
    • load vkGetAccelerationStructureDeviceAddressKHR and cache each AS address at creation (a spec-compliance fix);
    • accept hit_sbt_range 0 for ray-query-only use;
    • run the AS-invalidated check in compute_list_bind_uniform_set;
    • make raytracing_pipeline_create fail cleanly when SUPPORTS_RAYTRACING_PIPELINE is absent;
    • SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY additionally requires bufferDeviceAddress, and each driver returns a tier reason code with it; new SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE is driver knowledge only (Vulkan: a PCI vendor/device-ID allowlist of RT-core architectures, namely NVIDIA Turing+ RTX, AMD RDNA2+ and Intel Xe-HPG/Xe2 Arc); the CPU device-type check lives in the tier rule alone; AS limits exposed;
    • blas_create accepts storage buffers and validates their usage bits;
    • a shared transient scratch ring replaces the per-AS persistent scratch;
    • AS storage is counted in buffer_memory and the memory reports.
  5. Tier selection. Settings, rule and reason enum (§3), with the tier and reason in a minimal readout; the full debug overlay moves to P1b. SDFGI fallback wiring. Ray-query ShaderRDs are initialized only while the tier is active; their versions are created non-embedded, or filtered in _customize_shader_version for containers without acceleration-structure support, and baked only for Vulkan (Metal from P2 on).
  6. Windows driver default. EditorNode::get_initial_settings writes rendering/rendering_device/driver.windows = "vulkan" into new projects, a fork-level default. New GodotNext projects therefore give up Godot’s d3d12 HDR output and DComp transparency on Windows unless they switch back. In an existing d3d12 project, turning on nextgi/enabled shows a one-time modal offering the Vulkan switch: it keeps fallback_to_d3d12 = true, states the HDR/DComp loss and offers a restart. The fallback reason is still logged once.
  7. Toolchain check. #version 460 ray-query compute through ShaderRD; spirv-val from the Vulkan SDK, a dev dependency on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine and the M5 Max (not vendored); NVIDIA driver 621.01 acceptance of SPIR-V 1.3 modules using SPV_KHR_ray_query.
  8. RD smoke test. A testbed GDScript on a local RenderingDevice; a one-triangle fixture plus a 4096-ray fixture whose rays sit ≥1e-3 in barycentric distance from any edge with no ties in t, and a separate edge-straddling set; readback of t, instance custom index, primitive, barycentrics and front face. The same testbed runs the AS calibration microbenchmarks (G0-6).
  9. Reference path tracer v0 on Vulkan (D16). Near-unbiased (64-bounce cap, truncation bias < 1e-5), analytic only: it traces a TLAS that the GDScript RD testbed builds from known triangle soups (the furnace box, sealed box A with a 10 cm wall and sealed box B with a 20 cm wall), and each test passes in its material table. It does not depend on P1 infrastructure. Content lane: Color Box Room (sealed A, sealed B, an off-screen-light variant and a single mesh ≥40 m in extent with 10 cm interior walls) and Concave Courtyard with constant-parameter materials, for the P1a scene path. §13.4 metrics Q1–Q21 frozen.

Gate G0 (M0).

ItemModePass criteria
G0-1[V]RD smoke test on the RTX 4080 SUPER, run with --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --gpu-validation --gpu-abort, NV_ALLOW_RAYTRACING_VALIDATION=1 and synchronization validation on:
  • 0 validation errors;
  • spirv-val reports 0 errors on every ray-query module;
  • IDs exact on 100% of the 4096 rays;
  • against analytic values (Q17): t within 1e-5 relative, barycentrics within 1e-5 absolute;
  • edge-straddling set: exactly one of the adjacent triangles reported per ray, with 0 misses and 0 double hits.
G0-2[V/T]Tier selection, with 0 errors in every case:

  • --rendering-driver d3d12 falls back with D3D12_NO_DXR and exactly one log line;
  • M5 Max Metal falls back without crashing and without compiling any ray-query shader;
  • The Linux VM falls back with CPU_DEVICE or NO_RAY_QUERY;
  • the fallback’s GPU frame time is ≤2% above plain SDFGI.
G0-3[CPU/C]Reference path tracer v0; G0 closes on these analytic tests only:
  • furnace (closed box, Lambertian inner faces with ρ = 0.8, uniform emission Le on every inner face, no other lights): every pixel reads Le/(1 − ρ) within 1%;
  • sealed boxes A and B, lit only from outside: reference leak (Q3) <0.1%;
  • two runs with the same seed are bit-identical.
G0-4[T]Timing harness: 3 runs × 600 frames show ≤2% p95 spread, and capture mode is byte-identical across 2 runs.
G0-5[CPU]From a fresh clone, after git fetch origin 'refs/replace/*:refs/replace/*', git merge-base HEAD upstream/master resolves.
G0-6[T]AS calibration on the RTX 4080 SUPER, logged in Appendix A:

  • refit throughput;
  • BLAS build throughput, PREFER_FAST_TRACE vs PREFER_FAST_BUILD.
The results set line 1b’s per-frame triangle cap (provisional ≤250k triangles/frame) and are the first measurement of lines 1a and 1b.
G0-7[CPU]A project created by the GodotNext editor has driver.windows = "vulkan". In a d3d12 project, turning on nextgi/enabled shows the switch modal once, and accepting it leaves fallback_to_d3d12 = true.
