SPIR-V 1.3 is rejectedAdd the per-shader 1.4 opt-in (RD parameter plus ShaderRD cache key) before G0 closes; about +1–2 weeks.
The reference fails G0-3Fix units, BRDF or exposure. G0 stays open until it passes.

P1 — NextGI HWRT diffuse on Vulkan (G1a demo, then M1)

Depends on: G0. Estimate: P1a 4–6 weeks, then P1b about 3–4 months; the G1a demo at about month 3–4, M1 at about month 6–8. During P1 the content lane builds the seeded NextGI Perf timing scene (§13.3).

P1a — narrow quality spike

  • CPU-fed TLAS over static INSTANCE_MESH instances.
  • RT arena (D13): CPU-decoded float32 copies in 256 MiB chunks with free-list allocation; chunks never move. Each instance record carries chunk index + offset, read through buffer device address. Static BLAS, uncompacted.
  • Stage-1 cards, at most 12 normal-clustered per mesh, captured once with _render_material, outside the shadow batch.
  • World-space direct relight with ray-query shadows (D14). Light set: scenario lights culled against resident page bounds, with a scenario static-light list next to scenario->dynamic_lights. DYNAMIC lights relight every scheduled pass; STATIC lights dirty pages only when their properties change and are skipped for lightmapped receivers; DISABLED lights are excluded. Shadows are evaluated at 1/4 card resolution (a 2×2-texel mask) for ≤2 shadowed lights per texel per frame, rotated temporally; other lights reuse the last mask. A texel that projects into a valid camera directional split or positional shadow-atlas tile reuses that lookup; only the remainder is ray traced. High caps: ≤256k relit texels and ≤256k ray-traced shadow rays per frame.
  • HWRT diffuse gather at 1/16 rpp, hits shaded from the SRC by hit identity (D6); sky on a miss; ray-origin offset clamped to ≤5 cm. Temporal accumulation only, on static scenes with camera-only reprojection from depth.
  • Reference scene path (D16): Color Box Room and Concave Courtyard rendered through the RT arena and CPU TLAS. G1a depends on it. The LightmapGI cross-check on Color Box Room B is report-only: a mismatch above 10% opens an investigation, which may conclude LightmapGI is the wrong side.
  • Deliberately absent: WRC, per-instance LRU, dynamic invalidation, velocity.

Gate G1a — quality only. Cost is recorded against a sanity bound of 3× the 1.04 ms Ada diffuse-path ceiling, i.e. 3.1 ms.

ItemModePass criteria
G1a[C]Static Color Box Room (A, B, off-screen light), Concave Courtyard, and a single mesh ≥40 m in extent containing 10 cm interior walls:
  • indirect diffuse patch error ≤20% (Q1);
  • card-hit coverage ≥85% (Q2);
  • leak ≤1% at 10 cm and at 20 cm (Q3).
OutcomePre-committed response
Coverage below 85%Raise the card count or add an interior split, then re-run.
Leak above 1%Check the ray-origin offset and eps_rt, then re-run.
Error above 20% with coverage metRun the diagnostic mode that shades hits with path-traced hit radiance, to separate SRC error from gather error.
Still failing after two iterationsStop and hold a §2/§3 scope review. No silent relaxation.

P1b — production slice

  • AS (first P1b item, merged by about month 3–4): RD build modes (BUILD/UPDATE, §9) on Vulkan, landing before the Metal lane needs them; async BLAS compaction; an AS memory budget that falls back with AS_MEMORY.
  • Per-instance radiance pages (solid-angle priority, LRU, WRC fallback); material atlas per (mesh, override set).
  • Batched atlas card capture, ≤64 cards or ≤1M texels per frame: a capture variant of the scene shader reads each card’s view-projection from a storage buffer indexed by push constant, with the per-card transform folded into instance data. Up to 64 cards share one render pass, each with its own viewport and scissor. Fallback: one _setup_environment UBO per card inside the pass, with used_uniform_buffer_count budgeted.
  • WRC radiance cache (D15).
  • Three SRC work streams with light-change, transform and occluder invalidation. Relight texels and ray-traced SRC shadow rays are each capped per frame at 128k Medium, 256k High and 512k Epic. Transform dirtying is emitted inside RendererSceneCull::_update_instance.
  • Scenario-wide RT instance registry: rt_scene_radius, solid-angle cull, cap of 100k instances. Static MultiMesh, including non-baked GridMap octants, is CPU-fed from the existing CPU cache (_multimesh_make_local) within that cap. One RT scene and one TLAS rebuild per scenario per frame, anchored on the main viewport camera and shared by all views. NextGI is off in ReflectionProbe and preview renders, which sample the WRC and sky only.
  • Arena mutation: mesh_surface_update_vertex_region and the other surface-mutation paths update the CPU-decoded copy and mark the BLAS dirty. Surfaces updated every frame are treated as dynamic: refit, or excluded with RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED. Defragmentation rebuilds the affected BLASes and rewrites their records within line 1b.
  • Velocity: the forced NextGI prepass (depth + normal-roughness, +VoxelGI-id) writes MOTION_VECTORS for moving and skinned instances. While NextGI is active, the opaque color pass and RENDER_LIST_MOTION do not re-write velocity for them. Gather accumulation and denoise consume this frame’s velocity.
  • Environment, RenderingServer and ProjectSettings plumbing.
  • NextGI block placed after _render_shadow_end, replacing gi.process_gi for the view.
  • using_nextgi added to the GI-buffer instance flag; reflection-probe fallback weighting; SSIL auto-off.
  • SSEffects::allocate_last_frame_buffer gains a full-resolution, full-mip parameter.
  • NextGI ambient volume feeds volumetric fog.
  • Runtime safety net (§3): a startup self-test at tier selection (one triangle plus a 64-ray fixture against analytic IDs and t; on mismatch, SELFTEST_FAILED), and a crash latch (a device loss while HWRT is active persists an “HWRT faulted” flag under user://, keyed by driver version; later launches fall back with HWRT_FAULTED until the driver version changes or hardware_ray_tracing = force).
  • Moved from P0: harness timing-mode polish and the full debug overlay. The LightmapGI cross-check also left P0 and runs on the P1a scene path.

Gate G1 (M1).

ItemModePass criteria
G1-1[C]The G1a items hold with the full pipeline.
G1-2[C]Moving Light + Door and Time of Day, both including the off-screen light, with 0 rebakes:
  • step forms (Q4a: light toggled, sun jumping 15°, door teleported closed → open, all at frame 0): e(10) ≤25% and e(32) ≤10%;
  • continuous forms (Q4b: door opening 0 → 1 m over 2 s, sun sweeping 90° over 10 s): mean elag ≤25% over the motion, and e(32) ≤10% measured 32 frames after the motion ends.
G1-3[T]NextGI Perf, 1080p High:
  • diffuse path ≤1.04 ms p95 on the RTX 4080 SUPER and ≤2.60 ms on the RTX 3060 12 GB, with each pass ≤1.25× its line item (lines 2a and 2b timed separately);
  • per-pass Ada:3060 ratios recorded, replacing the 2.5× factor;

  • no scratch growth.
G1-6[CPU/V]Safety net: an injected self-test mismatch falls back with SELFTEST_FAILED; an injected device loss with HWRT active makes the next launch fall back with HWRT_FAULTED, which clears only on a driver-version change or hardware_ray_tracing = force.
G1-7[CPU]From an editor where HWRT is active, exporting Metal and D3D12 presets with shader_baker/enabled gives 0 errors.
M1: dynamic HWRT diffuse GI on RTX-class Vulkan GPUs (Windows validated on Ada; Linux enabled, unvalidated). Default off; SDFGI fallback elsewhere.

P2 — Metal HWRT (M2)

Depends on: G0 for the Metal driver lane (items 1–6 plus G2-1 parity), which starts right after G0 in parallel with P1, so the M5 Max runs ray query locally by about month 3–4. The lane needs these §9 items merged: the ray-query-only TLAS fix and the capability flags (G0), and build modes (P1b, merged by about month 3–4) for item 5’s refit; items 1–4, 6 and G2-1 parity do not wait for them. Item 7 and NextGI on Metal (G2-2 to G2-5) wait for G1. Estimate: 6–10 weeks (driver lane 4–6 from G0, the rest 2–4 after G1); M2 at about month 6–9.

  1. Capability detection. supportsRaytracing(); hardware RT = supportsFamily(Apple9). Extend the family scan to Apple10/Apple11 in the same change as MetalDeviceProfile::GPU and both switch statements; otherwise the profile code hits CRASH_NOW_MSG. SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY only on macOS ≥14, with argument buffers on and Barriers sync; otherwise the driver returns METAL_SYNC_MODE or METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS.
  2. AS objects. MTLPrimitiveAccelerationStructureDescriptor for BLAS; MTLIndirectInstanceAccelerationStructureDescriptor for the TLAS, with 72-byte column-major records that reference each BLAS by gpuResourceID; API_TRAIT_ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE_INSTANCE_SIZE = 72; a driver-owned dummy BLAS with mask 0 for empty instances; ExtendedLimits never set. Arena records are read through gpuAddress.
  3. Residency. AS objects are allocated from placement heaps (Heap::newAccelerationStructure), so the per-encoder useHeaps keeps indirectly referenced BLASes resident.
  4. Encoding. An AS command-encoder state joins the level fences. All BLAS builds finish before the TLAS build.
  5. Refit on the P1b build-mode API (needed by P3) and compaction (writeCompactedSize → copyAndCompact → rewrite TLAS records).
  6. Shaders. Shader-container case for UNIFORM_TYPE_ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE (DataTypeInstanceAccelerationStructure) and RayQueryKHR detection; argument-buffer gpuResourceID write; SPIRV-Cross: cherry-pick df0828ca and add an AccelerationStructure case to the argument-buffer padding switch. Ray-query ShaderRD versions join the Metal shader-baker set.
  7. Reference path tracer on Metal (the D16 port).

Gate G2 (M2).

ItemModePass criteria
G2-1[V]Ray-query parity (Q17) on the 4096-ray fixture, M5 Max vs RTX 4080 SUPER:
  • IDs exact on 100% of rays;
  • t within 1e-4 relative and barycentrics within 1e-4 absolute;
  • edge-straddling set: exactly one adjacent triangle per ray, 0 misses, 0 double hits;
  • front face matches on the winding/FLIP_FACING fixture.
G2-2[V]0 Metal API and shader validation errors in all G1 scenes (argument buffers, Barriers). Hazard-tracking mode and disabled argument buffers fall back with METAL_SYNC_MODE and METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS and 0 errors.
G2-3[C]NextGI on the M5 Max passes G1-1 and G1-2 against the Metal-run reference. FLIP mean ≤0.02 vs Vulkan (fixed seeds) on Color Box Room B and on Moving Light + Door at frame 32.
G2-4[T]M5 Max, NextGI Perf, 1080p High: diffuse path ≤2.6 ms p95.
G2-5[CPU]A tier-rule test with mocked Apple7/Apple8 families selects the fallback NOT_HW_ACCELERATED.
M2 deliverable: HWRT diffuse on Vulkan and Metal, validated on the M5 Max (Apple10); Apple M3/M4 enabled, unvalidated.

PU — DLSS Super Resolution and DLAA (gate GU; never blocks G1–G5)

Depends on: nothing for item 1, which can start now; G0 for PU-core (items 2–4); G1 for everything else. Estimate: PU-core is S + S + M in the rhi-vulkan lane, ordered behind that lane’s P1 items; the module part is about 3–4 months of upscalers-lane agent work under lead review; GU at about month 9–12 (planning assumption). If PU review competes with the P3 critical path, PU waits. PU issues target m3; if GU is still open at G3 sign-off they move to m4, and at G5 sign-off to post-v1, each move logged in Appendix A.

  1. License packet (pu-dlss-license-packet, now): the §19 records, the SDK tag and rel-library SHA-256 pins, the five NVIDIA questions, and check_proprietary_sdk_files.py in static_checks.yml, mirrored in .gitignore. It closes before anyone downloads the SDK for implementation or reads its headers for implementation; the only download inside it is the lead’s pin-recording clone outside any git work tree.
  2. Driver-callback pipeline stages (pu-rd-callback-stages; after G0, rhi-vulkan): RD::driver_callback_add gains p_stages (default PIPELINE_STAGE_ALL_COMMANDS_BIT), stored as the command’s self_stages, with a C++ dev sync fixture.
  3. Vulkan native interop (pu-rd-native-interop; after G0, rhi-vulkan): command_buffer_get_native_handle() and texture_get_native_info(); the base class returns 0 or ERR_UNAVAILABLE on Metal and D3D12 (there is no dummy RenderingDeviceDriver).
  4. Vulkan extension providers and the validation hook (pu-vk-extension-providers; after G0, rhi-vulkan): optional instance and device extensions and pNext features added before vkCreateInstance and vkCreateDevice, coexisting with VulkanHooks and OpenXR. The validation-message hook and a debug-build-only created-handle registry (DEBUG_ENABLED; never in template_release), populated only under --gpu-validation, implement the object-attributed rule (§14 Never cut).
  5. Scaling mode DLSS (pu-scaling-mode-dlss-fallback; after G1): VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_DLSS = 6, ViewportScaling3DReason, the DLSS → MetalFX temporal → FSR2 fallback chain (§3.2), scaling_3d_mode_get_availability and viewport_get_scaling_3d_status, the runtime hint entry, the DLSS project settings and the extension-API file.
  6. TemporalUpscaler hook (pu-temporal-upscaler-hook; after G1): SCALE_EXTERNAL dispatch in the pre-tonemap slot; dense velocity (the DENSE row in _nextgi_velocity_plan, exact sentinel, w clamp, MotionVectorsStore no longer limited to METAL_MFXTEMPORAL_ENABLED); reset_temporal_history(); one context per (viewport, view); VIEW_UNSUPPORTED for multiview.
  7. modules/dlss build (pu-dlss-module-build): DLSSBackend with NGX and mock backends, disabled by default; the pinned SDK installer; the runtime copy with bin/dlss_runtime.json; the CI mock-backend job (§19).
  8. NGX lifecycle and the SR/DLAA effect (pu-dlss-sr-effect): extension requirements, NVSDK_NGX_VULKAN_Init_with_ProjectID, per-view features, evaluate in a driver callback, the object-attributed validation filter and the loaded-DLL record.
  9. Export (pu-dlss-export-packaging): the module’s EditorExportPlugin (rel runtime with SHA-256 and signature checks, NVIDIA notices, the duty warning), Project ID generation and the About line.
  10. Content (pu-content-upscaler-torture): the Upscaler Torture scene (§13.3), the 2× scale + MSAA 8× Q19 references with their MSAA 4× convergence captures, and the Q21 velocity dump.
  11. Stretch: DLSS on D3D12 (pu-dlss-d3d12; after GU; one of §14’s cuts allowed at any time): record which barrier path the RTX 4080 SUPER machine uses and implement only that one, plus the D3D12 native handle and cached-state invalidation; single view.

Gate GU (phase PU; target m3; pu-gate-gu, lane lead). No G1–G5 item depends on GU. DLSS SR/DLAA joins the claim of the first milestone (M3, then M4) signed off after GU passes. DLSS on Linux/NVIDIA becomes validated only if optional Linux boot of the RTX 4080 SUPER machine (P3 item 9) also runs GU-2 and GU-5.

Preflight for every DLSS gate run. The lead’s DLSS-enabled editor and templates (dlss_backend=ngx), built on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine, with NVIDIA’s rel runtime from the pinned tag and allow_ota_updates = false. Presets are pinned unless an item says otherwise: K for DLAA, Quality and Balanced; M for Performance; L for Ultra Performance. A run is invalid, not failed, if the nvngx_dlss module loaded after feature creation (found with EnumProcessModules on Windows, /proc/self/maps on Linux) has a SHA-256 other than the pinned rel SHA in bin/dlss_runtime.json, or if the NVIDIA App / driver-profile DLSS override checklist for the GodotNext executable is not recorded as “off” with an archived screenshot. Every report records the loaded DLL path, version and SHA-256; the NVIDIA driver version (621.01 at plan time); the preset the module set; and the verbose NGX log (the app logging callback, guide §3.19, or __NGX_LOG_LEVEL on Linux).

ItemModePass criteria
GU-1[CPU]Contracts and doctests. Core tests (tests/servers/rendering/test_upscalers.cpp) on the M5 Max, the RTX 4080 SUPER machine and the Linux VM:
  • enum values: VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_DLSS == 6, NEAREST == 5, MAX == 7; Viewport.SCALING_3D_MODE_DLSS == 6; scaling_3d_mode_type(DLSS) == TEMPORAL;
  • each ViewportScaling3DReason is produced exactly once by the mocked availability fixture; scaling_3d_mode_get_availability() and viewport_get_scaling_3d_status() return the requested mode, active mode and reason;
  • DLSS helper math: mip bias = log2(scale) − 1.0 + user bias within 1e-6 at scales 1.0, 0.667, 0.58, 0.5 and 0.333; jitter phases 8/18/24/32/72 for DLAA/Quality/Balanced/Performance/Ultra Performance at 2560×1440 and 3840×2160 targets; InMVScale = internal_size; InJitterOffset = taa_jitter × internal_size × 0.5;
  • FSR2, MetalFX and TAA helpers unchanged: bias −0.5, truncating phase count;
  • two single-view viewports get two contexts; view_count 2 with DLSS gives VIEW_UNSUPPORTED;
  • the _nextgi_velocity_plan SCALE_EXTERNAL row equals the DENSE (MetalFX) row, with NextGI on and off;
  • the PRESERVE_WRITTEN sentinel test is exact equality: (-1.0, -1.0) is replaced, while (-1.0001, -1.2) and (-1.0, -0.999) are kept;
  • the temporal-history reset is one-shot;
  • extension providers, with a mock provider and a mock VulkanHooks: each requested name appears exactly once in the create infos passed to the hook, a missing optional extension reports EXTENSION_MISSING, and device creation succeeds;
  • the default and DLSS builds produce identical --doctool output; only the DLSS build’s rendering/scaling_3d/mode hint has a :6 entry;
  • validate_extension_api.sh passes, with misc/extension_api_validation/4.7-stable/GodotNext-DLSS-scaling-mode.txt listing both MAX changes (6 → 7).
Module tests: with dlss_backend=mock in CI, availability → reason mapping, mode snapping and the [0.30, 1.0] scale window; with dlss_backend=ngx on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine only, the NGX flag and parameter mapping (IsHDR, DepthInverted, MVLowRes and AutoExposure set; MVJittered clear; InPreExposure 1).
GU-2[V]Validation and synchronization on the RTX 4080 SUPER (--verbose --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --gpu-validation --gpu-abort, synchronization validation on): Upscaler Torture plus the 3 legacy scenes at DLAA, Quality, Performance and Ultra Performance, 1440p and 2160p output. This is the only v1 gate item that counts DLSS-on validation (§14 Never cut); post-v1, the P6i and P6j gates also apply the attribution rule.
  • Object-attributed rule: 0 messages that reference a GodotNext-created handle (the shared VkInstance, VkPhysicalDevice, VkDevice, VkQueue and the frame’s command buffer do not count). Any other message passes only if it is in docs/godotnext/dlss-validation-allowlist.md (VUID + DLL SHA-256 + driver version, lead-reviewed), is absent on the same run with dlss_backend=mock, and persists with GODOT_RG_FULL_BARRIERS=1;
  • callback-stage fixture (C++, dev builds only; compute write → driver callback → fragment read, called from godotnext/testbed/tests/rd_callback_sync.gd): 0 SYNC-HAZARD messages, and at least 1 when the fixture’s dev flag forces the stages to 0;
  • NGX synchronization A/B: DLSS Quality at 1440p, capture mode, 600 frames, once with the default graph and once with GODOT_RG_FULL_BARRIERS=1 GODOT_RG_REORDER=0; per-frame FLIP of the upscaled output ≤ max(0.002, 2 × the run-to-run spread) on every frame;
  • 30-minute soak on the RTX 4080 SUPER and the RTX 3060 12 GB (resize every 2 s; cycle DLAA/Q/B/P/UP every 10 s; presets K↔M every 60 s; DLSS↔FSR2 every 120 s): 0 device-lost events with VK_EXT_device_fault on; the attribution rule holds throughout; NGX-reported memory is 0 after the last switch to FSR2; the RD memory report is within ±1% of the pre-soak FSR2 baseline;
  • M5 Max, MetalFX temporal, after the callback-stage and MotionVectorsStore changes: 0 Metal API/shader validation errors, and FLIP ≤0.005 against a capture from the previous commit.
GU-3[V/CPU]Fallback matrix. Every case: no crash, 0 validation errors, the expected backend and reason, and exactly one log line.
  • the RTX 4080 SUPER machine --gpu-index 0 (UHD 770), DLSS build → FSR2, NOT_NVIDIA_RTX;

GU-4[C]Image quality on the RTX 4080 SUPER at 1440p and 2160p output, on Upscaler Torture and the 3 legacy scenes. Each scene’s Q19 reference is accepted only if FLIP between its MSAA 4× and 8× references is ≤0.01; content masks that fail this are excluded and reported.
  • Q21 = 0 on every frame of every run, except the scripted reset frame, which is reported;
  • Q19, moving form, mean across the gate scenes per output resolution, with tolerance = max(0.002, 2 × the measured run-to-run spread): DLAA ≤ TAA-native + tolerance and ≤0.03; DLSS Quality (0.667) ≤ FSR2 Quality + tolerance and ≤0.05; DLSS Performance (0.5) ≤ FSR2 Performance + tolerance and ≤0.07. The absolute values (0.03, 0.05, 0.07, 10%) are provisional and may reset once with the data; the relative comparisons are fixed.

P3 — Reflections, denoiser, dynamic actors, translucency (M3 = NextGI v1; Slice 1 complete)

Depends on: G1 for Vulkan, G2 for the Metal items. Estimate: about 6–10 months; M3 at about month 12–18.

  1. Specular path. Screen trace, then ray query for roughness ≤0.4 (High: 1/4 rpp, half resolution). Hits shaded from the SRC; WRC or sky on a miss. ReflectionProbe weighted by (1 − NextGI confidence).
  2. Denoiser split by signal (D8), reprojecting with the P1b prepass velocity.
  3. Firefly clamp with a clamped-energy counter; blue-noise sample rotation.
  4. Quality presets. quality = auto (default) | medium | high | epic. Medium: 1/32 rpp diffuse, reflections only for roughness ≤0.25 at quarter resolution, relight cap 128k texels. Epic total = 2× High; per-line values as tabled (§2.1). auto picks High, Medium or the SDFGI fallback from a ≤5 ms startup microbenchmark on the G0 ray-query fixture plus a coarse compute-unit or GPU-core class, and logs the choice with its reason.
  5. Skinned actors: arena slots, BLAS refit and bind-pose cards (D17). The content lane adds 4 skinned characters to NextGI Perf.
  6. Translucency volume (1/16 screen × 32 slices, about 261k froxels and 6 MiB at FP16 SH-L1 at 1080p) and a forward alpha branch.
  7. Emissive contract: emissives smaller than 4 card texels must be authored as lights (D17).
  8. GPU-written TLAS instances for particles and dynamic MultiMesh, via tlas_build_from_buffer plus an opaque driver-defined acceleration_structure_get_instance_reference (§14 cut list, item 1).
  9. Optional Linux validation: run G3 on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine booted into Linux (NVIDIA proprietary driver) from a separate disk. Only a pass moves Linux/NVIDIA to validated.
  10. Default-off MIT hooks for the post-v1 DLSS-RR mode (D21): hit distance (p3-specular-path), sampling mode and specular-filter bypass (p3-denoise-specular-fireflies), stabilizer switch and probe-history cap (p3-denoise-diffuse). The default path stays byte-identical.

Gate G3 (M3). The denoise and ghost-trail criteria depend on the P1b prepass velocity.

P4 — NextGeo offline, assets, spikes

Depends on: G3. The offline DAG prototype may start in an agent lane from P2 onward. Estimate: about 3–5 months.

  1. meshoptimizer SCsub additions (D3).
  2. DAG builder. One DAG per mesh over its eligible opaque surfaces, with material seams treated as attribute seams; borders are locked only against ineligible sibling surfaces. Group-shared LOD bounds. BVH8 over cluster groups.
  3. VirtualGeometryData and .ngm v1.
  4. RT proxy at eps_rt = min(max_extent/256, rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/rt_max_error), with the setting defaulting to 2 cm at import scale. Capped at 128k triangles per BLAS; when the cap binds, the proxy is split into several BLASes per mesh rather than coarsened.
  5. Importer integration. Per-importer option names. Single-mesh mode builds after merge_importer_meshes.
  6. Export. A built-in export plugin: an importer-written manifest plus add_file, independent of _customize_resource, with stable keys for embedded meshes and stale-file cleanup. Default stripping for nextgeo presets. The encryption check runs before the first byte is written.
  7. Spikes. Draw-identity benchmark of (c), (a) and (b) on Vulkan and Metal, with D3D12 reported. Material-bucket resolve prototype (D7) on the Feature Parity Room.
  8. Dense City seeded generator: INSTANCE_MESH nodes, ≥5k instances.

Gate G4.

ItemModePass criteria
G4-1[CPU]DAG and residency correctness:
  • across 10k random views × instance scales {0.5, 1, 4}: projected(parent) ≥ projected(child) on 100% of DAG edges;
  • 1k random cuts: 0 overlapped and 0 uncovered source triangles;
  • 10k random resident page sets: every selected cut is a valid partition.

P5 — NextGeo GPU draw and v1 integration (M4 = v1)

Depends on: G4 and G3. Estimate: about 5–7 months; M4 at about month 20–30.

  1. Persistent GPU instance records.
  2. Culling. GPU instance cull. Level-synchronous BVH traversal: one indirect dispatch per level, no persistent threads. Cluster cull at max_pixel_error 1.0. Two-phase Hi-Z.
  3. Drawing. Draw path (c); visibility raster into R32 (cluster | triangle).
  4. Resolve. Stage A and Stage B resolve variants (D7); routing in _fill_render_list per pass mode.
  5. Residency. Refine only into group-complete residency. Load parents first, evict children first. Only the main view requests pages. Root pages render through the virtual pipeline.
  6. Shadows. Virtual shadow cuts at ≤1 shadow-texel error, with no page requests. Positional lights become shadow-dirty when their cut changes.
  7. Other views and RT. Auxiliary views use the proxy. Proxy BLASes join the NextGI TLAS.
  8. Frame rules: per-upscaler velocity and load/clear rules (§6.1), including the DLSS DENSE row.
  9. Stage-2 cards.
  10. Content: Exclusion Matrix, Slow IO Flight; the Feature Parity Room (built in P4) gains texture streaming.

Gate G5 (M4 = v1).

Each item gets its own numeric gate. Where the gate is not yet written, it is set before the phase starts; the indicative costs below come from §2.1 and are not v1 claims. (P6i and P6j need none of P6a–P6h; the lead may pull them forward within P6.)

P6aD3D12 DXR ray query in compute (DXR Tier 1.1, SM 6.5), built on PR #118654 plus a forked Mesa NIR-to-DXIL patch (D12).A D3D12 vs Vulkan conformance corpus gives identical hits on the RTX 4080 SUPER; the DXIL validator debug path reports 0 errors; the G3 gates pass on D3D12; the UHD 770 falls back.
P6bSDF+SRC+WRC software tier, with world-space brick card lists and sparse near-field bricks of 5 cm or finer.Per §2.1: forced on the RTX 3060, High ≤4.0 ms and Epic ≤8.0 ms; UHD 770 class, P6b-only Low preset (outside the v1 quality enum) ≤6.0 ms at 720p internal; diffuse (Q1) ≤20%, coverage (Q2) ≥85%, leak (Q3) ≤5% at 20 cm, Q4a e(10) ≤25%.
P6cRay-query sun and local shadows, then MegaLights-class many-light sampling.Indicative: sun + ≤8 local shadows including denoise ≤0.4 ms Ada / ≤1.0 ms mid-range.
P6dHit lighting, Epic only.Indicative: +2–9 ms mid-range.
P6eFar-field TLAS: merged proxies from 200 m to 1 km, in a separate TLAS.Separate budget.
P6fVendor-optional NVIDIA cluster AS plus partitioned TLAS, OMM. (DLSS-RR moved to P6i, D21.)Set before the phase starts.
P6gM2 experimental opt-in (needs an M2 test machine); Metal 4 AS path (needs an MTL4 backend).Set before the phase starts.
P6hVSM, mesh shaders, software micro-raster, alpha-tested virtual geometry, MultiMesh as a virtual consumer, XR.Set before the phase starts.
P6iDLSS Ray Reconstruction mode (D21): RR guides, sampling-correlation spike, RR evaluate in the upscaler slot. It uses the P3 hooks.Set before the phase starts. Provisional minimums on the RTX 4080 SUPER, NextGI Epic + RR (preset F), OTA off, at DLAA 1920×1080 and DLSS Quality 2560×1440 output, against D16 at output resolution:
  • [C] Q20 on the Q1 patches ≤20% and ≤ D8 + DLSS SR at the same mode + 2 pp; Roughness Corridor Q20 per bin ≤30% for roughness ≤0.4 and ≤ D8 + SR + 2 pp per bin ([0, 0.1) reported); Q8 ≤1.5%; Q10 ≤10% at frame 4 after the occluder move (reset cut reported); the G1-2 forms (Q4a e(10) ≤25% and e(32) ≤10%; Q4b mean elag ≤25% and e(32) ≤10%); Q16 ≤2%; Glass & Water σ ≤3%; Q21 = 0;
  • [T] RR pass at Performance: line U2 = NVIDIA’s Table 3 values 1.06 / 1.80 / 3.95 ms, so pass at ≤1.33 / ≤2.25 / ≤4.94 ms for 1080p / 1440p / 2160p output; DLAA 1080p and Quality 1440p get provisional lines of 1.5× and 1.25× the Performance line at the same output; U3 = (colour-pass time with guides − without) + the pack-pass timestamp, each a p95 over 600 frames, ≤0.10 ms Ada at 1080p internal; line 8 timestamps absent; lines 1a–7 within their Epic items; NGX RR memory ≤193 / ≤336 / ≤724 MB; RTX 3060 report-only;
  • [V] the GU-2 attribution rule holds; a 30-minute soak toggling RR every 60 s gives 0 device-lost events and VRAM within ±1%.
On failure, RR stays behind the experimental setting and D8 is unaffected.
P6jDLSS Frame Generation / MFG and NVIDIA Reflex (D22). Spike first (Streamline manual hooking vs NGX-direct DLSS-G); then Reflex, the HUD-less 3D colour / UI split, and DLSS-G on the main window’s swapchain only.Set before the phase starts. Provisional minimums:
  • [V] the GU-2 attribution rule holds with FG and Reflex on (RTX 4080 SUPER);
  • [T] Reflex On vs Off on GPU-bound NextGI Perf: PC latency from the latency markers is at least 10% lower;
  • [T] FG 2x: displayed frame-time p99 ≤1.5× the median (PresentMon); the FG pass ceiling is set from the first measurement, because NVIDIA publishes FG costs only for the RTX 4090 (1.35–2.77 ms);
  • [C] static-UI scene: UI pixels with FG on vs off have FLIP ≤0.005, which proves the HUD-less split;
  • [CPU/V] fallbacks with reasons on the UHD 770, on GPUs older than RTX 40 (for FG), and on non-Windows targets if Streamline is chosen.
MFG 3x–6x is enabled but unvalidated.

13. Validation plan

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

The snapshot has doctest CPU coverage but no GPU golden-image harness; P0 builds one, and its timing-mode polish lands in P1b. --headless disables rendering and uses RendererDummy::MeshStorage, which hides GPU buffer readback bugs, so every GPU gate runs windowed on a real RenderingDevice driver. Each gate is tagged [C], [T], [V] (validation run) or [CPU] (doctest or CPU tool).

DLSS runs follow the GU preflight: NVIDIA’s rel DLLs from the developer-supplied SDK, with OTA off and pinned presets; the loaded-DLL SHA-256 checked against the manifest; the driver-override checklist recorded; the NGX log archived. Dev DLLs (overlay, jitter/MV hotkeys) are for local debugging only. RenderDoc does not support DLSS applications (guide §8.6), so captures use Nsight Graphics. DLSS-on validation counts only under the GU-2 attribution rule.

Capture mode [C]

--write-movie + --fixed-fps, fixed seeds in every stochastic pass. Used for image, error and convergence gates (FLIP, patch error, coverage, leak, e(t), σ).

Timing mode [T]

No movie writer, --disable-vsync, locked clocks, RD timestamps per named pass, wall-clock frame time, IO delays injected in wall time. Used for every ms, p95/p99 and streaming gate.

  • Local Apple M5 Max (Metal): daily build and parity loop. The P2 Metal driver lane starts right after G0, so ray query runs here by about month 3–4. Metal HWRT is validated here (Apple10 only).
  • RTX 4080 SUPER machine (Windows 11): GPU runs execute inside the active Session-1 console through an interactive Session-1 task runner with an explicit --gpu-index: device 1 = RTX 4080 SUPER (Vulkan HWRT), device 0 = Intel UHD 770 (non-RT fallback). Raw SSH Session 0 cannot create desktop swapchains. An optional P3 task runs G3 with the machine booted into Linux (NVIDIA proprietary driver) from a separate disk; Linux/NVIDIA moves from enabled-but-unvalidated to validated only if that run passes.
  • Linux VM (x86_64): builds, doctests and lavapipe functional tests only.
  • Mid-range: RTX 3060 12 GB, bought at G0 (or a second PCIe slot or another host confirmed for it). Mid-range [T] lines are measured on it, never inferred from Ada. G1-3 adds its diffuse-path line (≤2.60 ms on NextGI Perf), and G1’s per-pass Ada:3060 ratios replace the 2.5× factor from then on. M3 cannot be declared without it.

13.1 Standard repository & CI gates

# Local daily dev & Metal correctness (Apple M5 Max, 128 GB RAM):
scons platform=macos target=editor dev_mode=yes arch=arm64

# Vulkan HWRT gate host, plus UHD 770 and D3D12 fallback checks (RTX 4080 SUPER):
scons platform=windows target=editor dev_mode=yes d3d12=yes

# Linux builds, doctests, lavapipe functional tests (Linux VM, x86_64):
scons platform=linuxbsd target=editor dev_mode=yes

./bin/godot.<platform>.editor.<arch> --headless --test --force-colors
./bin/godot.<platform>.editor.<arch> --headless --test --force-colors --test-case='*NextGeo*'
./bin/godot.<platform>.editor.<arch> --headless --test --force-colors --test-case='*NextGI*'
./bin/godot.<platform>.editor.<arch> --doctool --headless
git diff --exit-code -- doc/classes && git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -- doc/classes | (! grep .)

HWRT validation runs [V]

# GODOT=./bin/godot.<platform>.editor.<arch> --path godotnext/testbed
# the RTX 4080 SUPER machine, Session-1 runner. Synchronization validation on; spirv-val on every emitted ray-query module.
NV_ALLOW_RAYTRACING_VALIDATION=1 $GODOT --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --gpu-validation --gpu-abort

# Fallback checks: expect NO_RAY_QUERY, then D3D12_NO_DXR with exactly one log line.
$GODOT --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 0
$GODOT --rendering-driver d3d12

# Memory (Vulkan only) and profiling, plus Nsight Graphics captures:
$GODOT --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --extra-gpu-memory-tracking
$GODOT --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --gpu-profile

# M5 Max: Metal API and shader validation, Barriers sync and argument buffers on.
MTL_DEBUG_LAYER=1 MTL_SHADER_VALIDATION=1 $GODOT --rendering-driver metal
  • spirv-val comes from the Vulkan SDK. It is a dev dependency on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine and the M5 Max, and is not vendored.
  • AS calibration [T] (G0, RTX 4080 SUPER): TLAS build at 10k, 50k and 100k instances; refit throughput; BLAS build throughput with PREFER_FAST_TRACE vs PREFER_FAST_BUILD. The result sets the per-frame triangle cap of §2.1 line 1b (provisional ≤250k triangles/frame).
  • MTL_DEBUG_LAYER and MTL_SHADER_VALIDATION are the standard Apple variables, not re-verified for this toolchain. Xcode captures that inspect acceleration structures go through the existing "Godot Frame" capture scope.
  • vulkaninfo JSON for each gate host is archived with every gate report. The testbed pins driver.windows=vulkan, which is also the GodotNext default for new projects (D12).
  • Template gates: target=template_release plus a minimal build with default modules off / 3D disabled where CI already exercises that shape, proving no editor-only symbols leak into runtime code.
  • Fork CI hygiene: trim .github/workflows/runner.yml to Linux, Windows, and macOS editor/template builds + static checks (instead of upstream’s 9-workflow matrix), guard missing HEAD~1 on shallow/single-commit clones in .github/workflows/static_checks.yml, and never attach self-hosted GPU runners (RTX 4080 SUPER) to public PR triggers on Legoless/GodotNext (use manual dispatch or SSH-triggered gate scripts only).
  • If ClassDB API changes, run the repository’s extension API validation script used by CI.
  • Windows covers Vulkan HWRT on the RTX 4080 SUPER, the non-RT fallback on the UHD 770, and the D3D12 fallback (NextGeo + SDFGI). On the Linux VM, lavapipe selects the fallback (CPU_DEVICE or NO_RAY_QUERY) rather than aborting; force runs functional ray-query tests there only if vulkaninfo shows VK_KHR_ray_query (unverified).
  • No skipped tests: a capability-specific test either asserts the fallback contract or is a separate hardware-gated test-project case with recorded machine/GPU/driver.

DLSS builds and runs

# DLSS-enabled builds (developer-supplied SDK, never committed; local hardware only).
# the RTX 4080 SUPER machine, Session-1 runner:
python misc/scripts/install_dlss_sdk.py --tag <pinned> --dest %USERPROFILE%\.godotnext\dlss-sdk --accept-nvidia-license
scons platform=windows target=editor dev_mode=yes module_dlss_enabled=yes dlss_sdk_path=%USERPROFILE%\.godotnext\dlss-sdk\<tag>
# ...and again with target=template_release.
$GODOT --verbose --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --gpu-validation --gpu-abort -- --scaling=dlss --dlss-preset=k

# Full-barrier A/B (GU-2): the same run with full barriers and no graph reordering.
GODOT_RG_FULL_BARRIERS=1 GODOT_RG_REORDER=0 $GODOT --verbose --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --gpu-validation --gpu-abort -- --scaling=dlss --dlss-preset=k

# Fallback checks: expect FSR2 with NOT_NVIDIA_RTX, then FSR2 with API_UNSUPPORTED (until pu-dlss-d3d12 lands).
$GODOT --rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 0 -- --scaling=dlss
$GODOT --rendering-driver d3d12 -- --scaling=dlss

# Linux under WSL2 on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine: expect FSR2 with NOT_NVIDIA_RTX on lavapipe.
scons platform=linuxbsd target=editor dev_mode=yes module_dlss_enabled=yes dlss_sdk_path=~/.godotnext/dlss-sdk/<tag>
VK_DRIVER_FILES=<lavapipe icd> $GODOT --rendering-driver vulkan -- --scaling=dlss

# CI (linux_builds.yml, windows_builds.yml): mock backend, no SDK:
scons platform=<platform> target=editor module_dlss_enabled=yes dlss_backend=mock tests=yes
  • .github/workflows/static_checks.yml runs misc/scripts/check_proprietary_sdk_files.py.
  • The Linux VM never runs NGX-backend builds (§19(g)); it runs only default and mock builds.

13.2 New CPU/doctest & GPU round-trip coverage

  • DAG [CPU]: material seams handled as attribute seams, with borders locked only against ineligible sibling surfaces; locked-border crack detection; degenerate/non-indexed input. Same-machine bit identity with deterministic partition and ordered reduction (.ngm SHA-256 and VirtualGeometryData).
  • DAG property tests [CPU] (G4-1): across 10k random views × instance scales {0.5, 1, 4}, projected(parent) ≥ projected(child) on 100% of DAG edges; 1k random cuts with 0 overlapped and 0 uncovered source triangles; 10k random resident page sets where every selected cut is a valid partition.
  • Format & export [CPU]: per-mesh .ngm round trip, truncated/corrupt/version-mismatched pages, root-page pinning, eviction order under a tiny budget. Export-once test: each .ngm appears in the PCK exactly once for ResourceImporterScene, ResourceImporterOBJ and save_to_file meshes and in “selected scenes” mode. Default stripping keeps exported geometry bytes ≤1.6× raw source vertex+index bytes. An encrypted preset without a *.ngm exclusion fails before any byte is written.
  • GPU-backed import/save/bake round trip [C] (mandatory because RendererDummy masks missing GPU buffers): on M5 Max Metal and RTX 4080 SUPER Vulkan, import a virtualized mesh under retain_authored → save/resave .scn/.res (exercising ArrayMesh::_get_surfaces → MeshStorage::mesh_get_surface) → reload → verify non-empty surface_get_arrays(), create_trimesh_shape(), and LightmapGI::bake FLIP ≤0.005 vs non-virtualized, plus default export-time stripping.
  • Invalidation & dispatch contracts [CPU]: per-(instance, surface, view) mixed-material eligibility; transform dirty regions emitted inside RendererSceneCull::_update_instance; light-change dirtying from the old and new influence AABB, with a directional change dirtying every page; SRC light set by bake mode (DYNAMIC relights every scheduled pass, STATIC dirties pages only when its properties change and skips lightmapped receivers, DISABLED is excluded); occluder swept volumes projected along directional lights and intersected with local-light radii; per-instance radiance-page LRU eviction with WRC fallback; dummy RenderingServer/driver fallback behavior.
  • RT equivalence and robustness [V/CPU]: ray-query parity Vulkan vs Metal (Q17), including the edge-straddling set; front-face/FLIP_FACING winding fixture; refit vs rebuild; GPU-written vs CPU-written TLAS; CPU-fed static MultiMesh and non-baked GridMap octants present in the TLAS; compaction equivalence; churn of 10k BLAS through the chunked arena’s free lists; mesh_surface_update_vertex_region updates the CPU-decoded copy and marks the BLAS dirty.
  • Tier selection and safety net [CPU/V]: mocked devices and settings produce each tier reason (§3) once: GTX and RADV emulated RT → NOT_HW_ACCELERATED (off the RT-core allowlist), lavapipe → CPU_DEVICE, Apple7/8 → NOT_HW_ACCELERATED, D3D12 → D3D12_NO_DXR, plus NO_RAY_QUERY, METAL_SYNC_MODE, METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS, SETTING_OFF and AS_MEMORY. A corrupted startup self-test (one triangle, 64 rays) yields SELFTEST_FAILED. A simulated device loss while HWRT is active latches HWRT_FAULTED under user:// until the driver version changes or hardware_ray_tracing = force. quality = auto maps mocked microbenchmark results and GPU classes to High, Medium or the SDFGI fallback and logs the reason; on real hardware the microbenchmark takes ≤5 ms.
  • Velocity [C] (P1b): with NextGI on, the forced prepass writes MOTION_VECTORS for moving and skinned instances, the color pass does not rewrite them, and the velocity buffer matches the NextGI-off render. P1a scenes are static and use camera-only reprojection from depth.
  • Material eligibility [CPU]: each per-(instance, surface) Fallback reason (§6.3) produced once by a minimal fixture, including whole-chain ineligibility for next_pass, material_overlay and stencil chains, BAKED_GRIDMAP, and the RT exclusion reasons NOT_IN_RT_SCENE, RT_INSTANCE_CAP and RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED; editor warning text and runtime counter agree.
  • Upscaler contracts [CPU] (GU-1): enum values and scaling_3d_mode_type classification; each ViewportScaling3DReason produced exactly once; DLSS helper math (mip bias, jitter phases, InMVScale, InJitterOffset); per-view contexts and VIEW_UNSUPPORTED; the exact-equality PRESERVE_WRITTEN test; the extension-provider merge with a mock VulkanHooks; FSR2, MetalFX and TAA helpers unchanged; extension-API validation passes.
  • Velocity under dense upscalers [C]: with NextGI High and the dev DENSE copy upscaler (any Vulkan GPU), and again under DLSS on the RTX 4080 SUPER (GU-4), velocity after the motion pass matches the NextGI-off render within 1e-4 per pixel, and Q21 = 0 except on the reset frame.
  • History reset [C]: Viewport.reset_temporal_history() sets reset for exactly one frame on DLSS, FSR2 and MetalFX.
  • Callback synchronization [V] (GU-2): the C++ dev fixture shows 0 SYNC-HAZARD with stages and ≥1 with stages forced to 0. The NGX output A/B is the GU-2 item.
  • DLSS export [CPU] (GU-6): rel SHA-256 match, signature, NVIDIA notices, refusal of dev-runtime builds, and the script-only project that exports with the DLL.

13.3 GPU test project scenes

A content lane builds each scene in its owner phase, before the gate that uses it. Scenes live in the in-repo godotnext/testbed project (at most 50 MB tracked, with a provenance log); the 10M+ triangle scenes come from seeded generators. The in-engine path tracer (D16; near-unbiased, 64-bounce cap, truncation bias < 1e-5) is the authoritative GI reference. In P0 it traces analytic triangle soups only; from P1a it renders the Godot scenes below through the RT arena and CPU TLAS. LightmapGI is a report-only cross-check: a mismatch above 10% opens an investigation, which may find LightmapGI at fault. NextGeo images compare against the full-resolution forward render. Metric IDs in parentheses (Q1–Q21) refer to §13.4.

Golden-image comparisons report FLIP mean/p99, patch errors and temporal sigma against §2. Cross-API parity is Vulkan (RTX 4080 SUPER) vs Metal (M5 Max) with fixed seeds. D3D12 is checked for fallback correctness only until P6a, except that the DLSS stretch item’s GU-7 compares D3D12 DLSS with Vulkan DLSS.

13.4 Metric definitions

Q1–Q21 are frozen at G0. §2 and every gate cite these Q IDs; milestones keep M0–M4.

IDMetricDefinition
Q1Indirect diffuse patch error64–256 seeded world-space patches (0.25 m discs on surfaces with roughness ≥0.8). Indirect = full render minus a direct-only render at the same exposure. Error = mean |Ltest − Lref| / max(Lref, 0.01 × scene mean indirect). Test value = average of 64 frames after 120 settling frames. Reference: ≥4096 spp, with per-patch relative standard error ≤1%.
Q2Card-hit coverageRay set: 1 seeded ray per half-resolution pixel from the gather origins of 4 fixed cameras. Denominator: rays whose HWRT hit is within 10 m. Numerator: those landing on a valid card texel (resident page, card chosen per D6, coverage mask 1, depth error ≤2 card texels). Counted by a GPU counter.
Q3Leak ratioMean indirect luminance of 16 dark-room wall patches divided by that of 16 lit-room wall patches. Sealed rooms share a 10 cm (variant A) or 20 cm (variant B) wall; the reference must show <0.1%.
Q4aConvergence, step scenesLight toggled, sun jumped 15° and then held, or door teleported from closed to open (1 m), all at frame 0. Lend = post-step static endpoint at frame 240; e(t) = mean |Lt − Lend| / Lend. Gate: e(10) ≤25% and e(32) ≤10%.
Q4bConvergence, continuous scenesDoor opens 0 → 1 m over 2 s; Time of Day sweeps the sun 90° over 10 s. elag(t) = mean |Lt − Lref(t)| / Lref(t), against a path-traced reference rendered at the same animation state (every 4th frame, ≥1024 spp). Gate: mean elag over the motion ≤25%, and e(32) ≤10% measured 32 frames after the motion ends.
Q5Crack pixelsA debug view draws back faces of closed meshes magenta. Crack = a magenta pixel, or a background pixel inside the reference coverage mask eroded by 2 px.
Q6Hole pixelsBackground pixels inside the eroded reference mask during flight.
Q7FLIPLDR FLIP on images tonemapped with the linear tonemapper at fixed exposure; report mean and p99. Fixed seeds in all stochastic passes.
Q8Temporal sigmaPer-patch relative luminance σ over 64 static frames.
Q9Reflection errorReflection-only buffer, per roughness bin; mean radiance error and FLIP.
Q10Ghost trailMean error in disoccluded pixels at frame 4 after a cut or occluder move.
Q11Pass timeRD timestamps per named pass in timing mode: 600 frames after a 120-frame warmup, p95, 3 runs with ≤2% spread. §2.1 line 1b (amortized BLAS builds + compaction copies) is excluded from the steady-state p95 total; frames that spend it must hold p99 ≤ High total + 1b. Clocks locked with nvidia-smi on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine. M5 Max plugged in, High Power mode, thermal state recorded.
Q12Frame time p95/p99Wall clock in timing mode (no movie writer, --disable-vsync).
Q13OverheadQ11 total delta vs baseline over 3 runs, at the stated threshold.
Q14Fallback coverageOpaque pixels drawn by the legacy path for surfaces of virtualized meshes.
Q15Geometry memoryRD memory report categories, including the new AS accounting.
Q16Clamped energy fractionEnergy removed by firefly clamps / total indirect energy; must be ≤2%.
Q17Ray-query parityFixture rays start ≥1e-3 in barycentric distance from any edge, with no ties in t. Instance and primitive IDs exact. Barycentrics within 1e-5 absolute of analytic values (G0) and 1e-4 across APIs; t within 1e-5 relative of analytic values and 1e-4 across APIs. A separate edge-straddling set asserts that exactly one of the adjacent triangles is reported, with no misses and no double hits.
Q18RT coverageFraction of GI-contributing opaque instances within rt_scene_radius that are present in the TLAS, read from the RT-coverage counter. Gate: ≥95% on NextGI Perf (G1, G3).
Q19Upscaler image qualityLDR FLIP (Q7 rules) of the final output against a reference of the same frames at the same output resolution: scaling_3d_mode bilinear, scaling_3d_scale 2.0, 3D MSAA 8× with alpha-to-coverage on alpha-scissor materials; TAA, FSR2 and DLSS off; same seeds and spline. A reference is accepted only if FLIP(MSAA 4× ref, MSAA 8× ref) ≤0.01 per scene; failing content masks are excluded and reported. Moving form: mean over the spline frames after a 120-frame warm-up, excluding the 32 frames after a reset cut; p99 reported. Static form: 64 frames of the static segment after 120 settling frames. Reported per scene, per content mask and per output resolution. Gates use the mean across the gate scenes per output resolution.
Q20Final-image indirect errorQ1’s patch method (and Q9’s roughness bins on Roughness Corridor) applied to the final pre-tonemap linear HDR output at output resolution. Itest = full − direct-only, both through the same upscaler/denoiser path, where direct-only zeroes NextGI’s ambient and reflection outputs. Iref = D16 full − direct at output resolution (≥4096 spp, per-patch relative standard error ≤1%). Error = mean |Itest − Iref| / max(Iref, 0.01 × scene mean indirect), over 64 frames after 120 settling frames. Defined now; first used by P6i.
Q21Invalid motion vectorsPer-frame count of velocity texels that are bit-equal to the pre-clear sentinel (-1.0, -1.0) or have any non-finite component. Read from the velocity texture bound to a dense-velocity temporal upscaler (DLSS; the dev DENSE copy upscaler; MetalFX reported) by a debug pass immediately before dispatch, in capture mode only. Gate: 0 on every frame except a scripted reset frame, which is reported.

14. Estimates and staffing

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

Evidence supports relative sizing. The calendar below is an explicit planning assumption for one lead plus AI agents, not a researched fact, and is re-estimated at every gate.

SizeWorkstreamsWhy
SHistory graft (replace refs); RD ray-query-only TLAS fix; AS address cache; limits/capabilities; tier rule; toolchain check; compute-bind validation; Metal capability detection; Metal heap AS allocation; Metal container AS case; SPIRV-Cross patch; meshoptimizer SCsub; settings; driver-callback stages; Vulkan native interop; DLSS license packet + CI guardLocalized driver fixes or existing in-tree substrate
M EACHBuild modes; storage-buffer BLAS inputs; GPU-buffer TLAS; compaction + scratch ring + accounting; reference path tracer (analytic v0 in P0, scene path in P1a, Metal port in P2); Metal AS objects; Metal AS encoder; harness (two modes); WRC; translucency volume; export plugin; Vulkan extension providers + validation hook; scaling-mode DLSS and fallback; TemporalUpscaler hook + dense velocity; modules/dlss build + mock backend; DLSS export plugin; Upscaler Torture; DLSS on D3D12 (stretch)New component with a bounded contract, usually on one or two drivers
L EACHRT scene/TLAS manager; legacy RT arena + BLAS lifecycle; skinned refit path; NextGI ray-query gather/reflection shaders; SRC with three work streams; denoiser; material-bucket resolve; DAG builder + BVH + .ngm; page cache; hierarchical cull; NGX SR/DLAA effectCross-cutting subsystems with a high regression surface; cost is dominated by GPU debugging, not code volume
L / PROGRAMD3D12 DXR (P6a); SDF software tier (P6b); cluster AS (P6f); VSM; mesh shaders; DLSS Ray Reconstruction (P6i); DLSS Frame Generation and Reflex (P6j)Post-v1: from-scratch driver/toolchain work, a second GI tracer, or vendor-specific tiers
Execution model — a solo lead plus AI agents (D18). Slice 1 (P0–P3, NextGI on legacy meshes) finishes at M3 before Slice 2 runtime work (P4–P5) starts; only the offline DAG prototype runs earlier, in an agent lane. G1a, the P1a quality spike, is the first demonstrable milestone. These are planning assumptions. The bottleneck is the lead’s GPU debugging and review loop, not code volume. Re-estimate at every gate.
PhaseSolo-lead estimateMilestone
P0 — bring-up, harness, RT substrate, reference v08–12 weeksM0, about month 2–3
P1a — narrow quality spike4–6 weeksG1a demo, about month 3–4 (first demonstrable milestone)
P1b — production Vulkan HWRT diffuse; also takes harness timing-mode polish and the full debug overlay from P0about 3–4 monthsM1, about month 6–8
P2 — Metal HWRT6–10 weeks of work: the driver lane (items 1–6, G2-1 parity) starts right after G0, in parallel with P1; item 5’s refit waits for the P1b build modes (about month 3–4); the NextGI items wait for G1Local ray query on the M5 Max about month 3–4; M2 about month 6–9
PU — DLSS SR/DLAA (PU-core after G0 in rhi-vulkan; the rest after G1 in upscalers)about 3–4 months after G1GU, about month 9–12. No M3 or M4 gate depends on PU, so their calendars are unchanged
P3 — reflections, denoiser, dynamic actors, translucencyabout 6–10 monthsM3 = NextGI v1, about month 12–18
P4 — NextGeo offline, assets, spikesabout 3–5 monthsG4 (no milestone)
P5 — NextGeo GPU draw and v1 integrationabout 5–7 monthsM4 = v1, about month 20–30
  • Agent lanes (all under single-lead review): (1) harness, content (including the NextGI Perf timing scene) and the reference path tracer; (2) RD and the Vulkan RT substrate; (3) the Metal RT driver, from right after G0, so the M5 Max runs ray query locally by about month 3–4; (4) NextGI solver and denoiser; (5) NextGeo offline DAG and export, from P4, with only a prototype from P2; (6) upscalers: DLSS after G1 (PU), then P6i and P6j. PU-core runs in lane (2), rhi-vulkan. Re-creating the fork on upstream history instead of the replace-ref graft needs a force-push, so it is the lead’s explicit action, not agent work.
  • PU lead review (planning assumption, unverified): about 1 lead-week for PU-core, spread over months 3–8, and about 4–6 lead-weeks for the module, export and GU runs over months 8–12. If this competes with the P3 critical path, PU waits; M3 never waits for PU.
  • Early gates exclude D3D12 HWRT (P6a), the Linux VM lane and per-phase Metal parity. Metal parity is first gated by G2-1, which belongs to the Metal driver lane that starts right after G0.
  • Hardware: an RTX 3060 12 GB is bought at G0 (or a second PCIe slot or another host is confirmed for it). G1-3 adds the mid-range diffuse-path line, G1’s per-pass Ada:3060 ratios replace the 2.5× factor, and G3 and G5 gate mid-range lines on it. Validated: Ada on Windows (RTX 4080 SUPER) and Apple10 (M5 Max). Enabled but unvalidated: Turing, Ampere (until the RTX 3060 arrives), Blackwell, all Linux drivers, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Arc, and Apple M3/M4. An optional P3 task runs G3 on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine booted into Linux (NVIDIA proprietary driver); only a pass moves Linux/NVIDIA to validated. The RTX 4080 SUPER and RTX 3060 also gate the DLSS U1 lines and the GU-2 soak. There is no RTX 50 (MFG) and no NVIDIA Linux machine besides that optional boot, so DLSS on those stays enabled but unvalidated unless the boot also runs GU-2 and GU-5.
  • Estimate multipliers: each RenderingDevice feature repeats across drivers; each new view (shadow, reflection, capture) repeats cluster-cut or proxy policy; each material feature expands eligibility, resolve, shadow, GI-cache, and RT representations simultaneously.
  • Rollback: every subsystem ships behind project/Environment gates defaulting off until its numeric gate passes. Default retain_authored keeps full authored ArrayMesh surfaces and GPU readback intact in the project, so disabling NextGeo (or shipping fallback tiers) restores exact legacy rendering and baking with zero reimport; a stripped export needs only a re-export without the nextgeo tag. nextgi/enabled defaults to false, so projects without NextGI pay no RT-arena memory. modules/dlss is off by default and absent from official builds. Removing it, or the NVIDIA runtime, falls back to FSR2 with no project change.

Cut list

Schedule slips consume the cut list in order. Gate failures go through that phase’s outcome rows in §12; a cut applies to a gate failure only if it removes the failing workload.

#CutConsequence
1GPU-written TLAS for particles and dynamic MultiMeshThey become receivers only, with the per-instance reason RT_DYNAMIC_EXCLUDED. Static MultiMesh and non-baked GridMap octants stay in the CPU-fed TLAS (P1b)
2Epic presetquality becomes auto | medium | high; it also removes the precondition for post-v1 DLSS-RR (P6i)
3Translucency volumeTransparents use WRC irradiance at the object centre plus a ReflectionProbe
4Skinned BLAS refitSkinned meshes are receivers only; the G3-4 occlusion item is dropped and documented
5Slice 2 in v1M3 ships as v1 and NextGeo becomes v1.1; applies if M3 is projected after month 18

Cuts allowed at any time, outside the gates

  • The Linux VM lane.
  • Encrypted .ngm support (the hard error stays).
  • The import-time strip_authored_to_coarse option (export stripping stays).
  • ShaderMaterial eligibility in the resolve.
  • DLSS on D3D12 (pu-dlss-d3d12): d3d12 projects keep FSR2 with API_UNSUPPORTED.
  • DLSS SR/DLAA from a milestone’s claim (the lead withdraws it in Appendix A; core MIT hooks stay).

Never cut

  • The reference path tracer.
  • The §13.4 metrics.
  • Tier fallback.
  • The 0-validation-error gates. DLSS-on runs are counted only in GU-2 (and, post-v1, the P6i/P6j gates), under the object-attributed NGX rule. Every other gate’s [V] items run DLSS-off at 0 errors.
  • The upscaler fallback chain (a requested DLSS mode that cannot run always resolves to MetalFX temporal or FSR2, or to the bilinear rule on Mobile and Compatibility, with a logged reason).
  • The NextGI-off regression gate.
  • The clean-room controls (including the proprietary-SDK file guard).

16. Alternatives rejected

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.
AlternativeDecisionReason
Copy/port Lumen or Nanite sourceREJECTProject policy: treat Unreal source and shaders as forbidden implementation inputs. Study public documentation/papers for a clean-room design; do not lift code, shader text, constants, or file structure. Confirm current license terms with counsel before relying on any broader permission.
Vendor NVIDIA RTXGI/DDGI codeREJECTInspected license is proprietary NVIDIA RTX SDK terms, incompatible with open GodotNext redistribution. Reimplement probe algorithms from cited papers if needed.
Vendor NVIDIA NRD/RTXDI codeHOLDLicense terms to be checked before anyone reads the source (unverified). Use the papers as references.
Vendor AMD Brixelizer GI wholesaleHOLDGPUOpen page states MIT, but repository license file has a binary-only default with per-file exceptions. Treat as clean-room reference until a file-by-file audit proves reusable parts.
SDF-first v1 (the v2.2 plan)REJECTUser decision of 2026-11-01 and the UE 5.8 model: HWRT is the v1 tracer wherever the §3 tier rule passes (D4, D11). The SDF+SRC+WRC tier moves to P6b as the fallback for non-RT GPUs; v1 falls back to existing SDFGI.
D3D12 DXR in v1DEFER TO P6aDepends on unmerged PR #118654 and a forked Mesa patch. Vulkan covers NVIDIA, AMD and Intel RT GPUs on Windows, and new GodotNext projects default to it; d3d12 projects get SDFGI until P6a. Pulled in only if upstream merges both during v1 (D12).
Silent d3d12 → vulkan switch for existing projectsREJECTIt would drop HDR output and DComp transparency without the user choosing to, and UE also selects the tier within the active RHI. Instead, new GodotNext projects default to Vulkan with that loss stated, and turning on NextGI in a d3d12 project offers the switch in a one-time modal (D12).
RT pipelines with closest-hit materialsDEFERRay query in compute (D5) avoids SBT and material-as-hit-shader problems. Revisit for hit lighting (P6d) or for Intel Arc’s Thread Sorting Unit.
NVIDIA cluster AS (Mega Geometry) for full-detail NextGeo RT in v1DEFER TO P6fVendor-optional: NVIDIA-only on Vulkan and preview-only on DXR (SM 6.10). UE 5.8 mainline traces fallback meshes; v1 traces fixed proxy BLASes (D13).
MoltenVK/KosmicKrisp for Mac RTREJECTNeither implements ray tracing. Mac HWRT uses the native Metal driver (P2).
Mesh shaders firstDEFERNo TASK/MESH stages, containers, translators, or vertex-less pipelines exist. Compute culling plus one indirect instanced draw (D2 path (c)) reaches the NextGeo gates without a three-driver toolchain program. Mesh shaders are P6h.
Hand-written StandardMaterial3D record interpreter as the Stage B resolve (v2.2 D7)REJECTCannot match Forward+ features; replaced by the ShaderCompiler material-bucket resolve (D7).
Option A bindless in v1DEFERNot needed with the material-bucket resolve; would require per-element descriptor writes and a texture_replace_rid hook.
New fourth rendering method firstDEFERRenderer selection and shader variants are hardcoded around current methods. Prove passes inside Forward+; extract a sibling renderer only if contracts stabilize.
Probes-only NextGIINSUFFICIENTProbe grids are low-frequency diffuse systems with leak/latency limits. The WRC (D15) is a probe radiance cache for far rays and misses, not the near-field or glossy solver.
Use an offline path tracer as the game rendererOUT OF SCOPEProgressive path tracing can supply bakes/references; it lacks the game-oriented temporal/resampling, dynamic-scene, and latency contracts required here without a separate real-time design such as the inspected Solari approach. The in-engine reference path tracer (D16) is a validation tool only.
Wait for an upstream SDFGI successorDO NOT BLOCKNo merged successor exists in this snapshot. Design NextGI to reuse whichever cascade improvements later land, but do not make an external merge the critical path.
Streamline for DLSS SR/DLAA in v1DEFER TO P6j / OUTCOME ROWIt keeps engine binaries free of NVIDIA object code (MIT headers; NVIDIA DLLs loaded at run time), which could allow DLSS-capable official binaries. But it is Windows-only and needs the interposer or manual hooking of present/acquire, with slInit before any Vulkan call. Reconsidered with FG in P6j, or if NVIDIA declines NGX-linked official builds (GU outcome row).
Vendor the NGX SDK (headers, libs, DLLs)REJECTLicenseRef-NvidiaProprietary headers; license §4(b), §4(e).
Clean-room NGX declarations written from the PDF guide, so official builds need no SDKREJECTRe-deriving a proprietary ABI from NVIDIA’s documentation is legally unclear and fragile. The mock backend covers CI instead.
DLSS as a GDExtension in v1DEFER (post-v1)driver_callback_add is not script-bound, nothing runs before vkCreateInstance, and the upscaler slot is fixed. Revisit once the D20 hooks are script-bound.
Ship NVIDIA DLLs in default export templatesREJECTThe license and marks duties fall on whoever distributes; the developer supplies the DLLs.
DLSS-RR as a replacement for the specular denoiser only (v2.3 P6f)REJECTRR replaces denoise and upscale for the whole colour signal (RR guide §3.2).
DLSS-RR as an opt-in v1 mode in P3 (v2.4 draft)DEFER TO P6iGuide and bypass churn in P3 critical-path shaders; correlated sampling; mid-range cost. P3 keeps only the default-off hooks (D21).
DLSS-RR as the only denoiser (Bevy Solari model)REJECTVendor-locked, with no fallback quality on non-RTX hardware. D8 stays the default.
DLSS gating M3 (G3 depends on GU)REJECTDLSS quality depends on NVIDIA’s model and lead time. GU is non-blocking, with a milestone-move rule.
Reflex via VK_NV_low_latency2 in v1DEFER TO P6jIts path (Streamline Reflex or Khronos) depends on the FG decision; DLSS-G rejects non-Streamline Reflex.
Study NVIDIA-RTX/godot (MIT)ALLOWED AS A STUDY INPUTAfter its provenance is recorded. Do not copy its thirdparty/streamline/include (it contains sl_nvperf.h), nvapi_minimal.h without provenance, or its hard-coded application ID.

17. First backlog — P0, P1a and the PU license packet

v3.0: §21 (UE 5.8 parity program) governs where this section conflicts with it. The user directive of 2026-11-02 makes full UE 5.8 rendering parity the v1 scope.

Items 1–12 form P0 (8–12 weeks; gate G0; M0 at about month 2–3). Item 13 is the P1a spike (4–6 weeks; gate G1a, the first demonstrable milestone, at about month 3–4). Item 14 holds both reviews. Item 15, pu-dlss-license-packet, can start now. PU-core starts after G0 in the rhi-vulkan lane. The rest of PU starts after G1 in the upscalers lane (§12 PU). Item 16 is post-v1. Agent lanes may run items in parallel; the lead reviews every item. Right after G0, the Metal driver lane (P2 items 1–6 plus the G2-1 parity gate) starts in parallel with P1, so the M5 Max runs ray query locally by about month 3–4; P2’s NextGI items still wait for G1.

  1. [P0] History graft, merge cadence, fork CI trim: a local graft does not travel to clones, and 95c0fa5 is not in the local object store, so fetch upstream, run git replace --graft 7cfeb47 95c0fa5, push refs/replace/* and document git fetch origin 'refs/replace/*:refs/replace/*'. Re-creating the fork on upstream history instead needs a force-push, so it is the lead’s explicit action, not agent work. G0-5 runs git merge-base HEAD upstream/master from a fresh clone. Merge each upstream 4.x stable tag; NextGI/NextGeo code lives in new files behind named hooks, with changed upstream lines tracked at each gate. Narrow .github/workflows/runner.yml to Linux/Windows/macOS editor+template builds and static checks, guard the missing HEAD~1 in .github/workflows/static_checks.yml, and keep the self-hosted GPU runs off public PR triggers.
  2. [P0] Builds and fleet capture: dev_mode=yes editor builds on the Apple M5 Max (macOS arm64), RTX 4080 SUPER machine (Windows, vulkan and d3d12; MSVC Build Tools, SCons, Vulkan SDK with validation layers) and Linux VM (linuxbsd). spirv-val comes from the Vulkan SDK and is a dev dependency on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine and the M5 Max, not vendored. Archive vulkaninfo JSON for the RTX 4080 SUPER (device 1), device 0 (Intel UHD 770) and the Linux VM, recording the AS and ray-query extensions, VkPhysicalDeviceAccelerationStructurePropertiesKHR limits and the AS vertex-format bit for R16G16B16A16_UNORM. Buy the RTX 3060 12 GB at G0, or confirm a second PCIe slot in the RTX 4080 SUPER machine or another host for it.
  3. [P0] Harness, two modes: capture mode (--write-movie --fixed-fps, fixed seeds) for image, error and convergence gates; timing mode (no movie writer, --disable-vsync, locked clocks, RD timestamps per named pass, wall-clock frame times, IO delays injected in wall time) for every ms gate. Session-1 runner on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine with --gpu-index, plus a local Metal runner on the M5 Max. P0 needs timing mode only as far as G0-4 and the AS calibration runs; timing-mode polish is P1b. G0-4: 3 runs × 600 frames within 2% p95 spread; capture output byte-identical across 2 runs.
  4. [P0] RD/Vulkan RT substrate (all size S): load vkGetAccelerationStructureDeviceAddressKHR and cache each AS address at creation; accept hit_sbt_range 0 in tlas_build for ray-query-only use; expose AS limits; SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY also requires bufferDeviceAddress, and each driver returns a reason code with it (D3D12_NO_DXR on D3D12, otherwise NO_RAY_QUERY); new SUPPORTS_RAY_QUERY_HARDWARE from driver knowledge only (on Vulkan, a PCI vendor/device-ID allowlist of RT-core architectures: NVIDIA Turing+ RTX, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Xe-HPG/Xe2 Arc); AS-invalidated check in compute_list_bind_uniform_set; clean raytracing_pipeline_create failure without SUPPORTS_RAYTRACING_PIPELINE; storage-buffer inputs to blas_create with usage-bit validation; a shared transient scratch ring in place of per-AS persistent scratch; AS storage counted in buffer_memory and the memory reports. Build modes (BUILD/UPDATE) and compaction are P1b.
  5. [P0] Tier selection: the rendering/global_illumination/nextgi/* settings (enabled, hardware_ray_tracing, quality = auto | medium | high | epic, rt_scene_radius, rt_max_error); the §3 rule, which alone holds the CPU device-type check; the tier reason enum (NO_RAY_QUERY, NOT_HW_ACCELERATED, CPU_DEVICE, D3D12_NO_DXR, METAL_SYNC_MODE, METAL_NO_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS, SETTING_OFF, AS_MEMORY, SELFTEST_FAILED, HWRT_FAULTED); the one-time log line and SDFGI fallback wiring. The full debug overlay is P1b. Ray-query ShaderRDs are initialized only while the HWRT tier is active; their versions are non-embedded (or filtered in _customize_shader_version for containers without acceleration-structure support) and baked only for Vulkan. Gate G0-2 checks every fleet configuration.
  6. [P0] Windows driver default: GodotNext’s EditorNode::get_initial_settings writes rendering/rendering_device/driver.windows = "vulkan" into new projects, giving up HDR output and DComp transparency on Windows unless a project switches back. On an existing d3d12 project, turning on nextgi/enabled shows a one-time modal that offers the Vulkan switch, keeps fallback_to_d3d12 = true, states the HDR/DComp loss and offers a restart; the fallback reason is still logged once.
  7. [P0] Ray-query toolchain check: #version 460 + GL_EXT_ray_query compute through ShaderRD; spirv-val 0 errors on every ray-query module; NVIDIA driver 621.01 accepts SPIR-V 1.3 modules using SPV_KHR_ray_query. If 1.3 is rejected, add the per-shader 1.4 opt-in (RD parameter plus ShaderRD cache key) before G0 closes, about +1–2 weeks.
  8. [P0] RD smoke test and AS calibration: a testbed GDScript on a local RenderingDevice with one-triangle and 4096-ray fixtures, reading back t, instance custom index, primitive, barycentrics and front face. Fixture rays sit ≥1e-3 in barycentric distance from any edge with no ties in t; a separate edge-straddling set covers shared edges. G0-1 on the RTX 4080 SUPER (--rendering-driver vulkan --gpu-index 1 --gpu-validation --gpu-abort, NV_ALLOW_RAYTRACING_VALIDATION=1, synchronization validation): 0 validation errors, IDs exact on 100% of rays, barycentrics within 1e-5 absolute and t within 1e-5 relative of the analytic values, and exactly one adjacent triangle reported for each edge-straddling ray (no misses, no double hits). Calibration microbenchmarks on the same GPU: TLAS build at 10k, 50k and 100k instances, refit throughput, and BLAS build throughput with PREFER_FAST_TRACE vs PREFER_FAST_BUILD. They set the §2.1 line 1b per-frame triangle cap (provisional ≤250k).
  9. [P0] Reference path tracer v0 (D16) on Vulkan: an analytic, near-unbiased (64-bounce cap, truncation bias < 1e-5) path tracer using compute ray query over a TLAS that the testbed GDScript builds from known triangle soups: the furnace box, sealed box A (10 cm wall) and sealed box B (20 cm wall). Each test passes in its material table, using the constant-parameter StandardMaterial3D BRDF, Godot light units and linear HDR. It does not depend on P1 infrastructure. G0-3 closes on the analytic tests only: in the furnace (a closed box with Lambertian inner faces, ρ = 0.8, uniform emission Le on every inner face and no other lights) every pixel reads Le/(1−ρ) within 1%; sealed boxes A and B and bit-identical same-seed runs complete the gate.
  10. [P0] Content: the in-repo godotnext/testbed project (≤50 MB) with a provenance log; Color Box Room variants A (10 cm wall), B (20 cm wall), off-screen light and a single mesh ≥40 m in extent with 10 cm interior walls; Concave Courtyard; constant-parameter materials; seeded generator scaffolding for the 10M+ triangle scenes. The seeded NextGI Perf timing scene follows in P1 (content lane).
  11. [P0] Metrics frozen: the §13.4 metric definitions Q1–Q21 are frozen at G0 (Q19–Q21 added in v2.4).
  12. [P0] License & clean-room packet: meshoptimizer NOTICE/source list; Brixelizer per-file license audit; NvRTX, NVIDIA’s DLSS sample source and the UE DLSS plugin source listed as forbidden inputs; NRD/RTXDI license check before anyone reads the source; provenance record (upstream URL, commit, license, modifications) for the SPIRV-Cross cherry-pick; commit the clean-room contributor rules. The DLSS record lives in pu-dlss-license-packet; clean-room.md links it.
  13. [P1a] Narrow HWRT diffuse spike: RT arena (256 MiB chunks with free-list allocation; chunks never move) holding CPU-decoded float32/uint32 copies (D13; eps_rt = min(max_extent/256, rt_max_error)), static BLASes and a CPU-fed TLAS over static INSTANCE_MESH instances; the reference path tracer’s scene path, rendering Color Box Room and Concave Courtyard through that arena and TLAS for G1a; Stage-1 cards (≤12 normal-clustered per mesh) captured once with RenderForwardClustered::_render_material, outside the shadow batch; world-space direct relight with shadow rays (D14, §8.3) within the High caps of ≤256k relit texels and ≤256k shadow rays per frame; HWRT diffuse gather at 1/16 rpp with hits shaded from the SRC by hit identity (D6), sky on a miss, temporal accumulation with camera-only reprojection from depth (static scenes only; prepass velocity is P1b). The LightmapGI cross-check (16 bounces, ULTRA, denoiser off, texture_for_bounces off) is report-only: a difference above 10% opens an investigation, which may conclude LightmapGI is the wrong side. Deliberately absent: WRC, per-instance LRU, dynamic invalidation. G1a [C], quality only: patch error ≤20% (Q1), card-hit coverage ≥85% (Q2), leak ≤1% (Q3) at 10 cm and 20 cm, on all variants including the ≥40 m mesh; cost recorded against the 3.1 ms Ada sanity bound.
  14. [P0 / P1a] G0 and G1a reviews: the G0 review closes P0, declares M0 and applies the G0 outcome rows (SPIR-V 1.4 opt-in; a failing analytic reference test keeps G0 open). The G1a review applies the P1a outcome rows (more cards or an interior split for coverage, origin offset and eps_rt for leaks, the path-traced-hit diagnostic for error) before P1b starts; still failing after two iterations triggers a §2/§3 scope review, never a silent relaxation. The calibration runs are the AS lines’ first measurement and G1a the diffuse lines’; each may reset its ceilings once, logged with the data in Appendix A, and later measurements may only tighten them.
  15. [now, PU] DLSS license packet: record the NVIDIA RTX SDK terms in §19 and the source log; land the proprietary-file CI guard (check_proprietary_sdk_files.py in static_checks.yml, plus .gitignore); pin the SDK tag, the rel SHA-256s and the guide revision; draft the five NVIDIA questions for the lead. No SDK header is read for implementation before it closes.
  16. [post-v1] Per-view reprojection in MotionVectorsStore, plus a stereo fixture, to enable DLSS multiview. Candidate upstream report for MetalFX multiview.

v2.2→v2.3 (phase map in §12): v2.2’s first spike is now P0 plus P1a, and its HWRT-vs-SDF comparator is gone because HWRT is the v1 tracer (D4). v2.2’s NextGeo spike items left the first backlog. The draw-identity benchmark and the DAG spike are P4 scope; the Vulkan 1.2 drawIndirectCount consolidation happens only if draw path (a) wins at G4-5. The Option A/B binding benchmark is replaced by the P4 material-bucket resolve prototype (D7), and the visibility-format question by the G4-6 resolve gate, whose failure row is a §2/§3 scope review with its own estimate; the software micro-raster is P6h. Denoiser guides are P3 scope; Slice 1 velocity comes from the P1b prepass, and Slice 2’s per-upscaler velocity and clear rules are P5 scope.

First code rule: every spike lands with its measurement scene and a short result note. A renderer subsystem without a reproducible capture does not advance a phase gate.

18. Open questions and unresolved evidence

None of these blocks P0. The plan proceeds on each lead-decision default below until the lead answers otherwise. Evidence questions are tagged with the phase or gate that answers them.

18.1 Decisions for the lead

QuestionDefault the plan assumes
v3.0 hardware purchases. v3.0 Windows default RHI. UE 5.8 is DX12-first.New projects switch to D3D12 when gate GD passes (pd-windows-dx12-default); Vulkan is the interim default until then and stays a supported RT path.

DXR is P6a, first after v1, and is pulled into v1 only if upstream merges both pieces during the v1 window.
Should new GodotNext projects default to Vulkan on Windows? Godot 4.6+ writes driver.windows = "d3d12" into new projects from EditorNode::get_initial_settings. Listed because the answer reverses Godot’s own default.Yes, in P0: new projects get rendering/rendering_device/driver.windows = "vulkan". Upstreaming? Will any of this work be offered to upstream Godot?No upstream PRs during v1. RD and driver RT fixes stay in isolated commits, so they can later be submitted as human-authored, AI-disclosed PRs under Godot’s policy; two earlier ray-query-only TLAS fixes were closed under it (§19).
Distribution of DLSS-enabled binaries? A build that links NGX contains NVIDIA object code. Until NVIDIA answers in writing and counsel signs off, official binaries stay 100% MIT and DLSS-free, DLSS exists only in developer-built editors and templates, and the Linux VM runs only default and mock builds.
Should DLSS SR/DLAA stay non-blocking? Gate GU never blocks M3 or M4; DLSS joins the first milestone signed off after GU passes (about month 9–12). The alternative is to require it for M3 by making G3 depend on GU.Non-blocking. The module work starts after G1, so it stays out of the months 3–8 review window.
DLSS SR/DLAA on the Direct3D 12 driver in v1? It needs no DXR, but it does need barrier-path handling and cached-state invalidation in the D3D12 driver, and new GodotNext projects default to Vulkan.A v1 stretch item after GU (pu-dlss-d3d12) that implements only the barrier path the RTX 4080 SUPER machine reports and is cut first if the schedule slips. Until it lands, d3d12 projects get FSR2 with API_UNSUPPORTED.
When should DLSS Ray Reconstruction ship? Option A: post-v1 (P6i), with only default-off MIT hooks added in P3. Option B: an opt-in, experimental, Epic-only RTX mode inside P3 behind a non-blocking gate, which adds RR guide and bypass work to P3’s critical-path shaders during the lead’s busiest review window.Option A, post-v1 (P6i). The RR design (D21: replace line 8 and the upscaler, keep line 7, add guides) and its gate minimums are fixed now. NextGI’s own D8 denoiser stays the default and the fallback.
