18.3 External evidence gaps

  • Epic’s Nanite SIGGRAPH deep-dive slides and the separate Lumen blog were not inspected; the Lumen SIGGRAPH 2022 and MegaLights 2025 talks were. The remaining material is read and logged under §19’s clean-room rules before P4 freezes NextGeo contracts.
  • Lumen world-radiance-cache internals were not detailed on the inspected documentation page; NextGI specifies its own WRC (D15) from convergent public literature, not a copied layout.
  • Original NVIDIA RTXGI product page redirects; GitHub SDK docs/license were inspected instead.
  • UE 5.8 sources conflict on Vulkan RT status: the 5.5 release notes say default with DX12 parity, while the 5.8 HWRT page says experimental.
  • NVIDIA’s answers to the five license questions (§19(j)) are pending.
  • The RTX UI Developer Guidelines’ sharpness-slider recommendation has no v1 implementation; DLSS sharpening is deprecated (DLSS guide §3.11).

19. Legal, naming, and clean-room protocol

This section is an engineering control, not legal advice. Obtain counsel before a commercial release or before accepting code from anyone with access to proprietary engine source.

  1. Names: ship systems as NextGI/NextGeo. Use “Lumen-class”/“Nanite-class” only as descriptive comparisons in internal planning; do not imply Epic affiliation or compatibility.
  2. Project-approved study inputs: public papers, official public documentation, conference talks, observed editor behavior/debug views, and permissively licensed source whose license has been recorded. This includes public UE 5.8 documentation and Epic/SIGGRAPH talks (Lumen 2022, MegaLights 2025), and Khronos, Apple and Microsoft specifications and samples whose licenses are recorded.
  3. Forbidden inputs: Unreal Engine source/shaders, NVIDIA’s NvRTX Unreal Engine branch (it is Unreal source), any UE plugin source (including NVIDIA’s UE DLSS plugin), NVIDIA’s DLSS sample/Donut source (separate pre-release license), any NVIDIA or other proprietary SDK source under terms incompatible with MIT (§19(c) lists the only NGX exceptions), copied constants/tables, decompiled binaries, and code generated by prompting a model to reproduce such source.
  4. Separation: contributors who have recently read relevant proprietary source should write behavior/spec notes from public sources, not implementation code for the same subsystem. Keep a source log per subsystem.
  5. Vendoring gate: every third-party file gets upstream URL, version/commit, license text, modifications, and NOTICE placement under Godot’s existing third-party conventions before merge. This covers the SPIRV-Cross df0828ca cherry-pick and, at P6a, the Skyth Mesa patch and the godot-nir-static fork: each gets upstream URL, commit, license (MIT for the Mesa patch and fork; Apache-2.0 or MIT for SPIRV-Cross, as COPYRIGHT.txt records) and modification records. NRD and RTXDI source is not read until their license is recorded (terms unverified). The DLSS SDK is never vendored. It stays a developer-supplied, pinned external path.
  6. Proprietary runtime SDKs (NVIDIA DLSS/NGX, v2.4): clauses (a)–(k) below are cited as §19(a)–(k).
    1. Governing terms: the NVIDIA RTX SDKs License (v. March 14, 2024) with the RTX Supplement (LICENSE.txt). NGX headers are SPDX LicenseRef-NvidiaProprietary.
    2. Nothing from the SDK is committed to the repository, testbed, CI caches or official artifacts: no headers, libraries, DLLs or .so files, samples, or documentation text. check_proprietary_sdk_files.py enforces this for SDK headers, libraries and runtimes; documentation text and samples are kept out by review and the source log.
    3. Allowed inputs: modules/dlss is MIT source written from NVIDIA’s public programming guides. It includes the SDK headers from dlss_sdk_path at build time. The headers and public guides are allowed inputs for writing SDK calls, once this clause is recorded. NVIDIA-RTX/godot (MIT) is a study input once its provenance is recorded, without its thirdparty/streamline/include and without unprovenanced files such as nvapi_minimal.h.
    4. Builds with dlss_backend=ngx contain NVIDIA object code. Distributing them requires:
      • §2(c): terms at least as protective for the NVIDIA portions, and protection of users’ privacy, security and legal rights;
      • §2(d): notifying NVIDIA of known non-compliant distribution;
      • §4(b): no stand-alone redistribution;
      • §4(e): never making the SDK subject to an OSS license. GodotNext’s MIT license covers GodotNext code only.
      Counsel confirms this mixed-terms model. Official GodotNext binaries exclude the module until NVIDIA confirms in writing and counsel signs off.
    5. Runtime binaries: rel DLLs only, unmodified, with the signature intact; dev DLLs never ship.
    6. Duties of anyone distributing DLSS-enabled games or editors, documented in docs/godotnext/dlss.md and shown by the exporter:
      • Supplement §1: NVIDIA GPUs only;
      • §3: cloud deployment only on NVIDIA vGPU systems;
      • §4: notify NVIDIA before commercial release;
      • §6.1: OTA consent;
      • §7.1(b), binding: attribution and NVIDIA Marks on splash, about box and credits;
      • §7.2(c): mark use submitted for approval two weeks ahead;
      • §2(c) EULA terms;
      • the guide §9.5 notices.
    7. GodotNext’s own testing: NGX-backend builds run only on local hardware the project controls (the RTX 4080 SUPER machine, including WSL2). The Linux VM (a cloud VM) runs only default and mock builds until NVIDIA answers question 5.
    8. Branding: GodotNext ships no NVIDIA logos. “NVIDIA DLSS” and “DLAA” appear only as plain nominative references. The About line in DLSS-enabled editors needs NVIDIA’s §7.2(c) approval before any such editor is distributed.
    9. Streamline (MIT except sl_nvperf.h/.dll, Nsight Perf SDK license) is not vendored in v1. If P6j adopts it, only MIT files go through the vendoring gate, and sl_nvperf.* never.
    10. The lead sends five questions to nvidia-rtx-license-questions@nvidia.com and records the answers in docs/godotnext/dlss-license.md:
      1. Is MIT source that #includes, but does not contain, NGX headers acceptable in a public repository?
      2. May GodotNext publish editor and template binaries with NGX linked, and on what terms?
      3. Is the public lib/…/rel/nvngx_dlssd.dll redistributable (RR guide §4.2)?
      4. Will NVIDIA assign an engine type or application ID, or should each game use its own Project ID with ENGINE_TYPE_CUSTOM?
      5. May we build and run DLSS-enabled test binaries on a cloud VM we control (GCP), given Supplement §3?
    11. Source-log disclosure: on 2026-11-02 the v2.4 research step downloaded and read the following into the session scratchpad (/private/tmp/claude-501/.../scratchpad), outside the repository, before this clause existed:
      • the complete NGX headers (dlss_hdr/: nvsdk_ngx.h, nvsdk_ngx_defs.h, nvsdk_ngx_vk.h, nvsdk_ngx_helpers_vk.h, nvsdk_ngx_defs_dlssd.h, nvsdk_ngx_helpers_dlssd_vk.h, nvsdk_ngx_defs_dlssg.h, nvsdk_ngx_params_dlssg.h, nvsdk_ngx_helpers_dlssg_vk.h, nvsdk_ngx_loader.h) and DLSS_LICENSE.txt;
      • the full DLSS, DLSS-RR, DLSS-FG and RTX UI guides as PDF and text (dlss_dl/, dlss_guide_dl/, dlss_rr_dl/, dl_dlssguide/, dl_rrguide/, dlss_txt/, dlssguide.txt, rrguide.txt);
      • Streamline docs and headers (sl_dl/);
      • source copies from NVIDIA-RTX/godot (nvgodot/*.nv, including dlss.cpp, streamline_context.cpp and the driver patches, plus nvapi_minimal.h).
      None is committed, and the event is recorded in the DLSS source log.
  7. Asset gate: test scenes use original or clearly permissively licensed content with recorded provenance. No marketplace/demo assets from proprietary engines. The skinned characters in Character Walk and NextGI Perf must be CC0 or original, with provenance recorded; no Mixamo or marketplace assets.
  8. Trademark gate: GodotNext remains clearly unofficial in repository description and release notes. Do not use Godot or Epic logos in a way that implies endorsement. NVIDIA trademarks follow the proprietary-runtime clause (h).
  9. Upstreaming: no upstream Godot PRs during v1 unless the lead decides otherwise. RD and driver RT fixes stay as isolated commits. Any upstream PR must be human-authored with AI use disclosed, per Godot policy; earlier ray-query-only TLAS fixes (godotengine/godot#123093 and #123095) were closed under it.
  10. Patent step: clean-room controls address copyright, not patents. Before any public release (not before P0), obtain a patent review of the virtual-geometry and GI techniques as implemented.

20.2 External primary sources inspected

Unreal Engine 5.8 documentation and Epic talks

  • UE 5.8 — Hardware Ray Tracing: HWRT used where the video card, RHI and OS support it; Vulkan RT labelled experimental; Nanite traced through its fallback mesh; skinned meshes traced.
  • UE — Hardware and software specifications: Lumen HWRT on NVIDIA RTX 2000+, AMD RX 6000+ and Intel Arc A-series; DX12 on Windows, Vulkan RT experimental.
  • Epic — Lumen Technical Details: screen traces first; SWRT mesh/global SDF; HWRT triangles; Surface Cache cards and limits.
  • Epic — Lumen Performance Guide: 4 ms (60 fps) and 8 ms (30 fps) budgets at 1080p on consoles; TLAS rebuilt every frame; under 100,000 RT instances after culling.
  • UE — macOS development requirements: Lumen HWRT and MegaLights on Apple Silicon M2+ as experimental; macOS 14.5 minimum; M3 recommended.
  • UE — Linux development requirements: Vulkan RHI with NVIDIA 570+ or RADV 24.2.8+; HWRT needs SM6 and RTX 2000 / RX 6000 / Arc A-series.
  • Epic — Nanite Virtualized Geometry: hierarchical clusters, crack-free runtime cut, fine-grained streaming/compression; the fallback mesh is the default RT representation and native Nanite RT is experimental.
  • UE 5.8 release notes: MegaLights production-ready and Lumen Lite in beta; no headline HWRT, cluster-BLAS or Mega Geometry items.
  • UE 5.5 release notes: Vulkan ray tracing enabled by default at DX12 parity, Linux included. This conflicts with the 5.8 HWRT page (§18.3).
  • Lumen, SIGGRAPH 2022 Advances: surface-cache HWRT through inline ray query; Matrix Awakens reflections 2.44 ms with the surface cache vs 11.54 ms with hit lighting on PS5; heavy-overlap SWRT vs HWRT gather 0.94 vs 10.03 ms; High and Epic ray rates.
  • MegaLights, SIGGRAPH 2025 Advances: HWRT as the main visibility method with inline ray tracing by default, near-field proxy meshes and a separate far-field TLAS.
  • Epic forum — Nanite RT maturity: Nanite ray tracing “isn’t considered game ready in 5.8”.

Graphics API specifications

  • VK_KHR_ray_query: requires VK_KHR_acceleration_structure plus VK_KHR_spirv_1_4 or Vulkan 1.2; uses SPV_KHR_ray_query.
  • vkGetAccelerationStructureDeviceAddressKHR: the AS address may differ from the buffer device address; basis for the P0 spec-compliance fix.
  • Vulkan formats: the mandatory AS vertex formats exclude R16G16B16A16_UNORM; basis for the D13 float32 RT arena.
  • VK_NV_cluster_acceleration_structure: NVIDIA-only cluster AS, paired with VK_NV_partitioned_acceleration_structure; basis for the vendor-optional P6f tier.
  • Apple — Metal feature set tables: M1 = Apple7, M2 = Apple8, M3/M4 = Apple9, M5 = Apple10, M6 = Apple11; ray tracing in compute from Apple6.
  • MTLIndirectInstanceAccelerationStructureDescriptor: requires macOS 14+; the P2 TLAS descriptor.
  • Microsoft — DXR CLAS/PTLAS preview: clustered geometry and partitioned TLAS in preview, requiring SM 6.10 and a preview Agility SDK.
  • Microsoft — DirectX adopting SPIR-V: Shader Model 7 to adopt SPIR-V as the interchange format (announced September 2024; shipping status unverified).

Vendor guidance and denoising

  • NVIDIA — RTX best practices: rebuild the TLAS rather than update it, compact static BLASes, PREFER_FAST_TRACE for static geometry.
  • NVIDIA — Ray tracing validation at the driver level: VK_NV_ray_tracing_validation with NV_ALLOW_RAYTRACING_VALIDATION=1, used at G0.
  • Intel — Real-time ray tracing in games: RayQuery disables Arc’s Thread Sorting Unit; full TLAS build every frame; compact static AS.
  • NVIDIA NRD repository: published REBLUR/RELAX timings only, used to size the denoise line; source is not read until its license is recorded (§19).

Toolchains, drivers and upstream work

  • Mesa features.txt: VK_KHR_ray_query done for anv gfx12.5+ (Arc), radv gfx10.3+ (RDNA2+), lavapipe, turnip a740+ and venus; Intel UHD 770 (gfx12) has no RT.
  • Phoronix — Lavapipe ray tracing: lavapipe gained acceleration structures and ray query in Mesa 24.1.
  • MoltenVK runtime user guide: no acceleration-structure, ray-query or ray-tracing-pipeline extensions.
  • SPIRV-Cross df0828ca: upstream fix for an inverted AABB-opaque ray-query result in MSL; cherry-picked in P2.
  • Godot PR #118654: draft D3D12 ray tracing by Skyth, with no reviews, merge conflicts and no updates since 2026-04-16; basis for P6a.
  • Skyth Mesa commit 99b60c90: spirv2dxil ray tracing and ray query, rejecting ray-query shaders below SM 6.5; basis for P6a.
  • Godot 4.6 dev 5: new projects default to D3D12 on Windows because “Vulkan is unfortunately unstable on Windows”. GodotNext reverses this default in P0 (§18.1).

Hardware and other engines

  • Apple — M3 announcement: hardware-accelerated ray tracing comes to the Mac with M3 for the first time.
  • Blender — MetalRT default on M3+: MetalRT is on by default only for M3 and newer.
  • Bevy 0.20 release notes and Solari technical post: real-time path tracing still terminates in a world cache, denoiser dependence, and macOS denoiser gap.
  • AMD GPUOpen — FidelityFX Brixelizer: runtime sparse SDF cascades plus Brixelizer GI radiance/irradiance caches and screen probes; relevant to the P6b software tier only.
  • AMD FidelityFX SDK repository and its license file: mixed terms require per-file audit before reuse.
  • meshoptimizer, repository, and MIT license: cluster build/partition/simplification building blocks.
  • NVIDIA RTXGI-DDGI repository, algorithm notes, and proprietary license: DDGI is a paper-level reference here, not reusable code.
  • SVGF module summary: guide/history-moment requirements used to scope the specular denoiser; not treated as a code source.

NVIDIA DLSS, Streamline and precedents (inspected 2026-11-02)

  • NVIDIA DLSS repository and releases: rel and dev NGX runtimes for Windows and Linux x86_64; the PU pin is v310.9.16, which the license packet verifies.
  • NVIDIA RTX SDKs License and RTX Supplement: the terms behind the §19 proprietary-runtime clause.
  • DLSS Programming Guide 310.6.0: NGX on Vulkan and the NGX libraries (§2.1, §5), input conventions (§3), rel-only runtime distribution (§4.2), Project ID initialisation (§5.2), no RenderDoc support (§8.6), and the Performance-mode timing and memory tables behind U1 and U1M.
  • DLSS-RR Integration Guide: RR as a unified denoise + upscale model that overrides SR, its guides and limits, and the Table 3 costs; basis for D21 and P6i.
  • DLSS-FG Programming Guide 310.7.0: NGX-direct FG with *_vk helpers and application-owned pacing; basis for D22 and P6j.
  • RTX UI Developer Guidelines: DLSS naming and quality labels, and the sharpness-slider recommendation (§18.3).
  • nvsdk_ngx_defs.h: the LicenseRef-NvidiaProprietary header banner and the engine-type enum, which has no Godot value.
  • NVIDIA software notification: the Supplement §4 pre-release notice.
  • NVIDIA Streamline, its license.txt, ProgrammingGuideDLSS_G.md and ProgrammingGuideManualHooking.md: MIT except sl_nvperf.*; Windows-only, with present/acquire proxies; the §16 alternative and a P6j spike input.
  • NVIDIA-RTX/godot, nvidia-pt-dlss: an MIT, Windows-only Streamline integration; a study input under §19(c) only.
  • Bevy dlss_wgpu and its build.rs: the user supplies the SDK at a pinned tag, nothing is vendored, and a mock feature exists; precedent for D19’s build split.
  • godot-proposals#2239: upstream Godot will not integrate DLSS officially.
  • godot-proposals discussion 5162: negative precedent, a Linux NGX proof of concept that commits the proprietary NGX headers and libraries.
  • VK_NV_low_latency2: Reflex on Vulkan, needing VK_KHR_present_id; P6j.
  • NVIDIA — DLSS 4.5: second-generation transformer SR (presets L and M), heavier on RTX 20/30, and Dynamic MFG up to 6x.
  • NVIDIA developer forum — Frame generation without Streamline and nvpro-samples/vk_streamline: P6j spike inputs for NGX-direct versus Streamline FG.

20.3 Assumptions, clearly separated from evidence

  • The 2.5× Ada:mid-range factor behind every Ada ceiling. It is replaced by the per-pass Ada:RTX 3060 12 GB ratios measured at G1.
  • The M5 Max ceilings, set equal to the mid-range ceilings and provisional until G2.
  • The Vulkan PCI vendor/device-ID allowlist of RT-core architectures that keeps GTX and RADV emulated RT off the HWRT tier. Lavapipe is caught by the rule’s CPU device-type check.
  • Behaviour on Turing, Ampere (until the RTX 3060 arrives), Blackwell, all Linux drivers, AMD RDNA2+, Intel Arc and Apple M3/M4, which is enabled but unvalidated. Only Ada on Windows (RTX 4080 SUPER) and Apple10 (M5 Max) are validated.
  • The UE-to-PC budget mapping: UE 5.8’s console targets are applied to a mid-range PC on the approximation that PS5 is roughly RX 6700 class.
  • Solo calendar ranges, including the PU calendar (GU about month 9–12) and its lead-review estimate, which are planning assumptions for one lead plus AI agents, re-estimated at every gate.
  • The RTX 4080 SUPER is assumed equivalent to NVIDIA’s RTX 4080 timing rows.
  • NVIDIA’s tables are command-line measurements at Performance mode, not in-engine.
  • U1A, U1Q and Q19’s absolute caps are provisional.
  • WSL2 on the RTX 4080 SUPER machine is assumed available.
Evidence status. No engine build, GPU capture or benchmark has run yet. Code facts were verified against 7cfeb47 on 2026-11-01 (the v2.4 DLSS facts on 2026-11-02, by code reading), including the d3d12 new-project default at editor/editor_node.cpp:8522 (EditorNode::get_initial_settings), which P0 changes to vulkan. Fleet facts come from the same day’s SSH audit. Proposed names, the .ngm format, page sizes and new class and file names are design proposals; none exist in the snapshot.

21. UE 5.8 parity program (v3.0)

On 2026-11-02 the user directed: “We should follow UE 5.8. So whatever they do, we should do it too,” and chose full rendering parity in v1. Everything Unreal Engine 5.8 does around Lumen, Nanite, ray tracing, shadows and lights, and upscaling is v1 scope. GodotNext keeps UE 5.8’s maturity labels and defaults: a feature that is experimental or off by default in UE 5.8 ships the same way here. This section governs wherever §1–§20 conflict with it. Their v2.4 content otherwise stands, including R1–R29, D1–D22, the NextGI caches, the NextGeo DAG and the DLSS module licensing.

Clean-room rule unchanged (§19). Parity means matching UE 5.8’s behaviour, feature set, status labels and defaults, learned from public documentation, release notes, talks and papers. No Epic source, shader, tuning constant or plugin source is read or copied. Where a §21 table quotes a UE value, it is a product-level setting, not an implementation constant.

21.0.1 Domains and phases

§DomainPhasesMilestoneGateSupersedes
21.1RHI foundations and D3D12 DXRPR, PDM5GR, GDP6a, the pu-dlss-d3d12 stretch item
21.2Lumen parity: software tier, Lumen Lite, hit lighting, far field, translucency, MacPS, PLM5GS, GLP6b, P6d, P6e; absorbs P6g
21.3Virtual Shadow Maps, MegaLights, ray-traced shadowsPV, PMM6GV, GMP6c; the VSM part of P6h
21.0.6Product path tracerPPM6GP— (new)

21.5TSR, full DLSS, vendor plugin seamsPT, PU+M8 = v1GT, GU+P6i, P6j

v1 is signed off at M8, when every gate from G0 to GU+ has passed. Phase dependencies are listed in the §12 v3.0 table. The GitHub issues carry them as blocked-by links.

21.0.2 Tier model changes (supersede §3.1 where they conflict)

  • Windows: D3D12 DXR is the primary HWRT path, as UE 5.8 is DX12-first. New projects keep the interim Vulkan default (R5) until gate GD passes. Then pd-windows-dx12-default restores D3D12, which brings back HDR output and DComp transparency. Vulkan RT stays a supported tier.
  • Non-RT hardware: with NextGI enabled, every HWRT failure selects TIER_SOFTWARE, the Lumen-SWRT-class tier (PS). SDFGI runs only when nextgi/enabled = false. New reasons are SWRT_REDUCED (tier), SDF_UNSUPPORTED_DEFORMATION (per instance), HIT_LIGHTING_UNSUPPORTED (feature) and METAL_OS_TOO_OLD (macOS < 14).
  • Mac: matches UE’s “M2+ experimental”. hardware_ray_tracing.macos defaults to off, so the software tier is the Mac default. auto enables Apple8+ with an EXPERIMENTAL label. This reverses v2.4 matrix row 11 at gate GL. If an Epic source shows Mac HWRT on by default, Apple9+ goes back to auto.
  • Quality: medium becomes Lumen Lite (Beta) on both tiers. The traced Medium survives as medium_gather = screen_probes at the same budget.

21.0.3 Cross-domain contracts

  • World-space light grid: PL hit lighting and PM MegaLights both need one. The first to land owns nextgi_light_grid.glsl, and the other reuses it. The owner is recorded in the merged PR and in Appendix A.
  • Translucency split: PL owns Lumen GI for translucency and fog, plus front-layer reflections. PM owns direct many-light lighting of translucency. Both read the same 1/16 × 32 froxel layout (R18).
  • Raster-feature table: every PN raster feature (SW raster, masked, WPO, two-sided, skinned, tessellation, foliage) lands its VSM variant through pv-nanite-raster’s shared table in the same PR.
  • SDF sources: SWRT mesh SDFs are built from the authored mesh, not the DAG. PN’s RT proxies must also write the RT-arena attribute stream that pl-hit-material-groups reads.
  • Denoise inputs: DLSS Ray Reconstruction (PU+) consumes pm-denoise’s bypass mode: the noisy demodulated signal plus guides.
  • Material data outside the color pass: the PM thin-material prepass targets (ml_albedo, ml_material) are the single owner. PL and PN reuse them instead of adding targets.
  • RT pipelines per driver: pr-rd-rt-pipelines covers RD and Vulkan, pd-d3d12-rt-pipelines covers D3D12, and pr-metal-ray-shaders covers Metal (experimental, off by default). Inline ray query stays the default everywhere.

21.0.4 Frame budget composition

The fixed caps stay at 6.6 ms Ada and 16.6 ms mid-range p95 for Dense City + NextGI High (R21). Virtual Shadow Maps replace the atlas shadow line: VSM steady state is 1.0 / 2.5 ms against v2.4’s 0.6 / 1.5. The extra 0.4 / 1.0 ms comes out of the “other passes” remainder, which drops from 2.6 / 6.5 to 2.2 / 5.5 ms. MegaLights (1.60 / 4.00 ms) is off by default, matching UE 5.8, so it is outside the default frame and has its own GM budget. Per-domain budget lines are in §21.1.x–§21.5.x. They join §2.1 under the same ceiling rule: provisional until first measured, reset once, then only tightened (R21).

21.0.5 Remaining deviations from UE 5.8

UE 5.8 featureGodotNext v1Reason
Nanite landscapeNot builtGodot core has no terrain system; it can be revisited if a core terrain lands
RTX Mega Geometry, cluster AS (NvRTX branch)PX, post-v1Not in UE mainline
Distance-field AO, capsule shadows; MegaLights hair, first-person and cloud-shadow featuresNot builtNo Godot equivalent systems (§21.3 risks)
Vendor upscaler plugins (AMD FSR 3.1/4, Intel XeSS)Extension seams only (§21.5)UE gets these through vendor plugins too; no vendor SDK enters the tree
SubstrateNot builtOutside the rendering-parity scope the user chose; §21 names any piece parity needs

21.0.6 Product path tracer (PP)

UE 5.8 ships a user-facing path tracer for reference and final-quality renders. PP turns the D16 reference tracer into that product. It is progressive, runs over the shared TLAS with inline ray query, evaluates materials through PL’s hit-material groups, covers every Godot light type including IES and projector textures, and handles emissive meshes, sky and fog, depth of field and motion blur. It runs on Vulkan, D3D12 (after PD) and Metal (Apple9+, experimental as in UE).

  • Denoising: a built-in screen-space denoiser adapted from the lightmapper’s compute denoiser (LightmapperRD::_denoise). Intel OIDN is an optional module built against a developer-supplied SDK, never committed (§19).
  • User surface: a viewport debug draw mode and a Viewport path-tracing mode with sample-count and bounce settings. It works with the movie writer for final frames.
  • Gate GP [C/T/V]:
    • Converges to the D16 reference within Q1 ≤ 2% on Color Box Room and Concave Courtyard, and within Q9 ≤ 5% on Roughness Corridor, at 4096 spp.
    • Every light type and material feature in the Feature Parity Room matches the reference within FLIP ≤ 0.05.
    • 0 validation errors on all three drivers.
    • Throughput is reported, not gated.

21.0.7 Calendar

Full UE 5.8 parity is a multi-year program for one lead plus AI agents. v1 (M8) lands at about month 36–60. The lanes run in parallel wherever dependencies allow, so M5–M8 overlap P3–P5. The critical path runs through:

  1. the RHI foundations and the D3D12 toolchain (PR/PD, about 7–10 calendar months with three RHI lanes);
  2. the Lumen software tier and hit lighting;
  3. Nanite’s software raster;
  4. VSM.

More engineers on that path shorten it roughly in proportion. Agents compress boilerplate and doctests, not GPU debugging. The calendar is re-estimated at every gate, and each re-estimate is logged in Appendix A.

21.1 RHI foundations and D3D12 DXR (PR, PD)

UE 5.8 is DX12-first on Windows. Its Nanite and VSM need SM 6.6 64-bit atomics, and its hit lighting and MegaLights use RT pipelines. GodotNext has none of these at the RenderingDevice (RD) level today. All D3D12 ray-tracing entry points are stubs (rendering_device_driver_d3d12.cpp:5736-5789). The pinned Mesa NIR-to-DXIL has no ray query, 64-bit atomics or mesh shaders.

Two phases close the gap:

  • PR adds the shared RD capabilities, using fixed keys that the other §21 designs depend on.
  • PD makes D3D12 a full HWRT driver, then makes it the default for new Windows projects.

21.1.1 Parity table

UE 5.8 featureUE status and defaultGodotNext v1 targetPhaseGate
Windows default RHIProduction; DX12 first, SM6 on in new projectsYES New projects get d3d12 once GD passes. Until then they get vulkan (R5, now interim). fallback_to_vulkan stays on.PDGD-11

21.1.2 Design contracts

C1. D3D12 shader toolchain. Stay on Mesa NIR-to-DXIL, because Godot's runtime specialization-constant patching depends on it. Fork it as Legoless/godot-nir-static with three patches:

  • Skyth's DXR patch, for ray query and DXIL libraries;
  • 64-bit atomics;
  • mesh and amplification shaders.

Format and shader-model changes:

  • The container version goes to 3 (and to 4 for the mesh stages). The D3D12 FORMAT_VERSION goes to 2.
  • The SM probe reaches 6.8. Reflection sets the shader model: 6.5 for ray query, 6.6 for int64.
  • A DEBUG_ENABLED build can run the DXIL validator.

Escape hatch: if the mesh patch misses its acceptance criteria after 5 weeks, the task and mesh stages alone go through SPIRV-Cross HLSL and DXC at bake time.

C2. Capability flags. These are appended to RenderingDeviceCommons::Features in merge order:

  • SUPPORTS_BUFFER_INT64_ATOMICS, SUPPORTS_IMAGE_INT64_ATOMICS and SUPPORTS_INT64_ATOMIC_ARITH;
  • SUPPORTS_BINDLESS;
  • SUPPORTS_DRAW_INDIRECT_COUNT and SUPPORTS_DRAW_INDIRECT_FIRST_INSTANCE;
  • SUPPORTS_MESH_SHADER and SUPPORTS_MESH_SHADER_INDIRECT;
  • SUPPORTS_ASYNC_COMPUTE;
  • SUPPORTS_RAYTRACING_REORDER.

Every flag has dummy-driver behaviour and graph usages. A shader that needs a missing capability fails shader_create with one named error.

C3. 64-bit atomics. The portable operations are uint64 max and min on storage buffers and on R64_UINT storage images.

  • D3D12 maps R64_UINT to an R32G32_UINT typed UAV.
  • Metal maps it to an RG32Uint ulong texture with shader-atomic usage, which needs MSL 3.1 and macOS 14.

C4. Bindless. The API is bindless_table_create/allocate/release. A table guarantees at least 65,536 slots, and its entries are read-only.

  • Freeing a resource that is still in a table is deferred until its slot is released.
  • A released slot is reused only after the frame ring has wrapped once.

C5. RT pipelines. Each pipeline has one layout. Material data is reached through bindless plus a ≤32-byte shader record.

  • Pipeline libraries (VK_KHR_pipeline_library; D3D12 collections and AddToStateObject) mean a new material compiles only its own hit group, off the render thread.
  • Metal has no SBT. Its ray shaders use visible and intersection function tables, and stay experimental.

C6. Async compute. compute_list_begin(COMPUTE_QUEUE_ASYNC) records work for a second queue. The graph adds cross-queue waits and ownership transfers. Without that queue, the work runs on the main queue in the same order.

C7. Windows default (reverses R5 at GD).
  • R5's Vulkan default stays only until GD passes. Then EditorNode::get_initial_settings writes d3d12 again (editor/editor_node.cpp:8522), and HDR output and DComp transparency come back.
  • The project-setting fallback default in main/main.cpp:2388 stays vulkan, and fallback_to_vulkan stays on. Together they give UE's "DX12 first, then the next RHI" order.
  • Projects created in the interim keep vulkan; D12 forbids silent switches.
  • Vulkan RT stays supported.

21.1.3 Phase scope and gates

PR (epic pr-epic, M5) starts after G0 on the rhi-vulkan, rhi-metal and rhi-d3d12 lanes, in parallel with P1–P5. Its items:

  • pr-rd-atomics64, pr-rd-bindless, pr-rd-mesh-shaders, pr-rd-async-compute and pr-rd-rt-pipelines (RD and Vulkan);
  • pr-rd-indirect-count, pr-metal-apple8-rt, pr-metal-ray-shaders and pr-procure-parity-fleet.

PD (epic pd-epic, M5) covers:

  • pd-dxil-toolchain, then pd-d3d12-as-ray-query (supersedes P6a), pd-d3d12-rt-pipelines and pd-dxil-mesh-shaders;
  • pd-d3d12-dlss (supersedes pu-dlss-d3d12) and pd-d3d12-interposer-hook;
  • pd-windows-dx12-default, which lands only after GD.

Driver coverage of the fixed keys:

  • pr-rd-rt-pipelines covers RD and Vulkan. D3D12 is pd-d3d12-rt-pipelines; Metal is pr-metal-ray-shaders.
  • pr-rd-mesh-shaders covers RD, Vulkan, Metal and the D3D12 driver plumbing. The D3D12 flag turns on in pd-dxil-mesh-shaders.
  • pr-rd-atomics64 covers all three drivers.
GateCriterion (all must pass)
GR-1 [V][T]Scatter 220 random depth<<32|id values with max into a 1080p R64_UINT image and a uint64 SSBO. The result is bit-identical to the CPU reference on Vulkan and D3D12 (RTX 4080 SUPER) and on Metal (M5 Max), with 0 validation errors. The pass takes ≤1.5× the same pass with 32-bit max.
GR-2 [V][T]A 65,536-slot table with 4,096 textures, sampled with non-uniform indices, matches the reference 100% on all three drivers. In a 10,000-frame churn soak there are 0 validation or GBV errors. 1,000 slot writes take ≤0.05 ms CPU.
GR-3 [V][T]Meshlet fixture (1M triangles): FLIP ≤0.001 against the vertex-pulling VS path on Vulkan and Metal. The mesh path takes ≤1.0× the VS path time on the RTX 4080 SUPER. The indirect variants give identical images.
GR-4 [V][T]Output with async on is byte-identical to async off on 3 scenes, with 0 sync-validation errors. The overlap microbenchmark frame takes ≤0.90× the async-off time on the RTX 4080 SUPER, on both Vulkan and D3D12.
GR-5 [V][T]RT-pipeline fixture: 16 hit groups with alpha any-hit, shader records and indirect trace. It matches the inline reference on 100% of 65,536 rays on Vulkan. Adding one material takes ≤50 ms p95 off-thread. With SER on, the output is identical.
GR-6 [V]The count-draw fixture has 0 validation errors. On D3D12 SM 6.8, gl_InstanceIndex with firstInstance≠0 matches Vulkan 100%, and strides of 20, 32 and 64 B are honoured.
GR-7 [V]On the M2, the Q17 corpus agrees with the RTX 4080 SUPER: IDs and front face exact, t ≤1e-4 relative, barycentrics ≤1e-4 absolute. GR-1 also passes on the M2 (max/min only).
GR-8 [CPU][C]The dummy-driver and graph tests pass. With NextGI off, Q13 is ≤2% and FLIP ≤0.005 on the 3 legacy scenes on every driver.
GR-9 [V]Metal ray shaders on the M5 Max (macOS ≥26.4) match the GR-5 fixture exactly in IDs and front face. 0 Metal validation errors.
GD-1 [V]Q17 corpus, D3D12 against Vulkan on the RTX 4080 SUPER: IDs and front face match on 100% of rays; t ≤1e-4 relative; edge-straddling set exact (0 misses, 0 double hits).
GD-2 [V]The DXIL validator reports 0 new errors on the corpus and on every ray-query, RT, mesh and int64 shader.
GD-3 [T]The NextGI v1 G3-1 lines on D3D12 stay within ≤1.10× of Vulkan in total and ≤1.25× per pass, on the RTX 4080 SUPER and the RTX 3060 12 GB.
GD-4/5 [V][T]The GR-5 and GR-3 fixtures pass on D3D12, with 0 debug-layer or GBV errors.
GD-6 [CPU][V]G1-6 (SELFTEST_FAILED, HWRT_FAULTED) and G1-7 (shader baker, 0 errors) hold on D3D12.
GD-7 [V]The UHD 770 falls back with D3D12_NO_DXR or NOT_HW_ACCELERATED, with ≤2% over the fallback tier. WARP reports CPU_DEVICE.
GD-8 [C][T]DLSS on D3D12, on both barrier paths: mean Q19 is within 0.005 of Vulkan, and each GU-5 line is ≤1.10× Vulkan.
GD-9 [V]2-hour G3-7 soak on D3D12 with 0 DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED.
GD-10 [V]A stub interposer DLL runs the GU-2 scenes with 0 errors. Without the hook, frames are byte-identical.
GD-11 [CPU]A new project writes d3d12. Opening 3 existing projects leaves project.godot byte-identical.

21.1.5 Risks and stated deviations

  • Toolchain (high). GodotNext carries three Mesa patches in its own fork. PR #118654 has been an idle draft since 2026-04-16, and its RT half has two open blockers: payload/attribute mismatches and unused resources that fail DXIL-library validation. pd-d3d12-rt-pipelines fixes both. Mitigations: the debug validator, the Q17 corpus, and the DXC escape hatch for mesh stages.
  • Silent-correctness traps.
    • D3D12 ignores the indirect stride and zeroes the base instance (rendering_shader_container_d3d12.cpp:340).
    • Vulkan never enables drawIndirectCount and forces shaderDrawParameters to 0 (rendering_device_driver_vulkan.cpp:1502).
    pr-rd-indirect-count fixes all three before any VSM or Nanite consumer uses them.
  • Hardware coverage. The fleet has no AMD, Intel Arc or M2 GPU. pr-procure-parity-fleet buys an M2 Mac, which GR-7 requires. An RDNA2+ card is bought only if the lead approves; until then AMD stays enabled but unvalidated.
  • Deviations.
    • No SER on D3D12.
    • No wave-size control in v1. UE has portable fallbacks.
    • Metal 64-bit atomics are max/min only.
    • M2 runs mesh shaders as direct draws only; indirect mesh draws need Apple9.
    • No work graphs and no sparse residency.
    • D3D12 base instance needs SM 6.8; below that, consumers keep firstInstance = 0, as in draw path (c).
  • Clean room. The Mesa fork is MIT, and the SPIRV-Cross patches are Apache-2.0, recorded in thirdparty/README.md. dxil.dll is used for debug validation only and is never committed or shipped. No NVIDIA or Epic file enters the repo.
  • Estimate. About 13–16 engineer-months of lane work in total. With the three RHI lanes in parallel, that is 7–10 calendar months. This is a planning assumption, re-estimated at GR and GD.

21.2 Lumen parity: software tier, hit lighting, far field, translucency, Mac (PS, PL)

v2.4 ships Lumen's HWRT surface-cache mode and nothing around it. Non-RT configurations get SDFGI, and hit lighting, far field and the Apple M2 opt-in are post-v1 (P6b, P6d, P6e, P6g). UE 5.8 ships all of these. It falls back to software ray tracing (SWRT) "when hardware ray tracing is unavailable", and it adds Lumen Lite (Beta) as its Medium quality. Two phases close the gap, both in M5:

  • PS builds the Lumen-SWRT-class tier on the existing surface cache (SRC) and World Radiance Cache (WRC). It covers mesh distance fields (import and runtime), the global SDF and voxel lighting, and the Lite Medium preset. It replaces SDFGI as the non-RT path whenever NextGI is on.
  • PL adds hit lighting, far field, front-layer translucency reflections, the traced translucency GI volume, the remaining reflection behaviours, and UE's Mac policy (M2+ experimental).

Sources: the UE 5.8 Lumen Technical Details and Lumen GI and Reflections pages; the 5.5–5.8 release notes; the 5.8 macOS requirements; the SIGGRAPH 2022 Lumen talk (§20). Items marked UNVERIFIED could not be confirmed on an Epic page; the Lumen Performance Guide could not be fetched in this pass.

21.2.2 Design contracts

L1. Tier rule v3 (replaces the §3.1 fallback). With NextGI on:
  • The HWRT rule is unchanged (§3.1, the PR/PD flags and the macOS override in L11).
  • Any failure selects TIER_SOFTWARE, keeping the HWRT reason that sent it there. This includes NO_RAY_QUERY, NOT_HW_ACCELERATED, D3D12_NO_DXR before PD, CPU_DEVICE, SELFTEST_FAILED, HWRT_FAULTED and AS_MEMORY.
  • SWRT always succeeds on Forward+. It degrades by dropping global-SDF levels, never by switching tier.
  • SDFGI runs only when nextgi/enabled is false. NextGI is then fully off and costs ≤2% over plain SDFGI, as before.
  • D9 (one RB_TEX_AMBIENT writer) holds in all tiers.

L2. Mesh SDF asset. A MeshDistanceField resource is held by ArrayMesh beside shadow_mesh and passed with RS::mesh_set_distance_field.

  • Layout: sparse 8³ bricks in an R8 narrow band of ±4 voxels, with 3 mips. Each mip halves the resolution.
  • Resolution: mesh_sdf/voxels_per_meter (*start* 10) × the per-mesh distance_field_resolution_scale, clamped to ≤128 voxels on the longest axis.
  • Distance: the closest-triangle BVH, extracted from GPUParticlesCollisionSDF3D::_find_closest_distance into core/math/mesh_sdf_builder.{h,cpp}.
  • Sign: the backface-hit fraction of 48 Fibonacci rays (*start*; inside above 25%). Rays are cast with StaticRaycaster (Embree), or with the TriangleMesh BVH where Embree is absent (Windows arm64).
  • Two-sided: materials with cull_disabled get an unsigned shell of 1 voxel and a two-sided flag.
  • Runtime: meshes without a baked SDF are generated asynchronously with the same builder.

L3. GPU mesh SDF atlas.

  • One brick pool (mesh_sdf/pool_size_mb, default 256) with a free list. Bricks never move.
  • Each frame a GPU pass writes per-instance mip requests from camera distance. The CPU reads them back one frame later and streams mips in or out, nearest first.
  • An instance→SDF table is indexed by the NextGI instance record (D13, §8.2), so HWRT and SWRT share instance identity.
  • MultiMesh and GridMap instances are separate SDF objects within the 100k registry cap.
  • Skinned instances and vertex-displacing materials (VERTEX written in vertex()) are excluded, as in UE (no skinning or WPO in SWRT). The new per-instance reason is SDF_UNSUPPORTED_DEFORMATION.

L4. Global SDF.

  • 6 camera clipmaps of 128³ voxels (*start*), 10 cm at level 0 doubling to 3.2 m. Level 5's half-extent of 204.8 m covers rt_scene_radius.
  • Sparse 8³ R16F bricks with a page table, plus a quarter-resolution coarse level per clipmap for empty-space skipping.
  • Bricks are rebuilt only when dirty, from the registry's _update_instance old/new AABB events (P1b), never from camera scroll alone. Static and dynamic bricks are cached separately.
  • Updates are time-sliced: level i updates every 2i frames.
  • Non-uniform scale is bounded by the distance to the oriented bounds.
  • SDFGI's scroll and region bookkeeping (GI::SDFGI) is reused; its raster voxelization (_render_sdfgi) is not.

L5. SWRT tracing and hit shading. The order is screen trace, then a detail trace (only with software_ray_tracing_mode = detail: per-mesh SDFs for the first 2 m through a froxel object grid), then the global SDF, then the WRC handoff at 1.5× probe spacing.

  • Detail hits know the instance, so they use the D6 card lookup.
  • Global-SDF hits sample voxel lighting: 4 clipmaps at 2× the SDF voxel size, with radiance for 6 axis directions plus a coverage weight. They are shaded from a cached per-voxel visibility buffer that only dirty bricks re-trace, and the SRC is resampled every frame.
  • SRC relight keeps D14 and R2 (caps, ¼-resolution masks, shadow-map reuse), with SDF shadow rays in place of ray query.

L6. Lumen Lite = quality = medium (Beta). It replaces the v2.4 traced Medium path on both tiers.

  • GI: an irradiance-field gather. Probes are placed automatically from the WRC clipmaps, with depth-moment occlusion. A probe found inside geometry is dropped, and a probe behind a surface is pushed out along the prepass normal.
  • Interpolation: at ½ resolution, reconstructed by the temporal upscaler.
  • Specular: no reflection rays. SSR handles roughness ≤0.25 and WRC mips handle rougher surfaces.
  • Translucency volume: reads the WRC with a clipmap bias.
  • Scene: the SRC atlas is ½ size, and skinned instances leave the TLAS, as in UE 5.8.
  • Budget: the v2.4 Medium lines (High × 0.5), which matches UE's "twice as fast" claim.
  • Label: the overlay and docs label it Beta. nextgi/medium_gather = irradiance_field (default) keeps the traced path reachable as screen_probes.

L7. Hit lighting. Sorted-deferred, on RT pipelines (PR C5).

  1. Mark. The inline reflection trace marks rays to hit-light and writes a record: instance, geometry, primitive, barycentrics, t and a 16-bit material key.
  2. Sort. Rays are compacted and sorted by key, with SER as well where present.
  3. Re-trace. A raygen re-traces each ray. The closest-hit group, compiled from the Godot material's fragment(), writes a payload: albedo, normal, roughness, metallic, emission and opacity. Textures come through the bindless table; attributes come from an RT-arena stream.
  4. Light. The raygen lights the hit from a world-space light grid built from the D14 scenario light set, with ray-query shadow rays (area shadows on). Indirect light at the hit comes from the SRC and WRC.

Custom light() functions are not run at hits, and each skipped one is counted. max_reflection_bounces defaults to 1. Translucent instances join the RT scene with a separate mask and SRC cards, so they receive GI and can be skipped up to 2 times with tint.

L8. Far field. A second TLAS (as in UE 5.6), covering [rt_scene_radius, far_field_distance = 1 km].

  • Proxies: 64 m cells of merged instances, simplified with meshopt_simplify, each with 6 axis cards.
  • Rebuilds: only when a cell changes or the camera crosses a cell boundary.
  • Inclusion: a per-instance gi_far_field = auto | always | never, the parity of UE's bForceRayTracingFarField.
  • Occlusion-only mode: far_field_occlusion_only; hits return sky occlusion.
  • Direct lighting: rays starting on far-field surfaces skip the near TLAS (5.8).

HWRT tier only; off by default.

L9. Translucency.

  • (a) Front layer. A new PASS_MODE_DEPTH_NORMAL_ROUGHNESS_FRONT_LAYER renders the nearest alpha-blended surface per pixel into a minimal G-buffer (depth, normal and roughness).
    • The reflection pipeline traces it at the opaque settings, using surface cache or hit lighting.
    • It is denoised with the specular denoiser and composited only on that layer in the forward alpha branch.
    • Deeper layers keep WRC glossy reflections.
  • (b) GI volume. The v1 froxel volume (1/16 × 32, FP16 SH-L1) gains a translucency radiance cache.
    • Probes are marked by rasterizing alpha surfaces at 1/16 resolution, and traced with the active tier.
    • The volume is reprojected temporally and gets skylight leaking.
    • Fog keeps reading it (§8.5).

Direct many-light lighting of translucency belongs to PM/MegaLights (§21.3).

L10. Reflection behaviours.

  • max_roughness_to_trace, default 0.4, replaces the fixed constant.
  • Environment fog is applied at reflection hits (default on, as UE 5.8's r.Lumen.HeightFog). Fog on GI rays stays off.
  • With NextGI reflections and SSR both off, rough specular comes from the gather's probes and not from unshadowed sky.
  • nextgi/mode = gi_and_reflections | reflections_only. Reflections-only runs NextGI reflections over LightmapGI or VoxelGI diffuse, as UE does with lightmaps.

L11. Mac policy.

  • hardware_ray_tracing.macos defaults to off, matching UE's Experimental M2+ label. SWRT (M1+, production in UE) is therefore the Mac default.
  • auto enables M2 (Apple8) and M3+ (Apple9+) with an EXPERIMENTAL overlay label. M1 reports NOT_HW_ACCELERATED.
  • The default flips only at GL, so a Mac never drops to SDFGI.
  • If an Epic source shows Mac HWRT on by default, Apple9+ goes back to auto.

21.2.3 Phase scope and gates

PS starts after G1, with mesh SDF import work possible right after G0. PL starts after G3 and PR's RT-pipeline and bindless items. Both close in M5. Every item passes, on the fleet named in §10 plus the PR M2.

GateCriterion (all must pass)
GS-1 [C]SWRT at High, forced on the RTX 4080 SUPER (device 1), against the D16 reference (Color Box Room A/B, off-screen variant, Concave Courtyard):
  • Q1 ≤20%;
  • SWRT coverage (Q2: share of global-SDF hits with voxel-lighting weight ≥0.5) ≥85%;
  • Q3 ≤5% at 20 cm walls, with 10 cm reported;
  • Q4a e(10) ≤25% and e(32) ≤10%.
GS-2 [T]SWRT forced on the RTX 3060 12 GB, NextGI Perf, 1080p p95: High ≤4.0 ms, Epic ≤8.0 ms, Medium (Lite) ≤2.18 ms.
GS-3 [T]UHD 770: Medium (Lite) SWRT ≤6.0 ms at 720p internal. Quality is report-only, because the GPU is below UE's GTX 1070 SWRT floor.
GS-4 [T][V]On the M5 Max with SWRT forced, High ≤4.0 ms. The M2 Medium (Lite) figure is recorded as its provisional line (R21). 0 Metal validation errors.
GS-5 [CPU]Mesh SDF import:
  • bit-identical over 3 runs;
  • sign agrees with a 512-ray parity reference on ≥99.5% of voxels for the closed meshes in the content set;
  • ≤2.0 s per 100k-triangle mesh on 16 threads;
  • SDF bytes ≤25% of mesh bytes on the Dense City kit.
GS-6 [T][CPU]Global SDF and runtime generation:
  • a static camera with one moving 2 m object updates in ≤0.10 ms Ada / ≤0.25 ms mid-range;
  • a 10 m/s fly-through costs ≤0.20 / ≤0.50 ms;
  • a runtime ArrayMesh of 20k triangles enters the global SDF in ≤30 frames, at ≤0.1 ms of main-thread CPU per frame.
GS-7 [C][T]Lite (Medium) on HWRT and on SWRT: Q1 ≤25% on the GS-1 scenes; total ≤0.87 ms Ada / ≤2.18 ms mid-range on HWRT; no reflection rays dispatched (timestamp check).
GS-8 [CPU][V]Tier rule:
  • Mocked GTX, RADV-emulated, UHD, lavapipe, D3D12-without-DXR and Apple7 devices each select SWRT with their reason.
  • With NextGI off, SDFGI is ≤2% over plain SDFGI and the image is byte-identical.
  • Toggling hardware_ray_tracing at runtime switches tiers in ≤2 frames with keep_when_hwrt.
  • 0 validation errors on Vulkan, D3D12 (UHD 770) and Metal.
GS-9 [C][T]Overlap-heavy kitbash scene: SWRT gather ≤0.75 ms on the RTX 3060 (High line 5). Detail mode runs, its Q3 is ≤ that of global mode, and its cost is reported.
GL-1 [C]Roughness Corridor, Epic, hit lighting on: the [0, 0.1) bin reaches Q9 ≤15% and reflection-only FLIP ≤0.10 against D16. The G3-2 bins do not regress.
GL-2 [T]Hit-lit reflections at Epic, 1080p p95, NextGI Perf: ≤+2.0 ms on the RTX 4080 SUPER and ≤+6.0 ms on the RTX 3060, on Vulkan and D3D12. Off: byte-identical output.

GL-4 [C][T]Hit-lit GI (advanced): Q1 is no worse than surface-cache GI on Color Box Room A/B, and the cost is reported. Off: byte-identical.

  • On M2, opt-in HWRT passes Q17 and G2 quality, and its timings are recorded.
  • With the default, the M2 and M5 Max run SWRT.
  • M1 (mocked) reports NOT_HW_ACCELERATED.
  • 0 Metal validation errors.

21.2.5 Risks and stated deviations

  • Leaks in SWRT (high). UE needs walls ≥10 cm. The 10 cm level-0 voxel, the two-sided shell and probe relocation are the mitigations, and GS-1 Q3 is the check. Detail mode is the fallback for interiors.
  • Hit-group explosion. Every material gets a closest-hit group. Mitigations: pipeline libraries (≤50 ms per add), one group per material shader and not per instance, and skipped light().
  • Memory. keep_when_hwrt keeps the SDF pool resident beside the AS. Projects can turn it off, as with UE's cvar, which loses the runtime toggle.
  • Reversal. L11 turns off the v2.4 Apple9+ HWRT default at GL. Rewrite §3.1 row 11.
  • Deviations.
    • No heightfields or Single Layer Water.
    • Far field uses GodotNext cells, not World Partition HLOD.
    • The Lite Medium preset replaces the v2.4 traced Medium by default.
    • Voxel-lighting internals follow the 2022 talk; UE 5.8's current internals are UNVERIFIED.
    • No Substrate closures at hits.
  • Clean room (§19). Concepts come from Epic's public docs and talks only. Every constant above is a GodotNext start value; no Epic code or shader is used. Embree (Apache-2.0) and meshoptimizer (MIT) are already vendored.
  • Estimate. PS is about 10–13 engineer-months and PL about 9–12. On 3–4 lanes that is roughly 7–10 calendar months each, overlapping with P3–P5. This is a planning assumption, re-estimated at GS and GL.

21.3 Shadows and lights: VSM, MegaLights, RT shadows (PV, PM)

UE 5.8 renders shadows with Virtual Shadow Maps (VSM) by default and lights many-light scenes with MegaLights, which became Production in 5.8. GodotNext v2.4 has neither. It uses fixed atlases: a 4096 directional atlas with ≤4 PSSM splits (rendering_server.cpp:3694) and a 4096 positional atlas with 88 slots. The cluster grid holds ≤512 elements (rendering_server.cpp:3829). Ray-traced shadows and many-light sampling were post-v1 (P6c), and VSM was post-v1 (P6h).

Two parallel phases close the gap, both in milestone M6 "Lights & shadows parity":

  • PV builds Virtual Shadow Maps: the page pool and tables, page marking, caching, SMRT, the Nanite and non-Nanite raster paths, local lights, fog and translucency lookups, and the distance-field handoff.
  • PM builds MegaLights and standalone ray-traced shadows. It also builds what MegaLights needs in a Forward+ renderer: material inputs before the color pass, a light grid that holds thousands of lights, IES profiles and the Light Function Atlas.

Labels and defaults follow UE 5.8. VSM becomes the default shadow method for new projects once GV passes; it is Beta on Apple M2+. MegaLights is opt-in per project, as in UE. It is Production on Windows and Experimental on Apple M2+. Project-level ray-traced shadows are off. Every value GodotNext uses (page size, virtual resolution, SMRT and sample counts) is derived by measurement and recorded in Appendix A, never copied from Epic (§19).

21.3.2 Design contracts

S1. Shadow method and tiers. rendering/lights_and_shadows/shadow_method = atlas | virtual. Per light, Light3D.shadow_method = PROJECT | VIRTUAL | ATLAS | RAY_TRACED | DISTANCE_FIELD. VSM needs the Nanite device tier (Forward+, 32-bit image atomics, indirect-count draws); below it, lights fall back to the atlas with one logged reason. Mobile and Compatibility keep atlases.

S2. Page pool and tables (pv-page-pool). A 2D R32_UINT array: layer 0 static depth, layer 1 dynamic depth, reversed-Z as uint. One page-table SSBO; each map owns a mip chain of 32-bit entries (12+12-bit physical page, 8 flag bits). Maps: one per directional clipmap level, 1 per spot, 6 per omni, 5 per area light. Map IDs persist per light instance so caches survive frames.

S3. Page marking. A compute pass over prepass depth requests, per pixel and per light reaching it, the page whose mip gives ≤1 shadow texel per pixel. Coarse mips stay resident for fog froxels and transparents. Allocation keeps cached pages, then uses an LRU ring. Overflow raises the LOD bias of the largest requesters by ≤3 mips.

S4. Page raster. NextGeo clusters are culled per (cluster, dirty page) with a per-page Hi-Z; large clusters go to HW raster, small ones to a depth-only SW raster. Both write with imageAtomicMax, so VSM needs no 64-bit atomics. Non-virtual meshes are culled per page on the GPU and drawn with indirect-count draws through a SHADER_VERSION_VSM_DEPTH variant. Every PN raster feature lands its VSM variant in the same PR.

S5. Caching. Static casters re-render only on invalidation; dynamic casters re-render on requested pages every frame. Invalidation is per page, from swept bounds. AUTO treats moved, skinned, blend-shaped and VERTEX/TIME-writing materials as dynamic, as UE treats WPO. The deferred invalidation budget is in pages per frame (0 = unlimited).

S6. Projection. vsm_shadow() replaces the atlas samplers in light_process_* and the directional loop. SMRT marches R rays toward the light's disk or sphere through the map. Fog and transparents use one coarse tap (translucent_quality = 0).

S7. Local lights and distance fields. All local maps raster in one batched dispatch; Light3D.shadow_resolution_bias shifts the mip. Beyond distance_field_shadow_start, non-Nanite directional casters hand off to DF cone traces through the PS SDFs.

M1. Direct-light inputs. The forced prepass writes ml_albedo and ml_material (F0, shading model, layer-group index). The path is chosen by a spike against two alternatives. Materials with custom light(), unshaded, toon or wrap modes are flagged as fallback and keep the clustered loop. MSAA disables MegaLights for the view.

M2. Light grid. 64×64 px × 32 log-Z froxels with packed per-cell lists (≤64 per cell, overflow counted). Built in two passes, coarse then fine, with Hi-Z cell culling, and cones, spheres and barn-door frusta for culling. Up to 65,536 lights. Lights that need no shadow map skip CPU pairing.

M3. Sampling. Weighted reservoir sampling over the cell list, weighted by unshadowed contribution (BRDF × falloff × IES × light function). Half-resolution by default. Per-tile guiding from last frame's visible lights, with a weight floor that keeps it unbiased. A reference mode renders exhaustively.

M4. Visibility. Hi-Z screen trace, then trace_shadow() in the near field. Then the far field (opt-in), VSM for VSM-method lights, and the global SDF on the non-RT tier. One include serves RT shadows, MegaLights and the D14 SRC rays. It holds the 8-bit mask policy (6 dynamic caster-mask bits, an alpha bit and a translucent bit, with a 20-layer word fallback), bindless alpha, coloured transmission and an RT-pipeline material retrace on Vulkan and D3D12.

M5. Denoise. Demodulate by albedo and EnvBRDF. Accumulate temporally with prepass velocity, then 2 variance-guided à-trous passes. The output goes to the upscaler at render resolution. rr_bypass hands the noisy signal and guides to DLSS-RR (PU+).

M6. Frame and NextGI. The order is prepass → VSM marking → grid → sample → visibility → shade and denoise → color pass. The color pass adds the denoised direct light and skips MegaLights lights in its loop. The SRC relight gives MegaLights lights ray-traced card shadows within R2's cap. The RT-scene composition rules are shared with NextGI presets.

M7. Fog and translucency. One froxel sample set lights both volumetric fog and a new direct SH-L1 channel in the D17 volume. An optional front-layer pass shades the first transparent layer per pixel. SSS pixels evaluate transmission per sample.

M8. Light sources. shader_type light_function baked into an atlas, R8 by default. IES from an LM-63 importer into a profile array. Barn doors and textured rects on AreaLight3D. Per-light contact-shadow parameters. All of them reach forward lighting, fog, SRC, RT shadows and MegaLights.

M9. Ray-traced shadows. 1 ray per pixel per light. Masks live in a tile-sparse atlas (16×16 tiles per light and screen tile, a preset tile budget), so there is no light-count cap, only a memory cap with a counted VSM fallback. A per-light temporal and spatial shadow denoiser.

21.3.3 Phase scope and gates

PV (epic pv-epic, M6) starts after G5 and GR, on the shadows, nextgeo-runtime, editor-ux and content lanes. It needs no ray tracing. Its items:

  • pv-content-scenes, pv-page-pool, pv-page-marking and pv-local-lights;
  • pv-nanite-raster, pv-non-nanite and pv-caching;
  • pv-shading-smrt, pv-fog-translucency, pv-df-shadows and pv-settings-debug.

PM (epic pm-epic, M6, supersedes P6c) starts after G3, GR and GD, on the lights, denoise and nextgi-rt-scene lanes. Its items:

  • pm-spike-direct-light-inputs, then pm-direct-light-inputs;
  • pm-light-grid, pm-tlas-masks-alpha, pm-light-function-atlas and pm-light-source-parity, in parallel;
  • pm-rt-shadows;
  • pm-sampling, then pm-visibility, pm-denoise, pm-shading-integration and pm-fog-translucency.

Cross-domain inputs: PN's raster features (each adds its VSM variant), the PS mesh and global SDFs (DF shadows and the SWRT continuation), the PL far-field TLAS (opt-in far field), PT for GM-7, and PU+ for the RR bypass. Scenes: Shadow Gallery, VSM Flythrough, Fog Hall, RT Shadow Yard and MegaLights Hall, all at 1080p internal and High preset. Devices: RTX 4080 SUPER (Vulkan and D3D12), RTX 3060 12 GB, M5 Max and M2.

GateCriterion (all must pass)
GV-1 [C]Shadow Gallery and VSM Flythrough: mean Q7 ≤0.05 against D16 direct-visibility references. ≥95% of shadowed pixels sample a texel footprint ≤1.0 screen pixel.
GV-2 [V]10,000 frames of VSM Flythrough: 0 sampled-but-unmapped pages, 0 validation errors. With the pool at 25%: 0 corrupted pages and a Q7 loss ≤0.05.
GV-3 [T]Dense City with sun and 16 local lights, cached steady state: V1–V5 ≤1.0 ms RTX 4080 SUPER, ≤2.5 ms RTX 3060. Time-of-day storm (0.5°/frame): ≤1.0 / 2.5 ms with a 512-page budget, and ≤2.5 / 6.25 ms unbudgeted.

GM-6 [C]Fog-only FLIP ≤0.06 and front-layer glass FLIP ≤0.08 against the reference mode. Shared froxel sampling costs ≤0.80× separate fog and translucency sampling.

21.4 Nanite parity (PN)

v2.4 NextGeo (§7) ships Nanite's core: the offline DAG, paged streaming, GPU culling with two-phase Hi-Z, a hardware visibility raster at ≤1 px error, the material-bucket resolve and one fixed RT proxy per mesh. It leaves out what UE 5.8 adds on top. There is no software rasterizer for small triangles. Masked, vertex-offset, pixel-depth-offset and two-sided materials fall back to legacy raster (the §7.4 residual table). Skinned meshes, MultiMesh foliage, tessellation, spline meshes and native Nanite ray tracing are also missing, and v2.4 parked all of these in P6h.

Phase PN (epic pn-epic, milestone M7 “Nanite parity”) closes the gap on top of the §7 DAG, records and resolve, without replacing them. It supersedes p6h-geometry-extensions together with PV, which owns VSM.

Labels and defaults follow UE 5.8. Production features ship on by default on the Nanite device tier. Features UE 5.8 labels Experimental ship Experimental and off: assemblies, voxel LOD, foliage wind, tessellation and native Nanite ray tracing. Where the 5.8 label is UNVERIFIED (skinned Nanite, spline meshes, pixel depth offset), the lead checks the 5.8 documentation before GN and records the result in Appendix A. Until that check, GodotNext uses the last label Epic documented. Thresholds, dicing rates, voxel sizes and bin layouts are derived by measurement and recorded in Appendix A, never copied from Epic code, shaders or cvar values (§19).

21.4.2 Design contracts

N1. Nanite device tier. Forward+, SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_64_BIT or SUPPORTS_BUFFER_ATOMIC_64_BIT from pr-rd-atomics64, and indirect-count draws from pr-rd-indirect-count. Below this tier, NextGeo keeps the v2.4 hardware raster with a depth attachment and logs NO_INT64_ATOMICS. This is a GodotNext extension: UE would use fallback meshes instead. PN features that need the SW path (tessellation, voxels) fall back per instance with SW_RASTER_UNAVAILABLE.

N2. Visibility buffer and hybrid raster (pn-sw-raster). One R64_UINT image (a buffer on Metal if images lack int64) at internal resolution. Each texel holds reversed-Z depth as uint32 in the high bits and the v2.4 visibility ID (25-bit visible cluster | 7-bit triangle) in the low bits. Both paths write with atomicMax:
  • SW: compute, one workgroup per cluster, edge functions with top-left fill.
  • HW: the v2.4 VS vertex-pulling draw (or N9 mesh shaders), whose pixel shader does the atomic with no depth attachment.
Cluster cull routes a cluster to SW when its largest projected edge is below T px and it does not cross the near plane. T is measured per GPU class (GN-2). An export pass then writes the depth buffer and the R32 visibility target, so Stage A/B and velocity (§7.4) keep their v2.4 inputs.
N3. Raster bins and the raster-feature table. RasterFeature bits: MASKED, VERTEX_OFFSET, DEPTH_OFFSET, TWO_SIDED, SKINNED, DISPLACED, SPLINE, VOXEL and ASSEMBLY.
  • A bin key is (feature mask, material shader version). The fixed-function bin covers every material for which uses_shared_shadow_material() (scene_shader_forward_clustered.h:300) is true and that has no geometry feature.
  • Cluster cull writes per-bin SW and HW counts. Each bin is one indirect SW dispatch plus one indirect-count HW draw.
  • The table lives in shaders/nextgeo/raster_features_inc.glsl and virtual_mesh_storage.h. It is shared with pv-nanite-raster, so each feature adds its VSM entry in the same PR.
N4. Material raster target (pn-raster-bins). ShaderCompiler gains a spatial raster target in two forms: SW compute (STAGE_COMPUTE, already in shader_compiler.h:42) and a HW raster pixel variant.
  • It runs vertex() at 3 vertices with barycentric varyings, and the fragment subset that decides coverage and depth: ALPHA, the alpha scissor/hash built-ins, discard and DEPTH_OFFSET. Implicit-LOD sampling becomes textureGrad with analytic derivatives (§7.4 rule).
  • A bin that uses FRAGCOORD, screen textures or an absolute DEPTH stays on legacy raster with UNSUPPORTED_RASTER_FEATURE.
N5. Vertex offset (WPO).
  • Bounds: materials declare vertex_offset_max (meters, a Material property, with a project default). Cull inflates cluster and instance bounds by it. Clusters displaced beyond it are clamped and counted in a debug stat.
  • Disable distance: per-instance vertex_offset_disable_distance selects the fixed-function bin past it.
  • Velocity: the motion-pass precedent applies; vertex() reruns with the previous TIME and transform.
  • Invalidation: such instances are dynamic for the VSM cache (pv-caching). The RT proxy evaluates vertex offset only with the per-instance rt_evaluate_vertex_offset (default off, like UE's per-primitive ray-tracing WPO evaluation; default UNVERIFIED for 5.8), refitted under the line-1b budget. Raster-side Evaluate WPO is on by default.

N6. Pixel depth offset. DEPTH_OFFSET is a float in view-space meters, ≥0, pushing away from the camera. The HW variant writes it with conservative-depth qualifiers; SW adds it before the atomic. Legacy forward gets the same built-in, so materials stay portable.

N7. Shading bins (pn-shading-bins). After visibility export:
  • a binning pass counts pixels per shading bin (material, shading-feature bits);
  • a prefix sum and scatter write packed 32-bit pixel coordinates per bin;
  • each bin shades through an indirect compute dispatch of the material's Stage A/B resolve compiled as compute.
This adds no new targets. When MegaLights is on, Stage A bins write PM's ml_albedo/ml_material. The v2.4 tile-quad raster path stays for materials the compute form rejects, and as the A/B baseline.
N8. Skinned Nanite.
  • Offline: the DAG is built in bind pose. Each cluster stores ≤16 bone indices and bone-space bounds for each of them. Simplification keeps the per-vertex weights of retained vertices.
  • Runtime: Skeleton (mesh_storage.h:328) gains a previous-frame palette. Cull transforms the bone bounds; SW, HW, mesh and resolve paths skin vertices in place, so there is no pre-skinned buffer.
  • RT: the fallback proxy is skinned through p3-skinned-actors' refit, with an RT-range skinning list for off-screen characters.
  • Meshes with blend shapes stay legacy with BLEND_SHAPES.

N9. Mesh-shader HW path. One task workgroup per HW-bin cluster batch and one mesh workgroup per cluster, at ≤128 triangles (the §7.1 cap) and the same visibility writes as N2. It is selected per driver by SUPPORTS_MESH_SHADER and GN-10.

N10. Foliage.
  • MultiMesh: a MultiMesh of a virtualized mesh becomes an instance-range source. Each instance gets its own record and its own cull and cut.
  • Assemblies: VirtualGeometryAssembly (parts plus local transforms, ≤2 levels) expands to parts during cull.
  • Voxels: per cluster group, the offline build emits sparse 4³ bricks holding coverage, albedo and normal. Cull swaps a group for its bricks when a brick projects below V px. Bricks are SW-rasterized into the same visibility buffer under a voxel ID namespace.
  • Wind: a procedural VirtualGeometryWind skeleton (trunk, branches) is evaluated per instance in compute and fed to the N8 skinned path.
N11. Tessellation and displacement.
  • Material: a new optional displacement() function writes DISPLACEMENT (a scalar along the normal), with displacement_magnitude and displacement_center as material properties.
  • Dicing: clusters are diced at runtime to nextgeo/tessellation/dicing_rate px (measured default). Edge factors come only from each edge's two endpoints in world space, so neighbours agree and no cracks form. Diced triangles are SW-rasterized; patches that would exceed T go to a bounded compute-written HW stream.
  • Bounds and other passes: bounds are inflated by the magnitude. VSM gets the directional and local toggles that 5.8 adds. The RT proxy stays undisplaced.

N12. Spline meshes. SplineMeshInstance3D (a MeshInstance3D subclass) holds start/end positions and tangents, roll, scale and the forward axis. It sets RenderingServer.instance_geometry_set_spline_deform(). The deform runs at vertex fetch in legacy raster (a scene-shader define) and in NextGeo cull (swept bounds), raster and resolve. LOD error is scaled by the spline's maximum stretch.

N13. Nanite ray tracing. nextgeo/ray_tracing_mode = fallback | native (default fallback).
  • Fallback is v2.4 D13.
  • Native (Experimental) builds one BLAS per (mesh, cut bucket) from the resident cut at error ≤ rt_max_error, with CPU-known maximum primitive counts over GPU-written buffers.
  • Rebuilds when residency changes are capped per frame. Above the cap, an instance keeps its previous BLAS or the proxy.

21.4.3 Phase scope and gate GN

PN (epic pn-epic, M7) starts after G5 and GR. The mesh-shader D3D12 leg waits for GD. Lanes: nextgeo-runtime, nextgeo-offline and nextgeo-resolve. Its items:

  • pn-content-scenes, then pn-sw-raster, the foundation of all later items. It also creates the raster-feature table, which pv-nanite-raster consumes;
  • pn-raster-bins, then pn-masked-two-sided, pn-vertex-offset and pn-depth-offset, in parallel;
  • pn-shading-bins and pn-mesh-shader-raster, in parallel with the above;
  • pn-skinned-dag, then pn-skinned-runtime;
  • pn-foliage-instancing, then pn-voxel-lod and pn-foliage-wind (the latter also after pn-skinned-runtime);
  • pn-displacement-material, then pn-tessellation;
  • pn-spline-meshes, pn-rt-native and pn-settings-debug;
  • gate pn-gate-gn.

Dependency direction with PV. pv-nanite-raster depends on pn-sw-raster (shared triangle setup, table file) and pn-raster-bins (the bin list that page raster iterates). Every later PN raster feature depends on pv-nanite-raster and adds its VSM entry in its own PR; GN-12 checks those entries. Those two PN foundations therefore gate an M6 item; the integrator schedules them early in M6, or moves them to M6.

New scenes, all generated by seeded scripts from CC0 or procedural sources:

  • Micro Detail: Dense City with 4× subdivided facades, so that ≥30% of visible triangles fall under 1 px².
  • Foliage Field: 200k MultiMesh trees and grass, masked leaves, wind, assemblies and voxels.
  • Character Crowd: 100 skinned characters of 500k triangles each.
  • Displacement Cliff and Spline Road.
GateCriterion (all must pass)
GN-1 [C]Hybrid raster, Micro Detail and Dense City at 1 px, against an all-HW raster of the same cut: visibility-ID mismatch ≤0.1% of pixels; depth max error ≤1 ULP of uint32 reversed-Z; coverage IoU ≥0.999; 0 holes over a 600-frame flythrough.
GN-2 [T]Cull + Hi-Z + visibility stays within the v2.4 line (0.8 / 2.0 ms) on Dense City. On Micro Detail, hybrid is ≤0.60× HW-only. The threshold T is recorded per GPU class in Appendix A.
GN-3 [C]Feature Parity Room and Foliage Field, NextGeo vs forward at the finest cut, for masked, vertex offset, depth offset, two-sided, custom UVs and the programmable distance:
  • FLIP ≤0.02;
  • alpha-edge coverage IoU ≥0.99;
  • DEPTH_OFFSET depth error ≤1×10⁻⁵ normalized;
  • vertex-offset velocity mean error ≤0.1 px;
  • 0 culled-but-visible clusters, checked against a no-cull render every 100th frame over 10,000 frames.
The §7.4 residual table loses its alpha and position-altering rows.
GN-4 [T]Foliage Field (masked + vertex offset on): programmable raster overhead ≤ line N2.
GN-5 [C][T]Character Crowd against legacy GPU skinning: FLIP ≤0.02 at the finest cut; velocity mean error ≤0.1 px; 0 culled-but-visible clusters over 10,000 frames; fallback BLAS refit current for 100% of RT-range characters, including off-screen ones; cost ≤ line N4.
GN-6 [C][T]Displacement Cliff (Experimental), 600-frame flythrough: 0 crack pixels inside the watertight reference mask; height error ≤1 px projected against a CPU reference dicing at the same rate; cost ≤ line N5.
GN-7 [C][T]Foliage Field:
  • assemblies vs flattened instances FLIP ≤0.005;
  • voxel LOD vs full geometry at the switch distance FLIP ≤0.05, temporal flicker (frame-to-frame FLIP while static) ≤0.01;
  • resident geometry with voxels ≤0.6× without;
  • wind velocity mean error ≤0.1 px;
  • cost ≤ line N6.
GN-8 [C]Spline Road: vertex error ≤0.5 mm against a CPU deform reference at 10 m; legacy vs NextGeo FLIP ≤0.01; 0 culled-but-visible clusters.
GN-9 [C][V]Fallback mode: G5-6 still passes. Native mode (Experimental) on Concave Courtyard: ray-hit vs raster depth mean error ≤1 px reprojected; rebuild cost ≤ line N7; 0 validation errors on Vulkan and D3D12. Metal is report-only.
GN-10 [T][V]Mesh-shader HW bins against the VS path: FLIP ≤0.001; time ≤0.95× on Vulkan and D3D12 (RTX 4080 SUPER, RTX 3060) and ≤1.0× on Metal Apple9+. A driver that fails keeps VS as its default, with the reason recorded.
GN-11 [C][T]Shading bins against the tile-quad buckets: FLIP ≤0.001; binning ≤ line N3; Stage A+B ≤1.0 / 2.5 ms; ≤0.95× tile-quad time on Dense City, or tile quads stay the default.
GN-12 [C][T]Every PN raster feature has its VSM entry. Shadow FLIP ≤0.01 against brute-force per-page raster on Foliage Field, Character Crowd and Displacement Cliff, and GV-3 still passes with all features on.
GN-13 [V][CPU]
  • 0 validation errors on Vulkan, D3D12 and Metal (M5 Max) across all GN scenes, plus a 10,000-frame soak.
  • M2 runs GN-1 and GN-3 for the Beta label (timings recorded only).
  • A device without int64 atomics renders the v2.4 HW path with NO_INT64_ATOMICS.
  • Render-thread CPU for PN on Dense City + Foliage Field is ≤ +0.30 ms.
GN-14 [C]Defaults and labels:
  • a unit test asserts every default in §21.4.1;
  • Experimental features show EXPERIMENTAL in the editor;
  • every visualization mode renders;
  • the UNVERIFIED-label checks are recorded in Appendix A.

21.4.4 Budgets (new lines for §2.1)

Units are ms p95 at 1080p internal. The §2.1 ceiling rule applies: a line may reset once at its first measurement and may only tighten after that. M5 Max lines for Production features equal the mid-range lines. M5 Max lines for Experimental or label-pending features are recorded only.

LineRTX 4080 SUPERRTX 3060 12 GBM5 Max
N1 Cull + Hi-Z + hybrid visibility, Dense City at 1 px (replaces the v2.4 line; same values)0.802.002.00
N2 Programmable raster overhead over fixed-function, Foliage Field0.501.251.25
N3 Shading binning and visibility export (inside the Stage A+B line)0.100.250.25
N4 Skinned Nanite increment, Character Crowd0.501.25recorded
N5 Tessellation increment, Displacement Cliff (Experimental)1.002.50recorded
N6 Foliage Field NextGeo total: cull, raster, voxels and wind1.503.75recorded
N7 Native Nanite RT BLAS rebuilds per frame (Experimental)1.002.50recorded
N8 Spline deform increment, Spline Road0.050.120.12

Memory lines:

  • MN1: the visibility buffer adds 8 B per internal pixel (16.6 MB at 1080p, 66.4 MB at 2160p).
  • MN2: skinning data adds ≤15% to a skinned mesh's .ngm.
  • MN3: the Foliage Field with voxels is resident at ≤0.6× its size without them (GN-7).
  • MN4: native RT BLASes take ≤2× the fallback BLAS memory and sit inside the AS memory line. Over it, instances keep the proxy.

Frame cap. The Dense City + NextGI High caps (6.6 / 16.6 ms) are unchanged. N1 keeps the v2.4 value and N3 sits inside the resolve line. N2 and N4–N8 apply only in their own scenes, which carry their own reported totals. VSM costs for PN features count against PV's V2 line (GV-3).

21.4.5 Risks and stated deviations

Risk or deviationMitigation or reason
Int64 atomics are missing on D3D12 (until the Mesa i64 patch lands) or on M2 (Apple8 support UNVERIFIED)N1 tier rule: the v2.4 HW raster stays, with a reason. The SW path never lands without pr-rd-atomics64's GR-1.
Each programmable material adds up to 3 variants (SW compute, HW raster, compute shading), so compile time and permutations growVariants compile on first bin use and in the shader baker. pn-raster-bins records bake-time growth on the Feature Parity Room; growth over +40% triggers a scope review before GN.
Voxel LOD has no public technical descriptionClean-room design (N10), Experimental and off. If GN-7's voxel rows fail after two measured iterations, the outcome row is a §21 scope review, logged in Appendix A.
Tessellation cracks at streaming LOD seamsEdge factors depend only on world-space edge endpoints (N11). GN-6 checks crack pixels across streaming transitions.
Conservative skinned bounds cause overdrawPer-bone bounds (N8). GN-5 gates cost and holes together.
D3D12 mesh shaders depend on the Mesa forkpd-dxil-mesh-shaders' DXC escape hatch. Until then D3D12 keeps the VS path (GN-10 rule).
Native Nanite RT rebuild cost and memory; no indirect AS build on D3D12 or MetalCPU-known maximum counts, a per-frame rebuild cap and the proxy as overflow (N13). The mode is Experimental and off, as in UE.
Deviation: Nanite landscapeGodot core has no terrain. The smallest in-scope path is a rendering contract, not a terrain system. Terrain plug-ins that emit ArrayMesh tiles virtualize them through ImporterMesh::build_virtual_geometry() and get NextGeo, N11 displacement, VSM and NextGI. Heightfield streaming, layer painting, grass and runtime virtual texturing stay out, like PCG and World Partition.
Deviation: below-tier HW-only rasterA GodotNext extension that keeps the v2.4 promise on devices without int64 atomics. UE would draw fallback meshes there.
Deviation: blend shapes on skinned NaniteThey stay legacy (BLEND_SHAPES) until UE 5.8 support is verified. If it is confirmed, a PN issue is filed before GN.
Deviation: multiviewIt stays on the v2.4 fallback pending the label check (§21.4.1).

21.5 Upscaling and frame generation: TSR, DLSS, vendor plugins (PT, PU+)

UE 5.8 makes TSR its default anti-aliasing and upscaling method on every RHI. Vendors plug in from outside the engine: NVIDIA, AMD and Intel each ship their own plugin over the public ITemporalUpscaler interface and hook presentation themselves. UE 5.8 has no first-party frame generation. GodotNext follows the same model:

  • PT builds a clean-room TSR-class upscaler and makes it the default for new Forward+ projects. It also adds the public plug-in surface and dynamic resolution.
  • PU+ completes DLSS the way NVIDIA's UE plugin ships it: Ray Reconstruction on D3D12 and Vulkan, and Frame Generation, MFG and Reflex through Streamline on D3D12 only.

DLSS SR/DLAA stays in PU (Vulkan, M3), and its D3D12 port is pd-d3d12-dlss (§21.1).
