21.5.2 Design contracts

U1. TSR. A new RendererRD::TSR effect runs in the existing pre-tonemap upscaler slot (render_forward_clustered.cpp:2565-2655), as SCALE_TSR. Its compute passes are written for GodotNext:

  • velocity dilation;
  • parallax disocclusion;
  • shading rejection, using the reactive mask for translucency;
  • history update at 100% or 200% of output;
  • spatial anti-aliasing for rejected pixels;
  • resolve.

The stability features are added per preset. The design follows Epic's public documents only, under the §19 clean-room rule: no UE source, shaders or tuning constants. The user-facing preset values are product behaviour and are matched.

The mode is appended as VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_TSR = 7, after DLSS = 6. TSR accepts scales from 0.25 to 2.0; above 1.0 it supersamples. Mobile and Compatibility keep the existing bilinear rule.

U2. Plug-in surface. UE vendors ship outside the engine, so GodotNext exposes the same two seams to GDExtension:

  • TemporalUpscalerExtension has the virtuals _get_name, _is_available, _get_scale_range, _get_mip_bias_offset, _get_jitter_phase_count, _process and _reset_history. It wraps the internal registry from pu-temporal-upscaler-hook.
  • FrameGenerationExtension receives the HUD-less colour, the UI layer, depth, velocity and the camera, through the hooks PU+ adds to the present path.

Only one frame-generation provider can be active per process, the same rule Intel states for UE.

Neither GodotNext nor its CI contains an AMD or Intel SDK. A test-only sample extension (a copy upscaler and a pass-through FG provider) proves both seams.

U3. Dynamic resolution. RendererViewport reads the previous frame's GPU time from RD timestamps. It steps scaling_3d_scale within the active upscaler's range, using 16 buckets and changing at most once every 30 frames.

When only the internal size changes, RenderSceneBuffersRD::configure takes a fast path: it recreates internal-resolution textures and keeps output-resolution ones, including histories and upscaler contexts. Contexts are created at the maximum fraction.

U4. DLSS Ray Reconstruction. This carries over the design fixed in v2.4 for P6i:

  • guides: diffuse and specular albedo MRTs, packed world-space normal with roughness, and hit distance;
  • RR runs in the upscaler slot and replaces §2.1 line 8;
  • line 7 stays;
  • at least 32 jitter phases;
  • quality = auto never enables RR.

New in v3.0: RR runs on D3D12 (dlss_ngx_d3d12.cpp) as well as Vulkan, and it ships in v1. Denoisers added later by PL and PM expose a bypass switch, so RR can replace them as NVIDIA's plugin does.

U5. Frame generation and Reflex. These use Streamline on D3D12, like NVIDIA's UE plugin. SR and RR stay on NGX directly; NVIDIA ships the two side by side in UE.

Streamline loads through pd-d3d12-interposer-hook. Reflex markers sit at the following points:

  • Main::iteration;
  • Input::flush_buffered_events;
  • RenderingServer::draw;
  • RenderingDevice::swap_buffers.

The HUD-less split runs only while FG is active. In RendererViewport::_draw_viewport, the 3D colour is copied right after _draw_3d. The canvas layers above it are drawn into a premultiplied UI layer, then composited.

FG runs only on the main window of games, never in the editor or XR. Vulkan and Metal report API_UNSUPPORTED.

U6. Distribution and licences.

  • NVIDIA. NGX/DLSS is covered by the RTX SDK licence (the §19 packet). Streamline source is MIT, but its NVIDIA-signed plugin DLLs (sl.*.dll, nvngx_dlssg, nvngx_dlssd) are under NVIDIA's terms, and sl_nvperf is excluded. All of them come from the developer's SDK checkouts, are copied by the module's export plugin and are checked by SHA-256. None is ever committed.
  • AMD. FSR 3.1 is MIT source; FSR 4 ML ships only as signed DLLs, under a licence that is unverified.
  • Intel. The XeSS SDK binaries are under Intel's licence (unverified). Intel's UE plugin is MIT, but it is UE plugin source and so a forbidden input (§19).
  • Epic. TSR uses Epic's public documents only.

NVIDIA ships its UE plugin itself. GodotNext's equivalent is developer-built until NVIDIA answers the §19(j) questions.

21.5.3 Phase scope and gates

PT (M8, lane tsr): the TSR core, the stability set, the extension API, dynamic resolution and async compute. The new-project default flips only after GT passes, the same way pd-windows-dx12-default waits for GD.

PU+ (M8, lane upscalers):

  • RR guides and RR mode, after G3, GU and GD-8;
  • the vendor-neutral frame-generation present path;
  • Streamline with Reflex;
  • DLSS FG, MFG and Dynamic MFG.

Shared rules for both gates:

  • Q19 tolerance = max(0.002, 2 × the run-to-run spread), as in GU-4.
  • Every line is provisional until it is first measured. It may reset once (Appendix A).
GateFailable criterion
GT-1 [CPU]Doctests: mode appends (TSR = 7, CUSTOM = 8), preset map, scale range 0.25–2.0, extension registry and availability, one-shot reset. No skips.
GT-2 [C]RTX 4080 SUPER, Vulkan; Upscaler Torture plus the 3 legacy scenes, at 1440p and 2160p output. Mean Q19:
  • TSR Epic at 1.0 ≤ TAA-native and ≤0.03;
  • TSR High at 0.667 ≤ FSR2 Quality − tolerance and ≤0.045;
  • TSR High at 0.5 ≤ FSR2 Performance and ≤0.06.
Temporal stability:
  • static Q8 ≤1.5%, and moiré-mask Q8 ≤2%;
  • Q10 at frame 4 after the occluder move ≤ FSR2 + 1 pp and ≤10%;
  • thin-wire-mask Q19 ≤ FSR2 at the same scale.
DLSS comparison: report only.
GT-3 [C]D3D12 (RTX 4080 SUPER) and Metal (M5 Max): mean Q19 within 0.005 of Vulkan at the same preset and scale
GT-4 [T]Budget lines T1–T6 (§21.5.4), at ≤1.25× each line
GT-5 [V]0 validation errors on Vulkan, the D3D12 debug layer with GBV, and Metal API validation, for all 5 presets. A 30-minute soak that changes preset every 60 s: VRAM stays within ±1%.
GT-6 [T]Dense City with a scripted 2× GPU load step at 60 Hz:
  • p95 GPU frame time ≤1.05 × target within 30 frames;
  • ≤1 reallocation per 30 frames;
  • Q10 after a scale change ≤ baseline + 1 pp.
GT-7 [T]TSR critical-path time with async compute ≤0.80× the synchronous time, on Vulkan and on D3D12. Output FLIP ≤0.001.
GU+-1 [CPU]Mock-backend doctests:
  • the reasons;
  • a single active FG provider;
  • FG on the main window only;
  • auto never enables RR;
  • RR uses ≥32 phases.
GU+-2 [V]GU-2 attribution rule, with RR, FG 2x and Reflex on. D3D12 runs with the debug layer and GBV; Vulkan runs RR only. 0 errors on GodotNext objects.
GU+-3 [C]RR quality:
  • Q20 ≤20% and ≤ D8 + SR + 2 pp;
  • Roughness Corridor ≤30% per bin for roughness ≤0.4;
  • Q8 ≤1.5%; Q10 ≤10%;
  • D3D12 vs Vulkan Q20 within 1 pp.
GU+-4 [T]RR lines U2 and U3, and RR memory (§21.5.4)
GU+-5 [T]Reflex On vs Off, GPU-bound: PC latency ≥10% lower. FG 2x on the RTX 4080 SUPER: displayed frame-time p99 ≤1.5× the median, and displayed rate ≥1.6× the FG-off rate.
GU+-6 [C]Static-UI scene: UI pixels with FG on vs off, FLIP ≤0.005. HUD-less colour vs a 3D-only render, FLIP = 0.
GU+-7 [V/CPU]Each fallback gives the expected reason and exactly one log line:
  • Vulkan or Metal FG → API_UNSUPPORTED;
  • RTX 3060 FG → FG_GPU_UNSUPPORTED;
  • MFG on Ada → MFG_REQUIRES_RTX50, running FG 2x;
  • the UHD 770.
With every PU+ feature off: frames are byte-identical, and the G3 and G5 lines are unchanged.
GU+-8 [CPU]check_proprietary_sdk_files.py also covers sl.*.dll. Default and mock builds contain 0 nvngx_dlssg or sl.interposer bytes. Export copies only rel files listed in the manifest, after a SHA-256 match.
GU+-9 [T]Applies only if an RTX 50 is in the fleet: MFG 4x and Dynamic MFG displayed frame-time p99 ≤1.5× the median. Otherwise they ship enabled but unvalidated, logged in Appendix A.

21.5.4 Budgets

Times are p95 over 600 frames. Each pass is measured by its RD timestamp: “TSR”, “DLSS-RR” or “DLSS-G”.

LineConfigurationCeiling
T1TSR High, 2160p output at 0.5, RTX 4080 SUPER≤0.80 ms
T2TSR Epic (200% history), 2160p output at 0.5, RTX 4080 SUPER≤1.60 ms
T3TSR High, 1440p output at 0.667, RTX 3060 12 GB≤1.10 ms
T4TSR High, 1440p output at 0.667, M5 Max≤0.90 ms
T5TSR Low, 1080p output at 0.5, Intel UHD 770≤2.50 ms
T6TSR memory, all textures including resurrection≤14 B per history pixel: 52 MB at 1440p High, 116 MB at 2160p High, 465 MB at 2160p Epic
T7Dynamic-resolution controller≤0.02 ms CPU per frame. A reallocation frame costs ≤2× the median frame.
U2, U3RR at Performance, output 1080p / 1440p / 2160p (Ada); guides and pack≤1.33 / 2.25 / 4.94 ms; guides ≤0.10 ms; memory ≤193 / 336 / 724 MB
F1DLSS-G pass at FG 2x, 2160p, RTX 4080 SUPER≤3.0 ms. NVIDIA lists 1.35–2.77 ms on the RTX 4090.

21.5.5 Risks and stated deviations

ItemDeviation or riskHandling
DOF orderUE runs DOF before TSR. Godot runs DOF after every upscaler (renderer_scene_render_rd.cpp:496-548).Kept for v1, because the order is shared by FSR2, MetalFX and DLSS. Stated deviation.
Translucency velocityUE has an early translucency velocity pass. Godot's translucent materials write no velocity.TSR rejects with the reactive mask, as FSR2 does. Stated deviation.
Dynamic resolutionUE renders into a fixed allocation with a per-frame view rect. GodotNext reallocates per bucket.Hysteresis and line T7. View-rect rendering is post-v1.
FSR and XeSSUE users install vendor plugins. No vendor ships one for GodotNext.v1 provides the same seams. Availability depends on vendors or the community. Stated deviation.
MFGNeeds RTX 50; the fleet has noneGU+-9 is conditional; the lead decides on an RTX 50 purchase.
TSR qualityA clean-room TSR may not beat FSR2 (GT-2)Two tuning iterations, then a scope review. New projects keep today's default until GT passes.
Clean roomThird-party write-ups of UE's TSR may be derived from Epic source.Forbidden as inputs (§19). Sources are logged per PR.

A. Revision history

VersionChanges
v2.0–v2.1Initial snapshot audit and contracts.
v2.2C++ verification and SSH fleet audit. Forward+ load/clear and frame order: velocity kept on tile GPUs, a forced depth + normal-roughness prepass, a dedicated NextGI call site, and shadow-view culling before _render_shadow_begin. Asset readback safety: GPU upload kept under retain_authored, because ArrayMesh::surface_get_arrays and ArrayMesh::_get_surfaces read back through mesh_get_surface; export-time stripping; one .ngm per mesh; encrypted-PCK collisions made an explicit error. Per-instance SRC radiance pages, separate from per-mesh card descriptors, with a card-hit coverage gate. Fleet recorded (M5 Max, the RTX 4080 SUPER machine, the Linux VM) and the v2.2 phase split (mapped to v2.3 phases in §12).
v2.3User decision of 2026-11-01 for HWRT-first per UE 5.8: Vulkan and Metal HWRT in v1, D3D12 DXR in P6a, SDFGI fallback, and fixed-proxy RT (D4, D11–D13). The 82-finding review applied, including the ShaderCompiler material-bucket resolve (D7), world-space SRC lighting (D14), the WRC as a radiance cache (D15), the in-engine reference path tracer (D16) and the dynamic-actor policy (D17). Budgets tiered: High 1.6 ms Ada / 4.0 ms mid-range, Epic 3.2 / 8.0. Phases P0–P6 with milestones M0–M4 and an ordered cut list (D18).
v2.3 (critique)Adversarial critique resolved. Velocity written by the forced NextGI prepass from P1b; an SRC relight and shadow-ray budget; the reference path tracer split into a P0 analytic v0 and a P1a scene path; step and continuous convergence metrics (Q4a, Q4b); split AS budget lines with G0 calibration. New GodotNext projects default to Vulkan on Windows; validated vs enabled-but-unvalidated wording; the auto | medium | high | epic quality setting, Medium at 0.87 ms Ada / 2.18 ms mid-range; a driver self-test and crash latch. The NextGI Perf timing scene; the RTX 3060 at G0; the Metal driver lane in parallel with P1. Calendar re-estimated (M3 about month 12–18, M4 about 20–30; schedule cut if M3 is projected after month 18). Metric ids renamed Q1–Q18, adding RT coverage (Q18).
v2.4 (2026-11-02)DLSS, per the user request “We need DLSS support as well”.
  • Decisions D19–D22; amendments to D1, D8, D12 and D18; R21 extended; §14 Never cut amended.
  • DLSS SR/DLAA as an NGX-direct Vulkan module built against a developer-supplied SDK, with a mock backend in CI. It joins the claim of the first milestone signed off after GU. Official binaries exclude it pending NVIDIA’s written answer.
  • New phase PU (PU-core after G0 in rhi-vulkan; the rest after G1 in a sixth lane, upscalers), with the non-blocking gate GU (GU-1 to GU-6; GU-7 conditional for D3D12) and the milestone-move rule.
  • Core MIT hooks: driver-callback stages, Vulkan native interop, extension providers with the validation hook, TemporalUpscaler, dense velocity with the exact sentinel, history reset.
  • The §6.1 DENSE row, ViewportScaling3DReason, §3.2 upscaler selection.
  • Metrics Q19–Q21 added before the G0 freeze; the Upscaler Torture scene; §2 row 10 and the §2.1 upscaler table.
  • DLSS-RR moved from P6f to P6i (design fixed; P3 gets default-off hooks). FG/MFG/Reflex become P6j.
  • G3-3, G5-1 and G5-7 get conditional DLSS rows. G3-7 and G5-8 are unchanged.
  • §19 proprietary-runtime clause, with the research-read disclosure.
  • 14 new issues, 30 edited.
v3.0User directive of 2026-11-02, “follow UE 5.8, whatever they do”, with full rendering parity in v1. New §21 parity program with phases PR, PD, PS, PL, PV, PM, PP, PN, PT, PU+ (milestones M5–M8) and PX (post-v1). D3D12 DXR becomes the primary Windows path and default after GD; the Lumen-SWRT-class tier replaces the SDFGI fallback; Mac HWRT matches UE (M2+ experimental); P6a–P6j superseded except P6f (PX) and P6g (PL). Calendar restated as multi-year (v1 at about month 36–60). §21 governs where older sections conflict.

The v2.2 → v2.3 phase map is in the §12 introduction.

Ceiling log. Ceilings are provisional until their first measurement: G1a/G1 for diffuse lines, G3 for reflection and denoise lines, G0 for AS lines. That measurement may reset each line once, logged here with the data; afterwards measurement may only tighten. The total-frame caps (6.6 ms Ada / 16.6 ms mid-range p95, Dense City + NextGI High) are fixed. GU is the first measurement of U1, U1M, U1A and U1Q; P6i of U2 and U3. No resets logged yet.
